FILE docs/architecture/reviews/reminder-producer-implementation-plan-scoping/round-1-claude-proposal.md
1:# reminder-producer implementation plan â€” Round 1 (Claude proposal)
2:
3:Scope: turn D-299 (architecture, APPROVED) into an executable implementation plan. No code
4:written in this phase. Grounded in the actual codebase: `infra/main.tf` naming (`reminder_producer`
5:module, `reminder-dispatch`'s two-queue+relay pattern), `infra/modules/sqs-worker-queue`
6:(fixed `visibility_timeout_seconds = consumer_timeout_seconds * 6`, fixed `maxReceiveCount = 5`),
7:`src/shared/observability/context.ts` (`runWithContext`/`getContext`/`correlationIdFromSqsRecord`),
8:`src/shared/observability/metrics.ts` (`emitMetric`, EMF, dimension-cardinality discipline),
9:`src/shared/dynamodb/occ.ts` (conditional builders), `src/shared/outbox/outbox.ts`
10:(`OutboxDestination` union), `src/shared/errors/app-error.ts`.
11:
12:## Research declaration (research-protocol.md, E-014)
13:
14:**SIM PARCIAL.** The macro-pattern (decouple scan from claim via two queues) is already
15:externally researched and cited in D-299 (AWS Prescriptive Guidance, Lambda+SQS developer guide,
16:SQS API reference). What's new at THIS level is a sub-decision AWS has a well-documented but
17:non-obvious answer for: **SQS visibility timeout sizing relative to Lambda timeout, and its
18:interaction with `maxReceiveCount`/DLQ redelivery for a handler that holds an idempotency lease**.
19:This project's own `sqs-worker-queue` module already encodes one specific answer (6x, fixed,
20:`maxReceiveCount=5`) as a blanket rule for the dispatch case â€” applying it unmodified to a
21:lease-holding consumer needs to be checked against AWS's own guidance on visibility timeout vs.
22:in-flight processing time, because a lease-based state machine changes what "redelivery mid-lease"
23:means (D-299's own R3â†’R4 finding). Checklist applied this round, sourced from AWS Lambda Developer
24:Guide "Using Lambda with Amazon SQS" (event source mapping / partial batch response) and AWS SQS
25:"Amazon SQS visibility timeout" page (both already cited as sources in D-299, re-read here for the
26:consumer-timeout-vs-lease-duration angle specifically, no new source needed):
27:1. Visibility timeout must exceed **worst-case** processing time, not average â€” for a lease-based
28:   consumer, worst case includes lease renewal, not just one page's work.
29:2. `maxReceiveCount` Ã— avg processing time bounds how long a poison message cycles before DLQ â€”
30:   must be reconciled with lease `leaseUntil` duration so an expired lease and an SQS redelivery
31:   don't race in a way that duplicates or drops work (D-299's exact concern, now at the queue-timer
32:   level instead of the state-machine level).
33:3. Batch size for the scan-continuation queue must stay effectively 1 (see Â§3 below) â€” AWS's own
34:   guidance warns partial-batch-failure handling gets materially harder above batch size 1 when
35:   messages are causally chained (continuation N+1 must not be visible before N's page commits).
36:
37:## 1. Names
38:
39:Following `infra/main.tf`'s existing convention (`module "reminder_producer"` /
40:`function_name = "${local.name_prefix}-reminder-producer"`, and `reminder-dispatch`'s two-worker
41:pattern: `module.dispatch_queue` + `module.reminder_dispatch`):
42:
43:- **Scan queue** (continuation messages only): Terraform module `module.reminder_scan_queue`,
44:  queue name `${local.name_prefix}-reminder-scan`, DLQ `${local.name_prefix}-reminder-scan-dlq`
45:  (module's own fixed `-dlq` suffix).
46:- **Claim queue** (per-occurrence candidates): `module.reminder_claim_queue`, queue name
47:  `${local.name_prefix}-reminder-claim`, DLQ `${local.name_prefix}-reminder-claim-dlq`.
48:- **Scan handler**: existing `reminder-producer-handler.ts` is repurposed (not replaced) â€” same
49:  Lambda (`module.reminder_producer`, `reminder-producer-handler`), same sole holder of
50:  `gsi3_read`. Its trigger becomes dual: EventBridge Scheduler (tick start, one message per
51:  eligible shard/minute â€” unchanged entry point) **and** `module.reminder_scan_queue` (continuation
52:  messages, same handler, discriminated by event shape: `ReminderProducerEvent` vs. new
53:  `ReminderScanContinuationEvent`).
54:- **Claim handler**: new Lambda, following the `reminder-dispatch` naming pattern exactly â€”
55:  `module.reminder_claim_consumer`, `function_name = "${local.name_prefix}-reminder-claim-consumer"`,
56:  `handler_name = "reminder-claim-consumer-handler"`, source file
57:  `src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-claim-consumer-handler.ts`.
58:- **Page-lease table**: reuses the **main table** (no new table â€” D-299 says "mesma tabela
59:  principal"), item shape below.
60:- **Outbox destinations** (new `OutboxDestination` union members in `src/shared/outbox/outbox.ts`,
61:  same file/pattern as `SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1` etc.): `SQS_REMINDER_SCAN_CONTINUATION_V1` and
62:  `SQS_REMINDER_CLAIM_CANDIDATE_V1`. **Correction from architecture round**: the scan step's own
63:  candidate-publish + continuation-publish + lease-write are NOT all in one DynamoDB transaction
64:  (SQS `SendMessageBatch` cannot participate in `TransactWriteItems`) â€” see Â§4 for how outbox is
65:  actually used here vs. the direct-relay pattern.
66:- **Worker module** (pure logic, clock-injected, observability-agnostic per AGENTS.md Â§7):
67:  `src/workers/reminder-scan/scan-page.ts` (one page of `queryGsi3` â†’ candidates + next lease
68:  state) and `src/workers/reminder-claim/claim-occurrence.ts` (the existing claim logic extracted
69:  from today's `runProducerTick`, now callable per-candidate instead of per-shard-loop).
70:
71:## 2. Page-lease state machine
72:
73:**Item** (main table, single-table design â€” PK/SK pattern matches existing shard-scan items in
74:`dynamodb-reminder-producer-store.ts`, confirmed against that file):
75:
76:```
77:PK: SCAN#<shardId>#<tickMinuteISO>     e.g. SCAN#3#2026-09-14T22:31:00Z
78:SK: LEASE
79:entityType: "ReminderScanLease"
80:status: "IN_PROGRESS" | "COMPLETED"
81:ownerToken: string (randomUUID, one per Lambda invocation attempt â€” NOT per SQS messageId,
82:            so a redelivered SQS message that re-enters the same invocation semantics gets a
83:            fresh token and must re-win the conditional acquire, closing the exact silent-drop
84:            hole D-299 R3 found in the stateless-fence design)
85:leaseUntil: number (epoch ms)
86:lastEvaluatedKey: string | undefined (JSON-serialized DynamoDB ExclusiveStartKey for queryGsi3,
87:            undefined on the first page, present on every continuation)
88:pagesProcessed: number
89:candidatesPublished: number
90:version: number (occ.ts optimistic concurrency, same convention as every other mutable item)
91:ttl: epoch seconds, 7 days after leaseUntil â€” cheap cleanup, matches this project's existing TTL
92:     convention on other transient coordination items (checked against `defaultShardConfig()`'s
93:     neighbors in dynamodb-reminder-producer-store.ts)
94:```
95:
96:**Acquire** (first page of a shard/minute, or reclaim after expiry) â€” `occ.ts`'s
97:`buildConditionalPut` with:
98:`ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(PK) OR (status = :inProgress AND leaseUntil < :now) OR status = :completedNoop"`
99:â€” the third arm exists only to make a duplicate "start" message (a Scheduler retry firing the same
100:shard/minute twice) a safe no-op rather than an error; it still writes nothing new when already
101:`COMPLETED` (checked in application code before the Put, not relied on as a silent race â€” same
102:"belt AND suspenders" pattern `runProducerTick` already uses for claim races today).
103:
104:**Renew** (before publishing each continuation) â€” `occ.ts`'s `buildVersionedUpdate` with
105:`ConditionExpression: "ownerToken = :myToken AND version = :expectedVersion"`: sets new
106:`leaseUntil`, `lastEvaluatedKey`, increments `pagesProcessed`/`candidatesPublished`, bumps
107:`version`. **This update happens AFTER the claim-queue `SendMessageBatch` succeeds and BEFORE the
108:continuation message is sent** â€” ordering matters: if the process dies between the batch send and
109:the lease renew, the next attempt (a fresh SQS redelivery of the SAME scan message, since it never
110:completed) re-reads `lastEvaluatedKey` from the STALE lease state (pre-this-page), re-queries the
111:same page from `queryGsi3` (deterministic per `lastEvaluatedKey`), and re-publishes the same
112:candidates. This is why **claim-queue candidates must be idempotent to redelivery independent of
113:this lease** â€” already true by construction, because the claim consumer's own conditional
114:`transactWrite` (Â§ below) is the actual idempotency boundary for claiming, not the scan side. The
115:lease's job is only to make forward SCAN progress resumable exactly-once-effectively, not to
116:dedupe claims (that guarantee already exists one layer down).
117:
118:**Release** (last page, `queryGsi3` returns no `LastEvaluatedKey`) â€” `buildVersionedUpdate` sets
119:`status = "COMPLETED"`, same ownerToken/version condition.
120:
121:**Expire**: no active "expire" write â€” a lease simply becomes reclaimable once `leaseUntil < now`,
122:matched by the Acquire condition above. No separate reconciliation pass is added for this (see Â§6
123:â€” `reminder-reconciliation.ts` is untouched; lease reclaim is self-contained inside the scan path,
124:triggered by the SAME continuation message that would have fired next, since EventBridge Scheduler
125:fires a new tick every minute and a new tick for an ALREADY in-progress shard is the natural
126:reclaim trigger â€” see Â§7 for why this is safe rather than racy).
127:
128:## 3. Timeout/visibility sizing
129:
130:Fixed module facts (not renegotiable without a `sqs-worker-queue` module change, out of scope
131:here): `visibility_timeout_seconds = consumer_timeout_seconds * 6`, `maxReceiveCount = 5` fixed.
132:
133:- **Scan Lambda timeout**: 30s (one `queryGsi3` page + one `SendMessageBatch` + one lease
134:  renew/acquire â€” bounded work, generous margin over p99). â†’ scan queue visibility timeout = 180s
135:  (module-derived, 6x).
136:- **Claim Lambda timeout**: 10s (unchanged from today's per-occurrence claim â€” a single `get` +
137:  `transactWrite`, same cost as today's inner loop iteration). â†’ claim queue visibility timeout =
138:  60s (module-derived, 6x).
139:- **Lease duration** (`leaseUntil - leaseAcquiredAt`): **90s** â€” set independently of the Lambda
140:  timeout (it is a business-level mutual-exclusion window, not a retry mechanism) but sized against
141:  it with an explicit invariant: **lease duration â‰¥ 2x scan Lambda timeout** (30sÃ—2=60s â‰¤ 90s),
142:  giving one full retry's worth of headroom before a DIFFERENT invocation is allowed to steal the
143:  lease, so a slow-but-alive attempt is never pre-empted by its own SQS-triggered retry racing in.
144:  Combined with **scan visibility timeout (180s) > lease duration (90s)**, an SQS redelivery of the
145:  SAME message can never arrive before the lease it would contend with has already legitimately
146:  expired â€” removing the specific race D-299 R3 found (two live workers believing they both hold
147:  the lease) by construction, not by luck.
148:- **`maxReceiveCount=5` (fixed)** on the scan queue means a poison continuation message cycles for
149:  up to 5Ã—180s = 900s (15 min) before DLQ â€” acceptable, since EventBridge Scheduler fires a new
150:  tick every 60s regardless, so a stuck shard's lease will be reclaimed by a FRESH tick's message
151:  well before the poison copy DLQs; the DLQ path exists for the case where `queryGsi3` itself is
152:  erroring (data/query bug), not for ordinary transient contention.
153:- **Batch size**: scan queue consumer batch size = **1** (continuation messages are causally
154:  chained per shard â€” processing two "next page" messages for the same shard concurrently is
155:  exactly the race this design eliminates via the lease, so the event-source-mapping must not
156:  create that race by delivering >1 at once). Claim queue consumer batch size = **10** (candidates
157:  are independent per occurrence, matches `reminder-dispatch`'s existing batch size â€” confirmed
158:  against `infra/main.tf`'s `reminder_dispatch_from_queue` event source mapping).
159:- **Concurrency invariant** (D-299, now with numbers): scan queue
160:  `reserved_concurrent_executions` = number of shards (`defaultShardConfig()`'s shard count,
161:  confirmed = `DEFAULT_SHARD_COUNT = 4` in `src/modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.ts`) â€” one in-flight scan per shard by
162:  construction (the lease already enforces this logically; the Lambda concurrency cap is defense in
163:  depth, cheap to set). Claim queue `reserved_concurrent_executions` = **16** (â‰¥ sum of scan's
164:  per-page candidate fan-out â€” a page of `queryGsi3` batches up to 25 candidates per
165:  `SendMessageBatch` call, Ã—4 shards worst-case-concurrent = 100 theoretical peak sends, but claim
166:  Lambda p50 is <200ms so 16 concurrent claim workers clears a 100-item burst in ~1.3s â€” matches
167:  PERF-12's own 10k-in-a-minute volume target with margin, verified against PERF-12's report
168:  numbers in `docs/architecture/reviews/reminder-producer-structural-fix-scoping/` round artifacts).
169:
170:## 4. Tracing/logging/metrics/errors/outbox/writes â€” concrete
171:
172:- **Correlation ID propagation**: the ORIGINAL scan tick's `randomUUID()` (today's
173:  `reminder-producer-handler.ts` behavior, unchanged) is carried as an SQS `MessageAttribute`
174:  named `correlationId` on EVERY message this mechanism sends â€” both the scan-continuation message
175:  and every claim-candidate message in a page's `SendMessageBatch` â€” using the EXACT mechanism
176:  `correlationIdFromSqsRecord()`/`outboxRecordCorrelationId` already establishes for
177:  `reminder-dispatch` (same attribute name, same sender-side convention in
178:  `src/runtime/aws/composition/*.ts`). The scan handler wraps ALL of its work (including
179:  continuation re-entry) in `runWithContext({ correlationId }, ...)`, reading the attribute via
180:  `correlationIdFromSqsRecord()` when triggered by SQS and using `randomUUID()` only on the
181:  EventBridge Scheduler entry point (page 1 of a fresh tick) â€” same branch shape as today's
182:  handler. The claim consumer does the same on its inbound message. Net effect: one
183:  `correlationId` traces a shard/minute's entire page-by-page scan AND every claim it produced,
184:  queryable end-to-end via CloudWatch Logs Insights.
185:- **Logging** (`SecureLogger`, no raw `console.*`): new event names â€”
186:  `"reminder-scan page complete"` (fields: `shardId`, `tickMinute`, `pageNumber`,
187:  `candidatesPublished`, `hasMore`), `"reminder-scan lease acquire failed"` (fields: `shardId`,
188:  `tickMinute`, `reason: "already-in-progress" | "conditional-check-failed"`),
189:  `"reminder-claim occurrence claimed"` / `"reminder-claim occurrence lost race"` (fields:
190:  `occurrenceId` type-discriminated per D-299's `GSI3SK` distinction, `outcome`).
191:- **Metrics** (`emitMetric`, EMF, namespace `ExpirationTracker/ReminderProducer` for the scan side
192:  reusing the existing namespace since it's the same Lambda, and a NEW namespace
193:  `ExpirationTracker/ReminderClaimConsumer` for the new Lambda, matching the
194:  per-service-namespace convention `metrics.ts` documents):
195:  - `ReminderProducer`: `PagesScanned` (Count, no dimensions), `CandidatesPublishedPerPage` (Count,
196:    dimension `entityType` âˆˆ {`ReminderOccurrence`,`DocumentChasingOccurrence`} â€” closed 2-value
197:    union, safe per dimension-cardinality rule), `LeaseAcquireOutcome` (Count, dimension `outcome`
198:    âˆˆ {`acquired`,`reclaimed`,`contended`} â€” closed 3-value union).
199:  - `ReminderClaimConsumer`: `ClaimOutcome` (Count, dimension `outcome` âˆˆ
200:    {`claimed`,`lost-race`,`unknown-entity-type`} â€” closed union, same shape as today's
201:    `runProducerTick` result fields), `ClaimQueueBacklogAge` â€” **not emitted by the handler**;
202:    already covered natively by the module's `ApproximateAgeOfOldestMessage` CloudWatch alarm
203:    (D-299 explicitly names this as native, not a custom metric â€” kept consistent here rather than
204:    duplicating).
205:- **Error taxonomy** (`app-error.ts`): a lease acquire race that loses is NOT an error (expected
206:  contention, logged/metric'd only). A `queryGsi3` failure inside the scan page throws the SAME
207:  taxonomy the current handler already uses (`ValidationError` for bad continuation payload shape,
208:  unchanged AppError subclasses for downstream failures â€” no new subclass needed, confirmed by
209:  reading the union in `app-error.ts`; if a future round finds a gap, add
210:  `ReminderLeaseConflictError extends AppError` only if contention needs to surface as a retryable
211:  error rather than a logged no-op â€” deferred as a round-2 open question, not assumed here).
212:- **Outbox**: candidates and the continuation message are **NOT** written via the transactional
213:  outbox pattern, and this is a deliberate deviation from AGENTS.md's "critical side-effect in the
214:  same transaction as the aggregate write" rule â€” because there IS no aggregate write at scan time
215:  (the scan step only reads GSI3 and writes its OWN lease item; the occurrence aggregate write only
216:  happens later, in the claim consumer). The outbox pattern's applicability is at the CLAIM step:
217:  the claim consumer's existing `get`+`transactWrite` (unchanged from today) is the aggregate
218:  write, and it already should (today's code should be checked, not assumed â€” round 2 action item)
219:  carry its resulting event via the SAME `TransactWriteItems`, exactly like `SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1`
220:  today. Round 1 conclusion: **outbox governs the claimâ†’dispatch boundary (unchanged from today),
221:  not the scanâ†’claim boundary (new, and necessarily a direct `SendMessageBatch`, not outbox-mediated,
222:  because outbox requires a DynamoDB transaction and the scan step's transaction is the lease
223:  write, which is unrelated to the individual candidates being published)**.
224:- **DynamoDB writes**: lease acquire/renew/release exclusively via `occ.ts`'s
225:  `buildConditionalPut`/`buildVersionedUpdate` (no raw `PutItem`/`UpdateItem`), confirmed pattern
226:  match against existing shard-scan-state usage in `dynamodb-reminder-producer-store.ts`.
227:
228:## 5. Test plan
229:
230:- **Unit** (`test/unit/reminder/`, following existing `in-memory-store.ts` fixture pattern):
231:  page-lease acquire/renew/release pure logic â€” concurrent acquire attempts (both should not
232:  succeed), renew with stale `ownerToken` (must fail), renew with correct token but expired
233:  `leaseUntil` (must fail â€” a zombie holder cannot renew past its own expiry), release from wrong
234:  owner (must fail), reclaim after expiry (must succeed for a NEW ownerToken), redelivery-of-same-
235:  message-mid-lease (must be a safe no-op re-publish, not a double-claim â€” this is the exact D-299
236:  R3 finding, gets its own dedicated test).
237:- **Integration**: extend the EXISTING GSI3-isolation coverage â€” this codebase's actual mechanism
238:  is `infra/tests/stack.tftest.hcl` (Terraform test asserting IAM policy scoping to GSI3, not a
239:  `.test.ts` file of that exact name â€” corrected from the task prompt's assumption after checking;
240:  no `test/integration/gsi3-isolation.test.ts` exists in this repo today). Add a new
241:  `terraform test` assertion (same file, new `run` block) that the NEW `reminder_claim_consumer`
242:  Lambda's IAM role has NO `gsi3_read` statement, mirroring the existing assertion pattern for
243:  `reminder_dispatch`.
244:- **Integration** (application-level, Vitest): a scanâ†’claim round trip using the in-memory SQS +
245:  DynamoDB test doubles this codebase already has for `reminder-dispatch` (composition root
246:  fixtures) â€” burst volume at PERF-12's 10k scale, asserting ZERO loss (the actual regression this
247:  whole design fixes) and that pausing/resuming a shard mid-scan (simulated Lambda death) via a
248:  fresh invocation with the same continuation message reaches `COMPLETED` with all candidates
249:  published exactly once each.
250:
251:## 6. `reminder-reconciliation.ts` impact
252:
253:**Confirmed: no change needed**, with actual reasoning (not just repeating D-299's stated
254:expectation): `reminder-reconciliation.ts`'s job (per its own file, to be re-read in round 2 for a
255:citation-level confirmation) is to catch occurrences stuck in `CLAIMED` past `claimExpiresAt` â€”
256:that contract is entirely downstream of the claim consumer's `transactWrite`, which writes
257:`claimExpiresAt` identically to today's code (D-299's own premise, unchanged by this plan since the
258:claim logic itself is only relocated, not rewritten). The scan-side lease this plan adds is a
259:DIFFERENT state machine on a DIFFERENT item (`SCAN#...#LEASE`, never `CLAIMED` occurrence rows) that
260:reconciliation never reads today and has no reason to start reading â€” a stuck scan lease
261:self-heals via the next EventBridge tick's reclaim path (Â§2), not via reconciliation. One residual
262:open question for round 2: does reconciliation's own scan of `CLAIMED` rows need its OWN
263:correlation ID propagation update for consistency, even though its logic doesn't change? (Leaning
264:no â€” it's not part of this mechanism's message chain â€” but flagging rather than asserting.)
265:
266:## 7. Migration/rollout
267:
268:Big-bang cutover, not staged, for a concrete reason: the scan and claim halves are not
269:independently useful (a scan queue with no claim consumer just accumulates candidates; a claim
270:consumer with no new-shape scan producer never receives anything) â€” there's no safe intermediate
271:state to stage through, unlike, say, a dual-write migration. Sequencing:
272:1. Deploy claim consumer Lambda + claim queue (inert â€” nothing publishes to it yet).
273:2. Deploy scan queue (inert â€” nothing subscribes to the OLD producer's shard-loop yet, since it
274:   doesn't publish to it).
275:3. Deploy the MODIFIED `reminder-producer-handler.ts` (now page-based) in the SAME release as step
276:   1/2's infra â€” this is the actual cutover moment; the old monolithic-per-shard loop code path is
277:   deleted in this same change, not kept behind a flag (D-299 didn't ask for a flag, and a flag
278:   here would mean maintaining two claim code paths against the same table, doubling the
279:   concurrent-claim race surface this whole fix targets).
280:4. **Occurrences already `SCHEDULED` at cutover**: confirmed safe, with reasoning â€” `SCHEDULED`
281:   occurrences are discovered by `queryGsi3`, not by any in-flight queue state; the OLD mechanism's
282:   only in-flight artifact at cutover is whatever's already sitting in the OLD single-invocation
283:   claim loop of a tick that started before deploy (which either finishes normally before the
284:   deploy completes, since Lambda in-flight invocations aren't killed by a code update, or times
285:   out and is picked up fresh by the NEW scan mechanism's next tick â€” no occurrence-level state is
286:   lost either way, since `SCHEDULED` occurrences are read fresh from GSI3 every tick regardless of
287:   which mechanism is running).
288:5. Rollback path: revert step 3's Lambda code only (keep the new queues/consumer deployed but
289:   inert) â€” since GSI3 access never moved, a revert to the old handler code resumes the old
290:   monolithic scan immediately with no data migration needed in either direction.
291:

296:points I can already see: (a) the outbox-boundary reasoning in Â§4 is my own inference, not
297:verified against what the CURRENT claim code actually does with outbox â€” flagged as a round-2
298:action item rather than resolved; (b) the corrected test-file reference (no
299:`gsi3-isolation.test.ts` exists) means I'm deviating from the task prompt's literal ask without
300:having fully traced whether some OTHER file is the intended target; (c) `ReminderLeaseConflictError`
301:error-taxonomy question left open rather than decided; (d) haven't verified `defaultShardConfig()`
302:shard count independently beyond one grep-confirmed read. Expect Codex to find at least one of
303:these, plus likely a sharper take on the claim-queue concurrency arithmetic in Â§3 (my 100-peak/16-

255:module "reminder_producer" {
256:  source = "./modules/lambda-function"
257:
258:  function_name = "${local.name_prefix}-reminder-producer"
259:  handler_name  = "reminder-producer-handler"
260:  source_dir    = "${local.dist_dir}/reminder-producer-handler"
261:  # PERF-12 1k load test (docs/engineering/performance/results/PERF-12-async-pipeline-1k.md):
262:  # the CDK-inherited 10s default was exceeded for 7 straight minutes under a realistic burst
263:  # (many occurrences due the same minute), fully sequential per-occurrence processing, no
264:  # parallelism at the time. Raised to 60s: producer.ts now processes occurrences with bounded
265:  # concurrency (PRODUCER_CLAIM_CONCURRENCY=8), so a get+transactWrite pair costing tens of ms
266:  # (PERF-12's own measurement) puts a burst of ~1,000 occurrences well under this budget even
267:  # with generous headroom for DynamoDB latency variance - not raised to Lambda's 900s max,
268:  # which would let a genuinely pathological tick run for 15 minutes before EventBridge's next
269:  # 1-minute-later invocation piles another one on top of it.
270:  timeout_seconds                = 60
271:  adot_layer_arn                 = var.adot_layer_arn
272:  environment_variables          = local.common_env
273:  reserved_concurrent_executions = var.enable_reserved_concurrency ? 2 : null
274:  # The ONLY function granted gsi3_read â€” never add this capability to any other function.
275:  policy_documents_json = [
276:    module.table.tenant_facing_read_write_policy_json,
277:    module.table.gsi3_read_policy_json,
278:  ]
279:  tags = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
280:}

710:# D-233: SEC-R2-02 fix. Two changes from the original mapping:
711:#  - filter_criteria now also matches on entityType - the marker's OWN Put/Update/Delete writes
712:#    (D-233's new claim/lease mechanism) already generate stream INSERT/MODIFY events on this
713:#    same table; without this filter they'd reach the handler, get classified "malformed" by
714:#    isDeliveryRecord(), and inflate batchItemFailures/the failure queue with pure noise (a latent
715:#    bug that predates D-233 too, since claim()'s original PutItem already wrote a marker row).
716:#  - maximum_retry_attempts/maximum_record_age_in_seconds set to -1 (unlimited, bounded only by
717:#    DynamoDB Streams' own native 24h retention) - a bounded retry count/age (the previous
718:#    3/3600) could exhaust BEFORE the marker's 30s claim lease ever expires, leaving
719:#    SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE retries stuck with no path to the SEND_UNCERTAIN reconciliation
720:#    deliver.ts relies on. The IteratorAge alarm below is the real backstop for a genuinely
721:#    stuck/poison record - it fires in minutes, not after the 24h native retention discards it.
722:resource "aws_lambda_event_source_mapping" "guest_credential_delivery_from_stream" {
723:  event_source_arn               = module.guest_credential_delivery_table.stream_arn
724:  function_name                  = module.guest_credential_delivery_handler.live_alias_arn
725:  starting_position              = "LATEST"
726:  batch_size                     = 5
727:  function_response_types        = ["ReportBatchItemFailures"]
728:  maximum_retry_attempts         = -1
729:  maximum_record_age_in_seconds  = -1
730:  bisect_batch_on_function_error = true
731:
732:  filter_criteria {
733:    filter {
734:      pattern = jsonencode({
735:        eventName = ["INSERT"]
736:        dynamodb = {
737:          NewImage = {
738:            entityType = { S = ["GuestCredentialDelivery"] }
739:          }
740:        }
741:      })
742:    }
743:  }
744:
745:  destination_config {
746:    on_failure {
747:      destination_arn = aws_sqs_queue.guest_credential_delivery_failures.arn
748:    }
749:  }
750:}
751:
752:# D-233: real-time backstop replacing the "Errors alarm gives visibility" assumption that turned
753:# out to be false - ReportBatchItemFailures means a retried record never surfaces as a Lambda
754:# Errors metric, the invocation returns normally. IteratorAge is the correct signal: a record
755:# stuck retrying (SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE/SEND_FAILED looping) delays the shard's cursor immediately,
756:# well before the stream's native 24h retention would silently discard it.
757:resource "aws_cloudwatch_metric_alarm" "guest_credential_delivery_iterator_age" {
758:  alarm_name          = "${local.name_prefix}-guest-credential-delivery-iterator-age"
759:  namespace           = "AWS/Lambda"
760:  metric_name         = "IteratorAge"
761:  dimensions          = { FunctionName = module.guest_credential_delivery_handler.function_name }
762:  statistic           = "Maximum"
763:  period              = 300
764:  evaluation_periods  = 1
765:  threshold           = 300000 # 5 minutes in ms - far below the stream's 24h native retention.
766:  comparison_operator = "GreaterThanThreshold"
767:  alarm_description   = "guest-credential-delivery: stream cursor is falling behind - a record is being retried repeatedly (SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE/SEND_FAILED) without converging to SENT/SEND_UNCERTAIN. Investigate before the stream's native 24h retention silently discards it."
768:  treat_missing_data  = "notBreaching"
769:  alarm_actions       = [module.alert_topic.topic_arn]
770:  tags                = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
771:}

1444:# --- Trilha de auditoria de seguranÃ§a (MVP desta sessÃ£o) -----------------------------------
1445:# docs/architecture/reviews/security-audit-trail-design/codex-reconciliation-round2-final-design.md
1446:# Detecta os 2 achados reais abertos (SeguranÃ§a-Logging/OWASP A09, SRE-DetecÃ§Ã£o): negaÃ§Ã£o de
1447:# autorizaÃ§Ã£o e acesso a GSI3/GSI6 nÃ£o tinham trilha dedicada. Reusa o alert-topic real de M5.
1448:
1449:# Real deploy failure (2026-08-28, PR #78, CD run 33210539869): brand-new profile_handler's log
1450:# group didn't exist yet when this module's PutMetricFilter tried to reference
1451:# "/aws/lambda/exptrk-dev-profile-handler" in the SAME apply that created the function -
1452:# ResourceNotFoundException, since a Lambda's log group is only auto-created by AWS on its FIRST
1453:# real invocation, which hadn't happened yet. Same bug class already fixed for malware_result/
1454:# reconciliation in document-observability (see that module's header comment) - fixed the same
1455:# way here instead of waiting for a real invocation to happen naturally.
1456:#
1457:# Logging-engineering review finding (Codex round 1, 2026-08-29): the lists below were also
1458:# genuinely INCOMPLETE, independent of the log-group-race bug - `documents_handler`/
1459:# `subjects_handler`/`imports_handler` all have real call sites of `auditAuthorizationDenied`
1460:# (document-handlers.ts/extraction-handlers.ts, subject-handlers.ts, import-handlers.ts) that
1461:# were never wired to a metric filter, and `document_purge_handler`/
1462:# `upload_slot_reconciliation_handler` both emit `security.global_index_access(_denied)` with a
1463:# `component` value the `GlobalIndexComponent` union in security-audit.ts explicitly names as a
1464:# real GSI6 consumer, also never wired. See `test/architecture/security-audit-observability-
1465:# coverage.test.ts` for the regression-proof cross-check (greps every real call site and asserts
1466:# its owning Lambda module appears in the two lists below) - the module-local Terraform test
1467:# (`infra/modules/security-audit-observability/tests/`) uses its own synthetic fixture and
1468:# cannot catch a root-wiring omission like this one.
1469:#
1470:# Of the 2 newly-added functions without a confirmed pre-existing log group (checked via `aws
1471:# logs describe-log-groups` against real `dev`, 2026-08-29): `subjects_handler`/
1472:# `imports_handler` had never been invoked in `dev` and need the same explicit-log-group
1473:# treatment as `profile_handler` above. `documents_handler` (real traffic already),
1474:# `document_purge_handler` (its EventBridge Scheduler already fired at least once), and
1475:# `upload_slot_reconciliation_handler` (already Terraform-managed by document-observability's
1476:# own `aws_cloudwatch_log_group.reconciliation`) all already have a real log group - adding a
1477:# managed resource for those would try to (re)create an existing one and fail with
1478:# ResourceAlreadyExistsException, same reasoning as the other 4 functions already noted above.

1792:# --- UploadFinalizerWorker: S3 event (via queue) -> validate + invoke ParserSandbox ---------
1793:
1794:# Real bug found via Camada 3 (2026-08-22): UploadFinalizerWorker only had READ access to the
1795:# quarantine bucket, but advanceAfterEvidence() (called identically by both workers) performs
1796:# the actual quarantine->clean promotion copy whichever worker's evidence completes the pair
1797:# LAST - which is not always MalwareResultWorker. A real upload where the finalizer's own
1798:# evidence-persist step landed after the malware scan result hit exactly this: the finalizer
1799:# won the PROMOTE race and got a real AccessDenied trying s3:PutObject on the clean bucket.
1800:# Both workers need IDENTICAL object-access permissions for this reason - kept as two
1801:# separately-named data sources (not a single shared one) so each function's IAM role
1802:# document stays self-explanatory in `aws iam` output, but the statements are deliberately
1803:# identical to malware_result_object_access below.
1804:data "aws_iam_policy_document" "upload_finalizer_object_access" {
1805:  statement {
1806:    sid       = "ReadDeleteQuarantineObjects"
1807:    effect    = "Allow"
1808:    actions   = ["s3:GetObject", "s3:GetObjectVersion", "s3:DeleteObjectVersion"]
1809:    resources = ["${module.document_buckets.quarantine_bucket_arn}/*"]
1810:  }
1811:  statement {
1812:    # Real bug found via Camada 3 (2026-08-22): advanceAfterEvidence() calls headObject() on
1813:    # the clean bucket right after copyObject() to VERIFY the promotion copy actually landed
1814:    # before confirming CLEAN - S3's HeadObject API requires the s3:GetObject IAM action (a
1815:    # well-known S3 quirk: there is no separate "HeadObject" IAM action), which was never
1816:    # granted here (only PutObject was). The copy itself succeeded every time; only the
1817:    # verification step 403'd, surfaced by the AWS SDK as an opaque "UnknownError" rather than
1818:    # a clean AccessDenied.
1819:    sid       = "ReadWriteCleanObjects"
1820:    effect    = "Allow"
1821:    actions   = ["s3:GetObject", "s3:GetObjectVersion", "s3:PutObject"]
1822:    resources = ["${module.document_buckets.clean_bucket_arn}/*"]
1823:  }
1824:  statement {
1825:    sid       = "DecryptQuarantineObjects"
1826:    effect    = "Allow"
1827:    actions   = ["kms:Decrypt"]
1828:    resources = [module.document_buckets.quarantine_kms_key_arn]
1829:  }
1830:  statement {
1831:    sid       = "EncryptCleanObjects"
1832:    effect    = "Allow"
1833:    actions   = ["kms:GenerateDataKey", "kms:Decrypt"]
1834:    resources = [module.document_buckets.clean_kms_key_arn]
1835:  }
1836:}
1837:
1838:data "aws_iam_policy_document" "upload_finalizer_invoke_parser_sandbox" {
1839:  statement {
1840:    sid       = "InvokeParserSandbox"
1841:    effect    = "Allow"
1842:    actions   = ["lambda:InvokeFunction"]
1843:    resources = [module.parser_sandbox.function_arn]
1844:  }
1845:}

2916:# `var.extraction_pipeline_enabled` for the same reason item 2's Lambda/queue/IAM aren't
2917:# gated either: the resources below are inert on their own. `START_OCR` can only ever run if
2918:# Step Functions invokes this function via `lambda:invoke.waitForTaskToken`, which requires
2919:# the `document_extraction` state machine to exist - it doesn't yet (`infra/modules/
2920:# extraction-workflow` is still deliberately uninstantiated, items 5-7's Lambdas don't exist).
2921:# Without that invocation, `startDocumentTextDetection` (the only paid call in this whole
2922:# handler) is never reached by any real event source, so leaving this infra always-on/
2923:# inspectable in `dev` carries the same "deployable but never triggered by real traffic" risk
2924:# profile item 2 already accepted for the identical reason.
2925:#
2926:# `module.textract_task_handler.live_alias_arn` is the ARN item 3's Terraform module
2927:# (extraction-workflow) needs once it's finally instantiated - exposed via
2928:# `local.textract_task_handler_function_arn` below so that wiring is a small diff, not a
2929:# redesign (same intent as `local.extraction_state_machine_arn` above).
2930:
2931:# Dedicated CMK for the Step Functions task-token ciphertext (`TextractJob.taskTokenCiphertext`)
2932:# - same D-054 disciplina as `infra/modules/bff-session-table`'s refresh-token CMK: a live
2933:# Step Functions callback credential is exactly the class of "live credential, not just
2934:# storage-at-rest" value that decision reserved a dedicated CMK for, distinct from the
2935:# AWS-managed key `document-buckets` uses for document blobs (a much less sensitive value).
2936:# Reusing an existing CMK was considered (see NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md) and rejected: sharing a
2937:# key across two independently-deployable modules (bff/extraction) would couple their key
2938:# policies/rotation and blur the "only this module's Lambda role may ever use this key"
2939:# invariant D-054 established.
2940:resource "aws_kms_key" "task_token" {
2941:  description             = "CMK for TextractTaskHandler's Step Functions task-token ciphertext at rest (D-035)"
2942:  deletion_window_in_days = 30
2943:  enable_key_rotation     = true
2944:  tags                    = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
2945:}
2946:
2947:resource "aws_kms_alias" "task_token" {
2948:  name          = "alias/${local.name_prefix}-task-token"
2949:  target_key_id = aws_kms_key.task_token.key_id
2950:}

2952:# `EXTRACTION_TRANSIENT` S3 class (privacy-lgpd.md Â§4) - dedicated bucket, no versioning/
2953:# backup/replication (the artifact is disposable OCR text, never a document of record), 24h
2954:# lifecycle safety net matching `EXTRACTION_TRANSIENT_LIFECYCLE_HOURS` (retention.ts) exactly
2955:# (24h = 1 day, S3 lifecycle `expiration.days` has no hour granularity). Explicit deletion is
2956:# always `ExtractionValidationTaskHandler`'s job (item 7, implemented - run-extraction-validation.ts
2957:# calls artifacts.delete() at both terminal states) - this lifecycle rule only catches a run
2958:# that never reaches a terminal state.

3213:# `START_OCR` invocation permission for Step Functions - deliberately granted here even
3214:# though no state machine exists yet (same "deployable, not yet wired" posture as the rest of
3215:# this section): item 3's extraction-workflow module needs this permission to already exist
3216:# when it's finally instantiated, and granting invoke to a specific state machine ARN (rather
3217:# than "*") is safe to create ahead of time (`local.extraction_state_machine_arn`, already
3218:# defined above, is deterministic even before that state machine is created).
3219:resource "aws_lambda_permission" "textract_task_from_state_machine" {
3220:  statement_id = "AllowInvokeFromDocumentExtractionStateMachine"
3221:  action       = "lambda:InvokeFunction"
3222:  # `function_name` must be the UNQUALIFIED function ARN (`function_arn`, never
3223:  # `live_alias_arn`, which is already alias-qualified and redundant with `qualifier` below) -
3224:  # confirmed by reading the real deployed state directly (`terraform plan` against `dev`):
3225:  # AWS's own read-back of this resource's FunctionName always normalizes to the plain function
3226:  # ARN, so a declared `live_alias_arn` (or, tried first, the bare function NAME) never
3227:  # converges against it - both made this resource perpetually non-convergent, replaced on
3228:  # EVERY `terraform apply` on this repo regardless of what the apply was actually about,
3229:  # confirmed by 2 consecutive unrelated deploys (2026-09-12) each showing the identical
3230:  # 4-resource replacement. Found and fixed incidentally while verifying an unrelated deploy's
3231:  # plan output; re-verified against real `dev` state after the fix (0 replacements, not just
3232:  # `terraform validate` passing).
3233:  function_name = module.textract_task_handler.function_arn
3234:  qualifier     = module.textract_task_handler.live_alias_name
3235:  principal     = "states.amazonaws.com"
3236:  source_arn    = local.extraction_state_machine_arn
3237:}
3238:
3239:locals {
3240:  # Item 3's extraction-workflow module wiring point - see the module's own `variables.tf`
3241:  # (parameterized by Lambda ARN variables, still not instantiated from infra/main.tf).
3242:  textract_task_handler_function_arn = module.textract_task_handler.live_alias_arn
3243:}

3814:# `rate`, not `cron` (same choice reminder-schedule makes for its non-time-of-day schedules): the
3815:# sweeper only needs a regular cadence, never a specific wall-clock hour. Daily is proposed by the
3816:# approved design and is explicitly NOT load-bearing - the repair half is bounded by the 1-hour
3817:# staleness filter, not by how often this runs.
3818:module "tenant_purge_sweeper_handler" {
3819:  source = "./modules/lambda-function"
3820:
3821:  function_name   = "${local.name_prefix}-tenant-purge-sweeper-handler"
3822:  handler_name    = "tenant-purge-sweeper-handler"
3823:  source_dir      = "${local.dist_dir}/tenant-purge-sweeper-handler"
3824:  adot_layer_arn  = var.adot_layer_arn
3825:  timeout_seconds = 300
3826:  environment_variables = merge(local.common_env, local.tenant_purge_bucket_env, {
3827:    TENANT_PURGE_STATE_MACHINE_ARN = local.tenant_purge_state_machine_arn
3828:  })
3829:  # The sweeper re-runs the SAME verifyTenant*Empty() passes the purge worker does, so it needs the
3830:  # same read surface - but it never deletes anything. D-234 (E-018, Codex Rodada 3): it now gets
3831:  # its OWN read-only DynamoDB policies (main table Scan+GetItem via cross_tenant_scan_policy_json
3832:  # ["tenant_purge_sweeper"]; session table Scan-only via tenant_purge_sweeper_session_table) rather
3833:  # than inheriting the worker's read/write grants (which included DeleteItem on both tables - the
3834:  # exact class of excess this finding closes). S3 policy is still shared with the worker
3835:  # (tenant_purge_worker_s3, which includes DeleteObject) - Codex Rodada 3 flagged this as a real
3836:  # but non-blocking follow-up for E-018 (scope is DynamoDB least-privilege); tracked as a nominal
3837:  # cleanup, not a new numbered finding.
3838:  policy_documents_json = [
3839:    module.table.cross_tenant_scan_policy_json["tenant_purge_sweeper"],
3840:    data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_worker_s3.json,
3841:    data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_sweeper_session_table.json,
3842:    data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_start_execution.json,
3843:    # D-127: states:DescribeExecution for the HELD_FOR_RECOVERY reconciliation branch.
3844:    data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_describe_execution.json,
3845:  ]

4602:# --- ScheduledReportsSchedulerHandler: D-204 decisions 3-4 (Roadmap P1 item 15), implemented
4603:# D-211 fatia 2 - claims due ReportSubscriptions via GSI8 (WORK#REPORT_SUBSCRIPTION) and writes a
4604:# durable outbox event per claim, same 2-action TransactWriteItems discipline as
4605:# reminder-producer's claim (see src/workers/scheduled-reports/scheduler.ts's doc comment).
4606:# Migrated straight onto GSI8 (never a base-table Scan) - gsi8_read_policy_json/
4607:# worker_transact_write_policy_json scoped to WORK#REPORT_SUBSCRIPTION/DLQ#REPORT_SUBSCRIPTION,
4608:# same pattern as core_user_data_purge_handler above.
4609:#
4610:# Cron cadence is DAILY (04:15 UTC, between requirement-reindex 04:00 and
4611:# document-request-recurrence 04:30), NOT literally weekly, despite D-204 decision 3's prose
4612:# saying "aws_scheduler_schedule Ãºnico semanal": read here as describing the FEATURE's delivery
4613:# cadence (ReportSubscriptionCadence = "WEEKLY", decision 1 - each subscription fires ~once/week)
4614:# rather than the infra schedule's own polling frequency - "Ãºnico" (one Lambda/one schedule
4615:# resource/one cross-tenant scan per execution, "mesmo padrÃ£o de requirement-reindex") is the load-
4616:# bearing part of that sentence. A daily poll is what actually honors decision 1's per-subscription
4617:# dayOfWeek/localTime/timeZone fields with real precision (bounded to ~24h drift, matching every
4618:# other GSI8-sparse-index worker's own precision bound) - a literally-weekly poll would desync
4619:# every subscriber's chosen delivery time toward whichever single instant the schedule happens to
4620:# fire at. Documented judgment call (decisions-log.md D-211) - open to being overridden if a
4621:# literal weekly cron was actually the intended reading.

FILE infra/modules/sqs-worker-queue/main.tf
1:# Generic SQS worker queue + DLQ, reused across milestones (M3.5 reminder dispatch, M4
2:# notification router/email-deliver/ses-callback) - extracted from the reminder-specific
3:# module of the same shape (round1 cross-critique of M4's design: "o mÃ³dulo atual
4:# reminder-queue nÃ£o deve ser reutilizado nominalmente porque contÃ©m nomes, SIDs e
5:# descriÃ§Ãµes especÃ­ficos de reminder"). See variables.tf for the sizing rationale
6:# (maxReceiveCount=5, visibility timeout = 6x consumer timeout, long polling, DLQ age alarm
7:# at 1h per implementation-blueprint.md line 879) - unchanged from the original module,
8:# only the naming is generic now.
9:
10:resource "aws_sqs_queue" "dlq" {
11:  name                      = "${var.queue_name}-dlq"
12:  message_retention_seconds = 14 * 24 * 60 * 60 # 14 days
13:  sqs_managed_sse_enabled   = true
14:  tags                      = var.tags
15:}
16:
17:resource "aws_sqs_queue" "this" {
18:  name                       = var.queue_name
19:  message_retention_seconds  = 4 * 24 * 60 * 60 # 4 days
20:  visibility_timeout_seconds = var.consumer_timeout_seconds * 6
21:  receive_wait_time_seconds  = 20 # long polling
22:  sqs_managed_sse_enabled    = true
23:
24:  redrive_policy = jsonencode({
25:    deadLetterTargetArn = aws_sqs_queue.dlq.arn
26:    maxReceiveCount     = 5
27:  })
28:
29:  tags = var.tags
30:}

32:# implementation-blueprint.md line 879: DLQ age alarm at 1h/4h.
33:resource "aws_cloudwatch_metric_alarm" "dlq_age" {
34:  alarm_name          = "${var.queue_name}-dlq-age"
35:  namespace           = "AWS/SQS"
36:  metric_name         = "ApproximateAgeOfOldestMessage"
37:  dimensions          = { QueueName = aws_sqs_queue.dlq.name }
38:  statistic           = "Maximum"
39:  period              = 300 # 5 minutes
40:  evaluation_periods  = 1
41:  threshold           = 3600 # 1 hour, in seconds
42:  comparison_operator = "GreaterThanThreshold"
43:  alarm_description   = "ReminderDispatchQueue DLQ has messages older than 1 hour - investigate and redrive per runbook (never blind redrive)."
44:  treat_missing_data  = "notBreaching"
45:  alarm_actions       = [var.alert_topic_arn]
46:  tags                = var.tags
47:}

49:# Queue ARN constructed deterministically (not read from aws_sqs_queue.this.arn) so the IAM
50:# policy documents below are plan-time-known and don't depend on the queue resource actually
51:# existing yet â€” same pattern as the dynamo-table module. SQS ARN format is stable/documented:
52:# arn:aws:sqs:<region>:<account>:<queue-name>.
53:locals {
54:  queue_arn = "arn:aws:sqs:${var.aws_region}:${var.aws_account_id}:${var.queue_name}"
55:}

57:# IAM policy documents mirroring scoped-lambda-function.ts's queueAccessFor() capabilities.
58:data "aws_iam_policy_document" "consume" {
59:  statement {
60:    sid = "WorkerQueueConsume"
61:    actions = [

FILE src/shared/observability/context.ts
1:/**
2: * Request-scoped correlation context via AsyncLocalStorage (m5-observability-design.md #2).
3: * runWithContext is called once per record for batch handlers (SQS, DynamoDB Streams, sweeper) -
4: * never once per invocation for those - so context never leaks across records in the same batch.
5: */
6:import { AsyncLocalStorage } from "node:async_hooks";
7:
8:export interface RequestContext {
9:  correlationId: string;
10:  tenantId?: string;
11:  requestId?: string;
12:}
13:
14:const storage = new AsyncLocalStorage<RequestContext>();
15:
16:export function runWithContext<T>(context: RequestContext, fn: () => T): T {
17:  return storage.run(context, fn);
18:}
19:
20:export function getContext(): RequestContext | undefined {
21:  return storage.getStore();
22:}
23:
24:/**
25: * m5-observability-design.md #2: correlationId for an SQS record whose sender (relay/
26: * sweeper's composition-root senders, src/runtime/aws/composition/*.ts) propagates the
27: * original business correlationId via this exact MessageAttribute name - see
28: * outboxRecordCorrelationId (src/shared/outbox/outbox.ts) for the sender side of this same
29: * contract. Falls back to the SQS messageId only for a message that never got the
30: * attribute (pre-M5 in-flight, or a queue whose producer has no upstream correlationId to
31: * propagate, e.g. SES/SNS-sourced messages) - pulled out as its own pure function so the
32: * extraction itself is unit-testable without a handler module's side-effecting top-level
33: * AWS client construction.
34: */
35:export function correlationIdFromSqsRecord(record: { messageAttributes?: Record<string, { stringValue?: string }>; messageId: string }): string {
36:  return record.messageAttributes?.["correlationId"]?.stringValue ?? record.messageId;
37:}
FILE src/shared/observability/metrics.ts
1:/**
2: * emitMetric â€” E-018/E-021 (full-audit round2): closes the "no custom metrics/dashboard exist"
3: * gap, protocol Claudeâ†”Codex APPROVED (3 rounds, 6,8â†’7,2â†’9,2, `docs/architecture/reviews/
4: * emf-metrics-dashboard-scoping/`). Writes CloudWatch Embedded Metric Format (EMF) â€” a
5: * structured log line CloudWatch Logs parses into a real custom metric, no `PutMetricData` SDK
6: * call, no new IAM permission (every Lambda already has `logs:PutLogEvents` via
7: * `AWSLambdaBasicExecutionRole`).
8: *
9: * **Never call this from `src/workers/**`** â€” workers are deliberately observability-agnostic
10: * (`AGENTS.md` Â§7/E-007). Only the real Lambda handlers in `src/runtime/aws/handlers/` call
11: * this, right next to the `logger.info(..., { outcome: ... })` call that already exists there.
12: *
13: * **Dimension discipline (E-014 external research, AWS EMF spec + this project's own
14: * `logger.ts` precedent)**: `tenantId` (or any other high-cardinality value â€” a user id, a
15: * request id) must NEVER be a metric dimension â€” each unique dimension combination is a
16: * separately-billed custom metric, and tenant count grows without bound. `Outcome` values used
17: * as dimensions here are closed, low-cardinality unions (5-9 members, enumerated at each real
18: * call site) â€” safe. Per-tenant investigation is a CloudWatch Logs Insights query against the
19: * structured logs (which already carry `tenantId` via `runWithContext`), never a metric
20: * dimension â€” see `infra/modules/observability-dashboard/`'s log widgets.
21: */
22:
23:export interface MetricPoint {
24:  name: string;
25:  value: number;
26:  unit: "Count" | "Milliseconds";
27:  /** Closed, low-cardinality dimension values only (an Outcome union member, a fixed
28:   * destination name) â€” never a tenantId/userId/requestId. See the module doc comment above. */
29:  dimensions?: Record<string, string>;
30:}
31:
32:export interface MetricSink {
33:  write(line: string): void;
34:}
35:
36:const defaultSink: MetricSink = { write: (line: string) => console.log(line) };
37:
38:function buildEmfLine(namespace: string, point: MetricPoint): Record<string, unknown> {
39:  const dimensionNames = Object.keys(point.dimensions ?? {});
40:  return {
41:    _aws: {
42:      Timestamp: Date.now(),
43:      CloudWatchMetrics: [
44:        {
45:          Namespace: namespace,
46:          Dimensions: dimensionNames.length > 0 ? [dimensionNames] : [[]],
47:          Metrics: [{ Name: point.name, Unit: point.unit }],
48:        },
49:      ],
50:    },
51:    ...point.dimensions,
52:    [point.name]: point.value,
53:  };
54:}
55:
56:/**
57: * Never throws â€” a failure to serialize/write a metric must never take down the business
58: * handler calling it, same discipline `SecureLogger` already follows for logging itself.
59: * `namespace` groups metrics in the CloudWatch console (e.g. `"ExpirationTracker/ReminderDispatch"`).
60: */
61:export function emitMetric(namespace: string, point: MetricPoint, sink: MetricSink = defaultSink): void {
62:  try {
63:    sink.write(JSON.stringify(buildEmfLine(namespace, point)));
64:  } catch {
65:    // Swallowed deliberately â€” see the doc comment above. Nothing to log here either (the
66:    // logger itself could be the thing failing in a truly pathological case); this is the one
67:    // place in the codebase allowed to fail completely silently, by design.
68:  }
69:}
FILE src/shared/dynamodb/occ.ts
1:/**
2: * Optimistic concurrency control helper - implementation-blueprint.md #5.2 / M0 deliverable.
3: *
4: * M0 ships no tables/Lambdas yet (that's M1+), so this module only builds the DynamoDB
5: * command *parameters* for OCC-safe writes; it does not depend on @aws-sdk/client-dynamodb
6: * so it stays testable without AWS credentials/mocks. M1 wires these builders into the
7: * actual DocumentClient calls once tables exist.
8: */
9:
10:export interface EntityKey {
11:  PK: string;
12:  SK: string;
13:}
14:
15:export interface VersionedUpdateInput {
16:  tableName: string;
17:  key: EntityKey;
18:  tenantId: string;
19:  expectedVersion: number;
20:  /** Additional SET clauses beyond version/updatedAt, e.g. { status: "CANCELLED" }. */
21:  set: Record<string, unknown>;
22:  /** Attribute names to REMOVE atomically in the same conditional update - e.g. dropping
23:   * GSI6PK/GSI6SK when a reconciliation-candidate pointer (M3.5) stops applying. Gap noted
24:   * since M3 (occ.ts was SET-only, cancelled GSI3 pointers were left orphaned because
25:   * nothing actively queried them); M3.5 needs REMOVE for real because GSI6 pointers ARE
26:   * actively queried by reconciliation. */
27:  remove?: string[];
28:  now?: string;
29:  /** Extra ConditionExpression clauses ANDed to the base condition, each with its own
30:   * caller-supplied names/values (W3-06 purge worker's claim/fence conditions - e.g. checking
31:   * `legalHold`/`GSI6PK`/`purgeAfter` atomically alongside the version check). Each entry's
32:   * `expression` is wrapped in its own parentheses before being ANDed in, so callers never need
33:   * to hand-balance parens against the base condition. Placeholder names/values MUST NOT collide
34:   * with the base condition's (#version/#tenantId/#updatedAt/:expectedVersion/:tenantId/:one/:now)
35:   * or with any `set`/`remove` generated key (#set<i>/:set<i>/#rem<j>) - buildVersionedUpdate
36:   * throws before building an invalid ConditionExpression rather than silently overwriting one
37:   * caller's placeholder with another's. */
38:  extraConditions?: Array<{ expression: string; names?: Record<string, string>; values?: Record<string, unknown> }>;
39:}
40:
41:export interface DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
42:  TableName: string;
43:  Key: EntityKey;
44:  UpdateExpression: string;
45:  ConditionExpression: string;
46:  /** Optional, not `Record<string, string>` with an empty-object default (Wave B2B-14, D-119
47:   * real finding): DynamoDB's TransactWriteItems rejects an explicitly-empty
48:   * ExpressionAttributeNames outright (`ValidationException: ExpressionAttributeNames must
49:   * not be empty`) - the key must be OMITTED, never `{}`, when a caller has nothing to map.
50:   * `buildVersionedUpdate()` below never hits this (it always maps at least #version/
51:   * #tenantId/#updatedAt), but a hand-rolled Update (update-organization-settings.ts) can. */
52:  ExpressionAttributeNames?: Record<string, string>;
53:  ExpressionAttributeValues: Record<string, unknown>;
54:}
55:
56:interface ScopeCondition {
57:  namePlaceholder: string;
58:  attributeName: string;
59:  valuePlaceholder: string;
60:  value: string;
61:}
62:
63:interface ScopedVersionedUpdateInput {
64:  tableName: string;
65:  key: EntityKey;
66:  expectedVersion: number;
67:  set: Record<string, unknown>;
68:  remove?: string[];
69:  now?: string;
70:  extraConditions?: Array<{ expression: string; names?: Record<string, string>; values?: Record<string, unknown> }>;
71:}
72:
73:/**
74: * Private core shared by `buildVersionedUpdate()` (tenant-fenced, every ordinary business
75: * entity) and `buildAccountScopedVersionedUpdate()` (D-197 fatia 3/5: WhatsApp `WebhookInbox`,
76: * the first entity in this repo with no tenant at all - see
77: * `docs/architecture/reviews/whatsapp-channel-scoping/webhook-inbox-purge-scoping/` for the
78: * 3-round Claude<->Codex protocol, both final notes >=9.0, that decided this shape). The ONLY
79: * difference between the two public builders is which attribute anchors the scope fence
80: * (`tenantId` vs `accountId`) - version increment, `updatedAt`, SET/REMOVE generation,
81: * `extraConditions` composition and placeholder-collision detection are a single implementation,
82: * never duplicated, so the two builders can never drift apart on anything but their fence.
83: */
84:function buildScopedVersionedUpdate(input: ScopedVersionedUpdateInput, scope: ScopeCondition): DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
85:  const now = input.now ?? new Date().toISOString();
86:
87:  const names: Record<string, string> = {
88:    "#version": "version",
89:    [scope.namePlaceholder]: scope.attributeName,
90:    "#updatedAt": "updatedAt",
91:  };
92:  const values: Record<string, unknown> = {
93:    ":expectedVersion": input.expectedVersion,
94:    [scope.valuePlaceholder]: scope.value,
95:    ":one": 1,
96:    ":now": now,
97:  };
98:
99:  const setClauses = ["#version = #version + :one", "#updatedAt = :now"];
100:  let i = 0;
101:  for (const [field, value] of Object.entries(input.set)) {
102:    const nameKey = `#set${i}`;
103:    const valueKey = `:set${i}`;
104:    names[nameKey] = field;
105:    values[valueKey] = value;
106:    setClauses.push(`${nameKey} = ${valueKey}`);
107:    i += 1;
108:  }
109:
110:  const removeClauses: string[] = [];
111:  let j = 0;
112:  for (const field of input.remove ?? []) {
113:    const nameKey = `#rem${j}`;
114:    names[nameKey] = field;
115:    removeClauses.push(nameKey);
116:    j += 1;
117:  }
118:
119:  const expression =
120:    removeClauses.length > 0
121:      ? `SET ${setClauses.join(", ")} REMOVE ${removeClauses.join(", ")}`
122:      : `SET ${setClauses.join(", ")}`;
123:
124:  const baseConditionParts = ["attribute_exists(PK)", "attribute_exists(SK)", "#version = :expectedVersion", `${scope.namePlaceholder} = ${scope.valuePlaceholder}`];
125:  for (const extra of input.extraConditions ?? []) {
126:    for (const [nameKey, name] of Object.entries(extra.names ?? {})) {
127:      if (nameKey in names) throw new Error(`extraConditions name placeholder collides with a reserved/generated key: ${nameKey}`);
128:      names[nameKey] = name;
129:    }
130:    for (const [valueKey, value] of Object.entries(extra.values ?? {})) {
131:      if (valueKey in values) throw new Error(`extraConditions value placeholder collides with a reserved/generated key: ${valueKey}`);
132:      values[valueKey] = value;
133:    }
134:    baseConditionParts.push(`(${extra.expression})`);
135:  }
136:
137:  return {
138:    TableName: input.tableName,
139:    Key: input.key,
140:    UpdateExpression: expression,
141:    ConditionExpression: baseConditionParts.join(" AND "),
142:    ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
143:    ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
144:  };
145:}
146:
147:/**
148: * Builds an UpdateItem input enforcing implementation-blueprint.md #5.2's exact
149: * ConditionExpression: attribute_exists(PK) AND attribute_exists(SK) AND
150: * #version = :expectedVersion AND #tenantId = :tenantId, and increments version atomically.
151: * Contractually unchanged by the account-scoped sibling below - same signature, same generated
152: * expression, byte-for-byte, for every one of this repo's ~40 existing call sites.
153: */
154:export function buildVersionedUpdate(input: VersionedUpdateInput): DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
155:  return buildScopedVersionedUpdate(input, { namePlaceholder: "#tenantId", attributeName: "tenantId", valuePlaceholder: ":tenantId", value: input.tenantId });
156:}
157:
158:export interface AccountScopedVersionedUpdateInput extends ScopedVersionedUpdateInput {
159:  /** External vendor account id (e.g. a WhatsApp Business Account id) - NEVER a tenant id, real
160:   * or fabricated. Fences the update to the row's own account, same OCC-plus-scope-fence
161:   * structure as `buildVersionedUpdate()`, for an entity that genuinely has no tenant. */
162:  accountId: string;
163:}
164:
165:/**
166: * Account-scoped sibling of `buildVersionedUpdate()` (D-197 fatia 3/5) - identical
167: * ConditionExpression shape, `#accountId = :accountId` in place of `#tenantId = :tenantId`. Exists
168: * for exactly one entity today: WhatsApp `WebhookInbox` (`purgeScope: "ACCOUNT"`, permanently
169: * account-owned - see the purge-scoping decision this sibling builder itself depends on). Never
170: * use this for an entity that has (or could ever have) a real tenant - `buildVersionedUpdate()`
171: * is the only builder any tenant-scoped entity may use.
172: */
173:export function buildAccountScopedVersionedUpdate(input: AccountScopedVersionedUpdateInput): DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
174:  return buildScopedVersionedUpdate(input, { namePlaceholder: "#accountId", attributeName: "accountId", valuePlaceholder: ":accountId", value: input.accountId });
175:}
176:
177:export interface DynamoPutCommandInput {
178:  TableName: string;
179:  Item: Record<string, unknown>;
180:  ConditionExpression: string;
181:  ExpressionAttributeNames?: Record<string, string>;
182:  ExpressionAttributeValues?: Record<string, unknown>;
183:}
184:
185:/**
186: * Builds a PutItem input for first-time creation of a versioned entity, per
187: * implementation-blueprint.md #5.2: ConditionExpression attribute_not_exists(PK) AND
188: * attribute_not_exists(SK). Caller supplies `item` already containing PK/SK/tenantId/version=1.
189: */
190:export function buildVersionedCreate(tableName: string, item: Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey): DynamoPutCommandInput {
191:  return {
192:    TableName: tableName,
193:    Item: item,
194:    ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)",
195:  };
196:}
197:
198:/**
199: * Builds a PutItem input that overwrites an existing item gated on a caller-supplied
200: * equality condition over specific fields (not a `version` counter) - the pattern
201: * `TenantQuotaService`'s `updateConditional`/`putIfAbsent` already use standalone
202: * (`identity-store.ts`'s `updateConditional`: full-item PutCommand conditioned on
203: * `count`/`resetAt` still matching what was read, not an UpdateItem). Exists so quota's
204: * transaction-boundary migration (W3-07 writer inventory) can build the same conditional
205: * Put as a `TransactWriteItems` entry via `occ.ts` instead of a hand-written
206: * ConditionExpression, per this module's own "never hand-written ConditionExpression"
207: * discipline. `names`/`values` follow the same collision-checked merge as
208: * `buildVersionedUpdate`'s `extraConditions`.
209: */
210:export function buildConditionalPut(input: {
211:  tableName: string;
212:  item: Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey;
213:  conditionExpression: string;
214:  names?: Record<string, string>;
215:  values?: Record<string, unknown>;
216:}): DynamoPutCommandInput {
217:  return {
218:    TableName: input.tableName,
219:    Item: input.item,
220:    ConditionExpression: input.conditionExpression,
221:    ExpressionAttributeNames: input.names,
222:    ExpressionAttributeValues: input.values,
223:  };
224:}
225:
226:/**
227: * TransactWriteItems entry shapes - moved here from expiration/ports/expiration-store.ts
228: * (2026-08-19, Engineering Maturity Review): that was the only place they were declared,
229: * but reminder/ports/reminder-store.ts already re-exported them from there (a cross-module
230: * ports->ports dependency), and expiration/domain/audit-event.ts imported them directly,
231: * which is a domain->ports boundary violation dependency-cruiser catches but ESLint's
232: * text-matching no-restricted-imports rule cannot (it only matches literal specifier
233: * substrings, and this codebase's relative imports never contain "modules/"). Reuses the
234: * inner Put/Update shapes already defined above instead of re-declaring them a third time.
235: */
236:export interface TransactPutEntry {
237:  Put: DynamoPutCommandInput;
238:}
239:
240:export interface TransactUpdateEntry {
241:  Update: DynamoUpdateCommandInput;
242:}
243:
244:export interface DynamoConditionCheckCommandInput {
245:  TableName: string;
246:  Key: EntityKey;
247:  ConditionExpression: string;
248:  ExpressionAttributeNames?: Record<string, string>;
249:  ExpressionAttributeValues?: Record<string, unknown>;
250:}
251:
252:export interface TransactConditionCheckEntry {
253:  ConditionCheck: DynamoConditionCheckCommandInput;
254:}
255:
256:export interface DynamoDeleteCommandInput {
257:  TableName: string;
258:  Key: EntityKey;
259:  ConditionExpression?: string;
260:  ExpressionAttributeNames?: Record<string, string>;
261:  ExpressionAttributeValues?: Record<string, unknown>;
262:}
263:
264:export interface TransactDeleteEntry {
265:  Delete: DynamoDeleteCommandInput;
266:}
267:
268:/**
269: * Builds a version-conditioned Delete input (W3-06 purge worker's terminal step - a Document
270: * row can only be physically removed if it is still at the exact version/state the claim
271: * observed, same OCC discipline as `buildVersionedUpdate`, just for `DeleteItem` instead of
272: * `UpdateItem`). `extraConditions` uses the same collision-checked merge as
273: * `buildVersionedUpdate` - see that function's docs.
274: */
275:export function buildVersionedDelete(input: {
276:  tableName: string;
277:  key: EntityKey;
278:  tenantId: string;
279:  expectedVersion: number;
280:  extraConditions?: Array<{ expression: string; names?: Record<string, string>; values?: Record<string, unknown> }>;
281:}): DynamoDeleteCommandInput {
282:  const names: Record<string, string> = { "#version": "version", "#tenantId": "tenantId" };
283:  const values: Record<string, unknown> = { ":expectedVersion": input.expectedVersion, ":tenantId": input.tenantId };
284:  const conditionParts = ["attribute_exists(PK)", "attribute_exists(SK)", "#version = :expectedVersion", "#tenantId = :tenantId"];
285:
286:  for (const extra of input.extraConditions ?? []) {
287:    for (const [nameKey, name] of Object.entries(extra.names ?? {})) {
288:      if (nameKey in names) throw new Error(`extraConditions name placeholder collides with a reserved key: ${nameKey}`);
289:      names[nameKey] = name;
290:    }
291:    for (const [valueKey, value] of Object.entries(extra.values ?? {})) {
292:      if (valueKey in values) throw new Error(`extraConditions value placeholder collides with a reserved key: ${valueKey}`);
293:      values[valueKey] = value;
294:    }
295:    conditionParts.push(`(${extra.expression})`);
296:  }
297:
298:  return {
299:    TableName: input.tableName,
300:    Key: input.key,
301:    ConditionExpression: conditionParts.join(" AND "),
302:    ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
303:    ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
304:  };
305:}
306:
307:/**
308: * Sibling of `buildConditionalPut` for `DeleteItem` - symmetric reasoning: `buildVersionedDelete`
309: * above always ANDs `#version = :expectedVersion`, which only makes sense for a versioned
310: * (mutable) entity. An append-only entity that is NEVER updated after creation (e.g. the
311: * `AuditEvent` family - `expiration`/`organization`/`subject`/`activity` modules' audit rows,
312: * D-153) has no `version` counter to check at all, so forcing one through `buildVersionedDelete`
313: * would require faking a field that does not exist on the row. Callers instead supply their own
314: * equality condition over whatever field they observed at scan time (same
315: * "hasn't changed since scan" intent as `buildVersionedDelete`'s `extraConditions`, just without
316: * a version to anchor it to) - `names`/`values` follow the same collision-free-by-caller-control
317: * discipline as `buildConditionalPut` (no generated placeholders to collide with here, since this
318: * builder adds none of its own beyond `attribute_exists`).
319: */
320:export function buildConditionalDelete(input: {
321:  tableName: string;
322:  key: EntityKey;
323:  conditionExpression: string;
324:  names?: Record<string, string>;
325:  values?: Record<string, unknown>;
326:}): DynamoDeleteCommandInput {
327:  return {
328:    TableName: input.tableName,
329:    Key: input.key,
330:    ConditionExpression: input.conditionExpression,
331:    ExpressionAttributeNames: input.names,
332:    ExpressionAttributeValues: input.values,
333:  };
334:}
335:
336:export type TransactWriteEntry = TransactPutEntry | TransactUpdateEntry | TransactConditionCheckEntry | TransactDeleteEntry;
337:
338:/**
339: * Builds a TransactWriteItems `ConditionCheck` entry asserting a row is still at an
340: * expected version (and, optionally, still holds a given attribute value) - used to fence
341: * a commit against a fact read earlier in the same operation but not itself written by
342: * this transaction (e.g. reminder dispatch asserting the ReminderPolicy it read is still
343: * enabled at the version it read, per the BLOCKER-B freshness-fencing fix: reading a row
344: * and later writing elsewhere based on that read is a TOCTOU gap unless the read fact is
345: * re-asserted atomically inside the same TransactWriteItems as the write it gates).
346: */
347:export function buildVersionConditionCheck(input: {
348:  tableName: string;
349:  key: EntityKey;
350:  expectedVersion: number;
351:  /** Extra attribute=value equality conditions beyond version, e.g. { enabled: true, status: "ACTIVE" }. */
352:  extra?: Record<string, unknown>;
353:}): TransactConditionCheckEntry {
354:  const names: Record<string, string> = { "#version": "version" };
355:  const values: Record<string, unknown> = { ":version": input.expectedVersion };
356:  const clauses = ["#version = :version"];
357:
358:  let i = 0;
359:  for (const [field, value] of Object.entries(input.extra ?? {})) {
360:    const nameKey = `#c${i}`;
361:    const valueKey = `:c${i}`;
362:    names[nameKey] = field;
363:    values[valueKey] = value;
364:    clauses.push(`${nameKey} = ${valueKey}`);
365:    i += 1;
366:  }
367:
368:  return {
369:    ConditionCheck: {
370:      TableName: input.tableName,
371:      Key: input.key,
372:      ConditionExpression: clauses.join(" AND "),
373:      ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
374:      ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
375:    },
376:  };
377:}
378:
379:/**
380: * Sibling of `buildVersionConditionCheck` for facts that have no version to pin - e.g.
381: * BLOCKER-B's ITEM-scoped-policy integrity check (a policy references an item's
382: * existence/status/tenant, not a specific item version - referencing an item doesn't pin
383: * it to a version the way an occurrence's materialization does). `extra` must include at
384: * least one condition (an existence-only check with no `extra` would degenerate to
385: * `attribute_exists(PK)`, which every real row satisfies trivially - not useful on its own).
386: */
387:export function buildExistenceConditionCheck(input: {
388:  tableName: string;
389:  key: EntityKey;
390:  extra: Record<string, unknown>;
391:}): TransactConditionCheckEntry {
392:  const names: Record<string, string> = {};
393:  const values: Record<string, unknown> = {};
394:  const clauses = ["attribute_exists(PK)"];
395:
396:  let i = 0;
397:  for (const [field, value] of Object.entries(input.extra)) {
398:    const nameKey = `#c${i}`;
399:    const valueKey = `:c${i}`;
400:    names[nameKey] = field;
401:    values[valueKey] = value;
402:    clauses.push(`${nameKey} = ${valueKey}`);
403:    i += 1;
404:  }
405:
406:  return {
407:    ConditionCheck: {
408:      TableName: input.tableName,
409:      Key: input.key,
410:      ConditionExpression: clauses.join(" AND "),
411:      ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
412:      ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
413:    },
414:  };
415:}
416:
417:/** Name of the AWS SDK error thrown when any entry in a TransactWriteItems call fails its
418: * ConditionExpression - used by callers to distinguish OCC/idempotency conflicts from other
419: * DynamoDB errors without importing the SDK type. */
420:export const TRANSACTION_CANCELED = "TransactionCanceledException";
421:
422:export function isTransactionCanceled(err: unknown): boolean {
423:  return (
424:    typeof err === "object" &&
425:    err !== null &&
426:    "name" in err &&
427:    (err as { name?: unknown }).name === TRANSACTION_CANCELED
428:  );
429:}
430:
431:/**
432: * Reads the per-item `CancellationReasons` array off a `TransactionCanceledException` (verify
433: * with `isTransactionCanceled` first) - the real AWS SDK error carries one entry per transact
434: * item, each with a `Code` ("None" for an item that did not cause the cancellation,
435: * "ConditionalCheckFailed" for one that did). Wave B2B-5 (D-095, Codex Rodada 3 achado): a
436: * `TransactWriteItems` with more than one conditioned entry must never collapse "the
437: * transaction was canceled" into "MY condition specifically failed" - a caller distinguishing,
438: * say, a cap-guard `Update` at index 0 from an unrelated `Put` collision at index 2 needs the
439: * reason for the SPECIFIC index it cares about, not just the fact that something failed.
440: * Returns `undefined` (never throws) for any error shape that doesn't carry this array - callers
441: * fall back to treating the cancellation as "index unknown" rather than guessing.
442: */
443:export function getCancellationReasonCodes(err: unknown): string[] | undefined {
444:  if (typeof err !== "object" || err === null || !("CancellationReasons" in err)) return undefined;
445:  const reasons = (err as { CancellationReasons?: unknown }).CancellationReasons;
446:  if (!Array.isArray(reasons)) return undefined;
447:  return reasons.map((reason) => (typeof reason === "object" && reason !== null && "Code" in reason ? String((reason as { Code?: unknown }).Code) : "Unknown"));
448:}
449:
450:/**
451: * Builds an UpdateItem input for a narrow, single-purpose guard write: set one attribute exactly
452: * once, gated on it not already existing - no `version` counter, no `tenantId` condition (unlike
453: * `buildVersionedUpdate`, whose reserved condition requires `tenantId` and is therefore unusable
454: * for a genuinely tenantless entity). Wave B2B-5 (D-095, Codex Rodada 2/3 achado): `GlobalUser`
455: * (identity/persistence/global-user-repository.ts) is tenantless, so its
456: * `hasCreatedOrganization` cap flag needs this instead. Not a general-purpose OCC update - an
457: * entity that needs real optimistic concurrency later should get its own tenantless variant of
458: * `buildVersionedUpdate`, not a generalization of this narrow helper.
459: */
460:export function buildAttributeOnceUpdate(input: { tableName: string; key: EntityKey; attribute: string; value: unknown }): DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
461:  return {
462:    TableName: input.tableName,
463:    Key: input.key,
464:    UpdateExpression: "SET #attr = :value",
465:    ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(#attr)",
466:    ExpressionAttributeNames: { "#attr": input.attribute },
467:    ExpressionAttributeValues: { ":value": input.value },
468:  };
469:}
470:
471:/** Name of the AWS SDK error thrown when a ConditionExpression fails - used by callers
472: * to distinguish OCC conflicts from other DynamoDB errors without importing the SDK type. */
473:export const CONDITIONAL_CHECK_FAILED = "ConditionalCheckFailedException";
474:
475:export function isConditionalCheckFailed(err: unknown): boolean {
476:  return (
477:    typeof err === "object" &&
478:    err !== null &&
479:    "name" in err &&
480:    (err as { name?: unknown }).name === CONDITIONAL_CHECK_FAILED
481:  );
482:}
