89:export interface OutboxRecord {
90:  PK: string;
91:  SK: string;
92:  entityType: "OutboxEvent";
93:  /** BLOCKER-B addition: previously only embedded in `PK` (`TENANT#<t>#OUTBOX#<shard>`),
94:   * never its own field - a sender whose payload doesn't already self-describe its tenant
95:   * (unlike DispatchCommand) needs this explicitly, without every caller re-parsing `PK`. */
96:  tenantId: string;
97:  eventId: string;
98:  eventType: string;
99:  aggregateType: string;
100:  aggregateId: string;
101:  aggregateVersion: number;
102:  status: OutboxStatus;
103:  occurredAt: string;
104:  payload: Record<string, unknown>;
105:  publishAttempts: number;
106:  nextAttemptAt: string;
107:  createdAt: string;
108:  GSI6PK: string;
109:  GSI6SK: string;
110:  destination?: OutboxDestination;
111:  /**
112:   * m5-observability-design.md #2: copied from `DomainEvent.correlationId` (already mandatory
113:   * on every event) at write time - never read from the ambient AsyncLocalStorage context here,
114:   * so this stays a pure, environment-agnostic builder. Optional only because records persisted
115:   * before M5 don't have it (historical data, never written without it again) - readers
116:   * (relay/sweeper) must fall back to `eventId` when absent, per the design's fallback table.
117:   */
118:  correlationId?: string;
119:}
120:
121:/** Monthly shard for the outbox partition, matching #5.3's `TENANT#t#OUTBOX#202608` example. */
122:function monthShard(isoTimestamp: string): string {
123:  return isoTimestamp.slice(0, 7).replace("-", "");
124:}
125:
126:export function buildOutboxRecord(event: DomainEvent, destination?: OutboxDestination): OutboxRecord {
127:  const shard = monthShard(event.occurredAt);
128:  return {
129:    PK: `TENANT#${event.tenantId}#OUTBOX#${shard}`,
130:    SK: `EVENT#${event.occurredAt}#${event.eventId}`,
131:    entityType: "OutboxEvent",
132:    tenantId: event.tenantId,
133:    eventId: event.eventId,
134:    eventType: event.eventType,
135:    aggregateType: event.aggregate.type,
136:    aggregateId: event.aggregate.id,
137:    aggregateVersion: event.aggregate.version,
138:    status: "PENDING",
139:    occurredAt: event.occurredAt,
140:    payload: event.data,
141:    publishAttempts: 0,
142:    nextAttemptAt: event.occurredAt,
143:    createdAt: event.occurredAt,
144:    GSI6PK: "RECON#OUTBOX#PENDING",
145:    GSI6SK: `${event.occurredAt}#${event.eventId}`,
146:    correlationId: event.correlationId,
147:    ...(destination ? { destination } : {}),
148:  };
149:}
150:
151:/**
152: * m5-observability-design.md #2: correlation id a relay/sweeper should use for the record
153: * being processed - read from the persisted `OutboxRecord`, never from the caller's own
154: * ambient context. Falls back to `eventId` only for records persisted before M5 (missing
155: * `correlationId`); this is historical-data compatibility, not a new write path.
156: */
157:export function outboxRecordCorrelationId(record: OutboxRecord): string {
158:  return record.correlationId ?? record.eventId;
159:}
160:
161:/** @deprecated kept as an alias of the shared `TransactPutEntry` (src/shared/dynamodb/occ.ts)
162: * for backward compatibility with existing imports - full-audit round1/qualidade found this
163: * was a duplicate, structurally-identical declaration of the same shape (same pattern as the
164: * expiration-store.ts/reminder-store.ts duplication decisions-log.md E-008 already fixed).
165: * Do not add fields here; add them to the shared type instead. */
166:export type DynamoTransactPutEntry = TransactPutEntry;
167:
168:/**
169: * OutboxPort.append from implementation-blueprint.md #5.1: appends the outbox Put to a
170: * caller-supplied TransactWriteItems array (`tx`) rather than writing directly - the caller
171: * owns the transaction and includes the aggregate's own conditional update alongside it.
172: * Accepts the full `TransactWriteEntry` union (Put | Update) so callers whose transaction
173: * also contains Update entries (e.g. reminder dispatch's CLAIMED->TRIGGERED transition) can
174: * pass their real array directly, without an unsafe cast to satisfy a narrower Put-only type.
175: */
176:export function appendToTransaction(
177:  tx: TransactWriteEntry[],
178:  tableName: string,
179:  event: DomainEvent,
180:  destination?: OutboxDestination,
181:): void {
182:  const record = buildOutboxRecord(event, destination);
183:  tx.push({
184:    Put: {
185:      TableName: tableName,
186:      Item: { ...record },
187:      ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)",
188:    },
189:  });
190:}
191:
192:/** Backoff schedule for OutboxPublisher retries (#5.3 step 6: "incrementa tentativas e
193: * aplica backoff"). Exponential with a cap; judgment call - blueprint doesn't pin exact values. */
194:export function nextAttemptDelayMs(publishAttempts: number): number {
195:  const base = 1_000;
196:  const cap = 5 * 60_000;
197:  return Math.min(cap, base * 2 ** publishAttempts);
198:}

1:/**
2: * ReminderProducer core logic - implementation-blueprint.md Â§9.3. Pure(ish) - takes an
3: * injected `ReminderProducerStore` and clock, no AWS SDK/Lambda runtime dependency, so it
4: * is directly unit/integration-testable with a fake clock and fake GSI3 index (per the
5: * task's requirement to test time-sensitive logic without real wall-clock waiting).
6: *
7: * Steps (Â§9.3):
8: *  1. read active shard generations (current + still-active legacy, Â§9.2's reshard runbook);
9: *  2. query GSI3 across N shards of minute M AND a lookback window [M-lookback, M] (default
10: *     5 minutes) - "sem lookback, 'o prÃ³ximo tick reprocessa' Ã© falso";
11: *  3. per eligible occurrence, conditional SCHEDULED -> CLAIMED with a short claim
12: *     `claimExpiresAt`, and emit one `reminder.dispatch.v1` command;
13: *  4. partial-batch-failure handling: only failed entries are surfaced in `failed`, callers
14: *     retry just those (never the whole batch);
15: *  5. returns counters for `scheduler_lag_seconds`/per-shard/lookback-depth metrics (left to
16: *     the Lambda handler to emit via SecureLogger/EMF - this module stays observability-agnostic).
17: *
18: * M10 cluster 4 (D-039/D-046/D-048): GSI3 is now a SHARED scheduler for two entity types,
19: * discriminated by the SHAPE of the GSI3SK read back from the index (`...#OCCURRENCE#...` for
20: * `ReminderOccurrence`, unchanged; `...#CHASING#...` for `DocumentChasingOccurrence`, new) - a
21: * single `queryGsi3` call for a given shard/minute can return BOTH types mixed together, since
22: * they share the same physical partition space. The reminder branch below is byte-for-byte
23: * identical to before this change (same transaction shape, same command, same event) - only the
24: * dispatch/routing at the top of the loop is new. An unrecognized GSI3SK shape (neither pattern
25: * matches) is fail-closed: counted in `unknownEntityType` (a SEPARATE counter from `failed` -
26: * an unrecognized row never has a real occurrenceId/tenantId to put there) - the Lambda handler
27: * (reminder-producer-handler.ts) throws whenever it's nonzero, via `shouldAlarm()` below, so a
28: * real CloudWatch alarm fires - never processed or silently skipped by omission.
29: */

44:export interface DispatchCommand {
45:  /**
46:   * Real fix for a pre-existing bug found during M5's observability review (registered in
47:   * NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md): `schemas/queues/reminder-dispatch.v1.json` extends
48:   * `command-envelope.v1.json` via `allOf`, which already required `messageVersion`/
49:   * `messageId`/`createdAt`/`correlationId` at the top level - but this command never
50:   * carried them, so `reminder-dispatch-handler.ts`'s own schema validation against the
51:   * real SQS body would always reject it as schema-invalid. This is a bug fix making the
52:   * implementation match its own already-approved v1 contract, not a new schema version.
53:   */
54:  messageVersion: 1;
55:  messageId: string;
56:  createdAt: string;
57:  correlationId: string;
58:  commandType: "reminder.dispatch.v1";
59:  tenantId: string;
60:  deduplicationKey: string;
61:  data: {
62:    itemId: string;

71:export interface ProducerDeps {
72:  store: ReminderProducerStore;
73:  shardConfig: ShardConfig;
74:  tableName: string;
75:  now: () => string;
76:  /** Short claim TTL - default 2 minutes, comfortably longer than one producer tick (1 minute) but short enough that a crashed dispatch worker's claim is reclaimable quickly by reconciliation (Â§9.5). */
77:  claimTtlMs?: number;
78:  /**
79:   * Lookback window in minutes, inclusive of the current minute (implementation-blueprint.md
80:   * Â§9.3 example: [M-5min, M]). Default raised from 5 to 15 (PERF-12 1k load test,
81:   * docs/engineering/performance/results/PERF-12-async-pipeline-1k.md): under a realistic
82:   * burst (many occurrences due the same minute) the producer's 10s timeout could be
83:   * exceeded for several consecutive minutes, and any occurrence still SCHEDULED once its
84:   * minute aged out of a 5-minute lookback was never reconciled by any other pass (see
85:   * reconciliation.ts's own file header - CLAIMS reconciliation only reverts already-CLAIMED
86:   * occurrences, DST reconciliation only re-materializes missing ones; neither covers
87:   * "SCHEDULED, past lookback, never claimed"). 15 minutes gives roughly 2x margin over the
88:   * observed 7-minute burst-recovery window, at a bounded extra cost (shardCount Ã—
89:   * lookbackMinutes extra GSI3 partition queries per NORMAL tick, most of them empty) - a
90:   * dedicated reconciliation pass for this case was considered but rejected: GSI3 query
91:   * access is deliberately isolated to ONLY this worker (reminder-store.ts's own doc comment,
92:   * enforced by test/integration/gsi3-isolation.test.ts and infra/main.tf's "the ONLY
93:   * function granted gsi3_read" policy comment) - widening the SAME worker's own lookback,
94:   * combined with the timeout raise and the parallelism below (both fix the actual cause,
95:   * this is the safety net), stays inside that existing boundary instead of punching a new
96:   * hole in it.
97:   */
98:  lookbackMinutes?: number;
99:  /** Deterministic-in-tests ID generator for the durable outbox event written in the same
100:   * transaction as the claim (M3.5 "DecisÃ£o central: outbox durÃ¡vel" - see runProducerTick). */
101:  newEventId: () => string;
102:  correlationId: () => string;
103:}

105:export interface ProducerTickResult {
106:  claimed: DispatchCommand[];
107:  /** M10 cluster 4: DocumentChasingOccurrence claims from the SAME GSI3 scan, kept in a
108:   * separate array (different command shape) - never merged into `claimed`. */
109:  chasingClaimed: ChasingDispatchCommand[];
110:  failed: { occurrenceId: string; tenantId: string; error: unknown }[];
111:  /** GSI3SK shape matched neither the reminder nor the chasing pattern - fail-closed, never
112:   * processed. Should always be 0 in practice; a nonzero count is alarm-worthy (D-039). */
113:  unknownEntityType: number;
114:  scanned: number;
115:  minutesScanned: string[];
116:  shardPartitionsScanned: number;
117:}
118:
119:function minuteFloor(d: Date): Date {
120:  return new Date(Math.floor(d.getTime() / 60_000) * 60_000);
121:}
122:
123:/**
124: * PERF-12 1k load test finding (docs/engineering/performance/results/PERF-12-async-pipeline-1k.md):
125: * this loop used to process one occurrence at a time - one `store.get` + one
126: * `store.transactWrite` per occurrence, fully sequential - inside a Lambda with (at the time)
127: * a 10s timeout. With ~250 occurrences concentrated in one shard/minute under a realistic
128: * burst, that budget was nowhere near enough, the function was killed mid-loop repeatedly, and
129: * occurrences that hadn't been claimed yet eventually aged out of the lookback window,
130: * permanently stuck in SCHEDULED. Bounded concurrency here mirrors the exact pattern already
131: * used for the same reason in reminder-dispatch-handler.ts (D-170) - each occurrence's own
132: * `transactWrite` stays a single, independent atomic conditional claim (never merged across
133: * occurrences), so parallelizing here does not touch the transactional or idempotency
134: * guarantees of any individual claim, only how many claims run concurrently. A plain numeric
135: * constant, not unbounded `Promise.all`, to keep the burst of concurrent DynamoDB round trips
136: * per invocation predictable.
137: */
138:const PRODUCER_CLAIM_CONCURRENCY = 8;
139:
140:/**
141: * Pure alarm decision, extracted so the Lambda handler's "when should this tick throw" logic
142: * is unit-testable without mocking the whole handler/composition root (achado real de revisÃ£o
143: * adversarial, D-039/D-046/D-048: an earlier version of this fail-closed path was silently
144: * absorbed by the handler - `unknownEntityType` was counted but nothing ever read it). Two
145: * independent reasons, checked in this order for a stable, deterministic message:
146: * unrecognized GSI3SK shape (fail-closed, never a retry-race) first, then genuine per-occurrence
147: * claim failures.
148: */
149:export function shouldAlarm(result: ProducerTickResult): { alarm: boolean; reason?: string } {
150:  if (result.unknownEntityType > 0) {
151:    return { alarm: true, reason: `reminder-producer: ${result.unknownEntityType} GSI3 row(s) matched neither the reminder nor the chasing entityType - fail-closed` };
152:  }
153:  if (result.failed.length > 0) {
154:    return { alarm: true, reason: `reminder-producer: ${result.failed.length} occurrence(s) failed to claim` };
155:  }
156:  return { alarm: false };
157:}
158:
159:/** Runs one producer tick for wall-clock minute `tickMinute` (already floored to the minute by the caller/Lambda trigger). */
160:export async function runProducerTick(deps: ProducerDeps, tickMinute: Date): Promise<ProducerTickResult> {
161:  const generations = activeGenerations(deps.shardConfig, deps.now());
162:  const lookback = deps.lookbackMinutes ?? 15;
163:  const claimTtlMs = deps.claimTtlMs ?? 2 * 60_000;
164:
165:  const minutesToScan: Date[] = [];
166:  for (let i = lookback; i >= 0; i--) {
167:    minutesToScan.push(minuteFloor(new Date(tickMinute.getTime() - i * 60_000)));
168:  }
169:
170:  const claimed: DispatchCommand[] = [];
171:  const chasingClaimed: ChasingDispatchCommand[] = [];
172:  const failed: { occurrenceId: string; tenantId: string; error: unknown }[] = [];
173:  let unknownEntityType = 0;
174:  let scanned = 0;
175:  let partitions = 0;
176:
177:  // Dedup guard: the same occurrence could show up in more than one scanned minute only if
178:  // materialization ever wrote it under a stale bucket, which shouldn't happen - but a
179:  // Set guards against double-claiming within a single tick regardless.
180:  const seen = new Set<string>();
181:
182:  for (const minute of minutesToScan) {
183:    for (const generation of generations) {
184:      const partitionKeys = gsi3PartitionsForMinute(minute, generation.shardCount);
185:      partitions += partitionKeys.length;
186:      for (const gsi3pk of partitionKeys) {
187:        const rows = await deps.store.queryGsi3<{ PK: string; SK: string; GSI3SK: string }>({ gsi3pk });
188:        // PERF-12: this used to be `for (const row of rows)`, one `get`+`transactWrite` pair
189:        // processed strictly sequentially - see PRODUCER_CLAIM_CONCURRENCY's doc comment for
190:        // why bounded concurrency replaces that here. `seen`/`claimed`/`failed`/
191:        // `chasingClaimed`/counters are shared mutable state across concurrent callbacks, but
192:        // every access happens either synchronously (no `await` in between, so the JS event
193:        // loop cannot interleave two callbacks' synchronous sections) or as an atomic
194:        // push/increment - never a race in practice under Node's single-threaded model.
195:        await mapWithConcurrency(rows, PRODUCER_CLAIM_CONCURRENCY, async (row) => {
196:          scanned += 1;
197:
198:          // M10 cluster 4: try the chasing shape FIRST (never throws, `undefined` on no
199:          // match) - the reminder branch below is completely unchanged otherwise.
200:          const chasingParsed = parseChasingGsi3Sk(row.GSI3SK);
201:          if (chasingParsed) {
202:            if (seen.has(chasingParsed.occurrenceId)) return;
203:            seen.add(chasingParsed.occurrenceId);
204:            try {
205:              const outcome = await claimChasingOccurrence(
206:                { store: deps.store, tableName: deps.tableName, now: deps.now, claimTtlMs, newEventId: deps.newEventId, correlationId: deps.correlationId },
207:                { PK: row.PK, SK: row.SK },
208:              );
209:              if (outcome.kind === "CLAIMED") chasingClaimed.push(outcome.command);
210:              // SKIPPED_NOT_SCHEDULED / LOST_CLAIM_RACE: not failures, same as the reminder path below.
211:            } catch (err) {
212:              failed.push({ occurrenceId: chasingParsed.occurrenceId, tenantId: chasingParsed.tenantId, error: err });
213:            }
214:            return;
215:          }
216:
217:          let reminderParsed: { tenantId: string; occurrenceId: string };
218:          try {
219:            reminderParsed = parseGsi3Sk(row.GSI3SK);
220:          } catch {
221:            // Fail-closed (D-039): neither pattern matched - never process a row we can't
222:            // identify, never skip it silently either. Alarm-worthy, surfaced via the tick result.
223:            unknownEntityType += 1;
224:            return;
225:          }
226:          const { tenantId, occurrenceId } = reminderParsed;
227:          if (seen.has(occurrenceId)) return;
228:          seen.add(occurrenceId);
229:
230:          try {
231:            const occurrence = await deps.store.get<ReminderOccurrence>({ PK: row.PK, SK: row.SK });
232:            if (!occurrence || occurrence.status !== "SCHEDULED") {
233:              // Already claimed/cancelled/triggered by a prior tick or race - not a failure.
234:              return;
235:            }
236:
237:            const claimExpiresAt = new Date(Date.parse(deps.now()) + claimTtlMs).toISOString();
238:            const newVersion = occurrence.version + 1;
239:            const now = deps.now();
240:            // Same value used for both the command's own envelope correlationId (read
241:            // directly from the SQS body per m5-observability-design.md #2's general SQS
242:            // rule) and the outbox DomainEvent's correlationId - one business correlation
243:            // per dispatch, not two independently-generated ids for the same operation.
244:            const correlationId = deps.correlationId();
245:
246:            const command: DispatchCommand = {
247:              messageVersion: 1,
248:              messageId: deps.newEventId(),
249:              createdAt: now,
250:              correlationId,
251:              commandType: "reminder.dispatch.v1",
252:              tenantId,
253:              deduplicationKey: `${tenantId}|${occurrenceId}|${newVersion}`,
254:              data: {
255:                itemId: occurrence.itemId,
256:                occurrenceId,
257:                occurrenceVersion: newVersion,
258:                scheduledAt: occurrence.scheduledAt,
259:                itemVersion: occurrence.itemVersion,
260:                policyVersion: occurrence.policyVersion,
261:              },
262:            };
263:
264:            // M3.5 "DecisÃ£o central: outbox durÃ¡vel": TransactWrite(claim) followed by a
265:            // direct SendMessage in the handler is NOT atomic (Lambda can die between the
266:            // two steps, leaving a CLAIMED occurrence with no queued command - neither the
267:            // producer's own lookback window nor the daily DST reconciliation reliably
268:            // covers that gap). The fix: write a durable OutboxEvent
269:            // (destination=SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1) in the SAME transaction as the claim;
270:            // DispatchOutboxRelay (DynamoDB Streams) publishes it to SQS, a sweeper recovers
271:            // publication failures. Also set the GSI6 WORKSTATE#CLAIMED pointer so the
272:            // reconciliation job can find this claim if it expires unrecovered - removed
273:            // when ReminderDispatch advances to TRIGGERED or reconciliation reverts it.
274:            const event: DomainEvent = {
275:              specVersion: "1.0",
276:              eventId: deps.newEventId(),
277:              eventType: "ReminderDispatchRequested",
278:              source: "expiration-tracker.reminder-producer",
279:              occurredAt: now,
280:              correlationId,
281:              tenantId,
282:              actor: { type: "SYSTEM" },
283:              aggregate: { type: "ReminderOccurrence", id: occurrenceId, version: newVersion },
284:              data: command as unknown as Record<string, unknown>,
285:            };
286:            const outboxEntries: DynamoTransactPutEntry[] = [];
287:            appendToTransaction(outboxEntries, deps.tableName, event, "SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1");
288:
289:            await deps.store.transactWrite([
290:              {
291:                Update: buildVersionedUpdate({
292:                  tableName: deps.tableName,
293:                  key: { PK: row.PK, SK: row.SK },
294:                  tenantId,
295:                  expectedVersion: occurrence.version,
296:                  set: {
297:                    status: "CLAIMED",
298:                    claimedAt: deps.now(),
299:                    claimExpiresAt,
300:                    GSI6PK: GSI6PK_WORKSTATE_CLAIMED,
301:                    GSI6SK: buildExpiredClaimGsi6Sk(claimExpiresAt, tenantId, occurrenceId),
302:                  },
303:                }),
304:              },
305:              ...outboxEntries,
306:            ]);
307:
308:            claimed.push(command);
309:          } catch (err) {
310:            if (isTransactionCanceled(err)) {
311:              // Lost the claim race to a concurrent producer tick - not a failure to retry.
312:              return;
313:            }
314:            failed.push({ occurrenceId, tenantId, error: err });
315:          }
316:        });
317:      }
318:    }
319:  }
320:
321:  return {
322:    claimed,
323:    chasingClaimed,
324:    failed,
325:    unknownEntityType,
326:    scanned,
327:    minutesScanned: minutesToScan.map((d) => d.toISOString()),
328:    shardPartitionsScanned: partitions,
329:  };
330:}

1:/** Real handler for ReminderProducer (EventBridge Scheduler, 1 min), replacing the 501
2: * placeholder. EventBridge Scheduler's Lambda target does NOT wrap the payload in a
3: * `detail` envelope the way legacy EventBridge Rules do - the event IS whatever `input`
4: * the schedule sets (infra/lib/reminder-schedule.ts), never `event.time` (a Rule-shaped
5: * field that doesn't exist for Scheduler invocations - bug found by Codex implementation
6: * review, event.time was always undefined -> Invalid Date). */

8:import { createDocumentClient } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/client.js";
9:import { buildReminderProducerDeps } from "../composition/reminder.js";
10:import { runProducerTick, shouldAlarm } from "../../../workers/reminder-producer/producer.js";

18:const tableName = process.env["TABLE_NAME"];
19:if (!tableName) throw new Error("TABLE_NAME env var is required.");
20:const deps = buildReminderProducerDeps(client, tableName);
21:const logger = new SecureLogger({ baseContext: { service: "reminder-producer" } });
22:const NAMESPACE = "ExpirationTracker/ReminderProducer";
23:
24:export interface ReminderProducerEvent {
25:  scheduledTime: string;
26:}

28:export const handler = withHandlerTiming<ReminderProducerEvent, void>(NAMESPACE, "reminder-producer handler", async (event) => {
29:  if (!event.scheduledTime) {
30:    throw new ValidationError("reminder-producer: missing scheduledTime in event payload.");
31:  }
32:  const tickMinute = new Date(event.scheduledTime);
33:  if (Number.isNaN(tickMinute.getTime())) {
34:    throw new ValidationError("reminder-producer: scheduledTime is not a valid date.", { scheduledTime: event.scheduledTime });
35:  }
36:  // m5-observability-design.md #2: EventBridge Scheduler producer, no upstream request to
37:  // inherit a correlationId from - new UUID per invocation.
38:  await runWithContext({ correlationId: randomUUID() }, async () => {
39:    const result = await timeSpan(NAMESPACE, "lambda.business_operation_ms", "reminder-producer business operation timing", () =>
40:      runProducerTick({ ...deps, shardConfig: defaultShardConfig() }, tickMinute),
41:    );
42:    logger.info("reminder-producer tick complete", {
43:      scanned: result.scanned,
44:      claimed: result.claimed.length,
45:      chasingClaimed: result.chasingClaimed.length,
46:      failed: result.failed.length,
47:      unknownEntityType: result.unknownEntityType,
48:      minutesScanned: result.minutesScanned,
49:    });
50:    // Achado real de revisÃ£o adversarial (Codex, D-039/D-046/D-048): o comentÃ¡rio original de
51:    // producer.ts prometia que `unknownEntityType` seria "surfaced via failed so a real alarm
52:    // can fire", mas nada de fato lanÃ§ava por causa dele - uma linha de GSI3 com forma
53:    // desconhecida ficava silenciosa em produÃ§Ã£o. Corrigido: decisÃ£o de alarme extraÃ­da para
54:    // `shouldAlarm()` (funÃ§Ã£o pura, testada diretamente), separada de `failed` (uma linha
55:    // desconhecida nunca teria occurrenceId/tenantId reais para colocar lÃ¡).
56:    const alarm = shouldAlarm(result);
57:    if (alarm.alarm) {
58:      throw new Error(alarm.reason);
59:    }
60:  });
61:});
FILE src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-reconciliation-handler.ts
1:/** Real handler for ReminderReconciliation (EventBridge Scheduler, `mode` field), replacing
2: * the 501 placeholder. Two schedules invoke the same Lambda with different `mode`
3: * (m3.5-runtime-design.md Â§"ReconciliaÃ§Ã£o"): CLAIMS (every 5 min) and DST (daily). Also one
4: * of exactly two roles granted gsi6Read() - see infra/lib/dynamo-table.ts.
5: *
6: * EventBridge Scheduler's Lambda target does NOT wrap the payload in a `detail` envelope
7: * the way legacy EventBridge Rules do - the event IS whatever `input` the schedule sets
8: * (infra/lib/reminder-schedule.ts: `{mode, scheduledTime}` at the top level), never
9: * `event.detail.mode` (bug found by Codex implementation review - event.detail was always
10: * undefined, both schedules would throw on every invocation). */
11:import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
12:import { createDocumentClient } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/client.js";
13:import { buildReconciliationDeps } from "../composition/reminder.js";
14:import { runReconciliation, type DstReconciliationCandidate as FullDstCandidate } from "../../../workers/reminder-reconciliation/reconciliation.js";
15:import { itemKey } from "../../../modules/expiration/domain/expiration-item.js";
16:import { authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity } from "../../../modules/identity/domain/authorization.js";
17:import { policyKey, type ReminderPolicy } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/reminder-policy.js";
18:import { defaultShardConfig } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.js";
19:import type { ReminderOccurrence } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/reminder-occurrence.js";
20:import { defaultSchemaRegistry } from "../../../shared/contracts/schema-validator.js";
21:import { runWithContext } from "../../../shared/observability/context.js";
22:import { SecureLogger } from "../../../shared/observability/logger.js";
23:
24:const client = createDocumentClient();
25:const envTableName = process.env["TABLE_NAME"];
26:if (!envTableName) throw new Error("TABLE_NAME env var is required.");
27:const tableName: string = envTableName;
28:const { store, candidateSource, now } = buildReconciliationDeps(client, tableName);
29:const shardConfig = defaultShardConfig();
30:const logger = new SecureLogger({ baseContext: { service: "reminder-reconciliation" } });
31:
32:export interface ReminderReconciliationEvent {
33:  mode: "CLAIMS" | "DST";
34:  scheduledTime: string;
35:}
36:
37:/** Hard cap on pages drained per invocation - matches the design's "pÃ¡gina lÃ³gica mÃ¡x. 200"
38: * per page with a bounded number of pages, so a pathological backlog can't make a single
39: * invocation run indefinitely; anything beyond this is picked up by the NEXT scheduled
40: * run (every 5 min for claims, daily for DST) rather than blocking this one. */
41:const MAX_PAGES = 25;
42:
43:const RECONCILIATION_EVENT_SCHEMA_ID = "https://expiration-tracker/schemas/events/reminder-reconciliation-event.v1.json";
44:
45:export async function handler(event: unknown): Promise<void> {
46:  // m5-observability-design.md #2: EventBridge Scheduler producer, no upstream request to
47:  // inherit a correlationId from - new UUID per invocation.
48:  await runWithContext({ correlationId: randomUUID() }, async () => {
49:    // P2.1 (external audit 2026-09-11): `event` was previously typed (not runtime-validated)
50:    // as `ReminderReconciliationEvent` straight off the Lambda invocation - a TS annotation
51:    // enforces nothing at runtime. Same schema-registry pattern reminder-dispatch-handler.ts's
52:    // `DISPATCH_COMMAND_SCHEMA_ID` already uses for its own boundary, mirrored here.
53:    const { valid, errors } = defaultSchemaRegistry.validate(RECONCILIATION_EVENT_SCHEMA_ID, event);
54:    if (!valid) {
55:      logger.error("reminder-reconciliation schema-invalid event", { errors });
56:      throw new Error("schema-invalid ReminderReconciliationEvent");
57:    }
58:    await handleReconciliation(event as ReminderReconciliationEvent);
59:  });
60:}
61:
62:async function handleReconciliation(event: ReminderReconciliationEvent): Promise<void> {
63:  const mode = event.mode;
64:
65:  const expiredClaimCandidates: ReminderOccurrence[] = [];
66:  const dstCandidates: FullDstCandidate[] = [];
67:
68:  if (mode === "CLAIMS") {
69:    // GSI6 is ALL-projected - each candidate row already IS the full ReminderOccurrence
70:    // item (see ExpiredClaimCandidate's doc comment), so no extra fetch is needed.
71:    // Pagination is drained (bug found by Codex implementation review: the first version
72:    // only ever read page 1, silently leaving any backlog over one page unprocessed).
73:    let cursor: string | undefined;
74:    for (let page = 0; page < MAX_PAGES; page++) {
75:      const result = await candidateSource.listExpiredClaims({ before: now(), cursor });
76:      expiredClaimCandidates.push(...(result.items as unknown as ReminderOccurrence[]));
77:      if (!result.cursor) break;
78:      cursor = result.cursor;
79:    }
80:  }
81:
82:  if (mode === "DST") {
83:    // ReminderMaterializer (M3.5) writes a WORKSTATE#DST_PENDING GSI6 pointer whenever a
84:    // trigger's schedule was computed at an ambiguous/nonexistent local time, OR whose
85:    // timeZone observes DST at all (see reminder-materializer.ts - broadened per Codex
86:    // implementation review: a non-ambiguous 09:00 reminder in a DST-observing zone can
87:    // still need re-evaluation after an offset change, not just literally-ambiguous ones).
88:    // This query finds those candidates; occurrences in a fixed-offset timeZone never
89:    // appear here at all.
90:    const windowStart = now();
91:    const windowEnd = new Date(Date.parse(windowStart) + 7 * 24 * 60 * 60_000).toISOString();
92:    let cursor: string | undefined;
93:    for (let page = 0; page < MAX_PAGES; page++) {
94:      const result = await candidateSource.listDstCandidates({ window: { start: windowStart, end: windowEnd }, cursor });
95:      for (const light of result.items) {
96:        const policy = await store.get<ReminderPolicy>(policyKey(light.tenantId, light.policyId));
97:        if (!policy) continue;
98:        const item = await store.get<{ PK: string; SK: string; dueDate: string; version: number }>(itemKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(light), light.itemId));
99:        if (!item) continue;
100:        dstCandidates.push({
101:          tenantId: light.tenantId,
102:          itemId: light.itemId,
103:          itemVersion: item.version,
104:          itemDueDate: item.dueDate,
105:          policy,
106:        });
107:      }
108:      if (!result.cursor) break;
109:      cursor = result.cursor;
110:    }
111:  }
112:
113:  const result = await runReconciliation({ store, tableName, now, shardConfig }, { expiredClaimCandidates, dstCandidates });
114:  logger.info("reminder-reconciliation complete", { mode, scheduledTime: event.scheduledTime, ...result });
115:}
FILE src/modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.ts
1:/**
2: * Versioned shard configuration - implementation-blueprint.md Â§9.2 "Reshard versionado":
3: * doubling N cannot make occurrences already materialized under the OLD generation
4: * invisible. `shardFnVersion` is the generation id; the producer/reconciliation query
5: * every ACTIVE generation's partitions during a reshard transition window (Â§9.6's runbook
6: * acceptance criterion), not just the newest one.
7: *
8: * A "generation" here is simply (shardFnVersion, shardCount) - the shard() function itself
9: * (stableHash mod N) never changes, only N does, so a generation is fully described by its
10: * shard count. `activeUntil` is set on a generation being retired so the producer knows
11: * when it can stop double-querying it (the runbook's "atÃ© a janela de materializaÃ§Ã£o
12: * antiga expirar, ~7 dias Ã  frente no pior caso").
13: */
14:export interface ShardGeneration {
15:  shardFnVersion: number;
16:  shardCount: number;
17:  /** ISO instant after which this generation no longer needs to be queried (undefined = still active/current). Set when a reshard retires this generation. */
18:  retireAfter?: string;
19:}
20:
21:export interface ShardConfig {
22:  /** The generation newly materialized occurrences are written under. */
23:  current: ShardGeneration;
24:  /** Older generations still holding not-yet-triggered occurrences, queried in parallel by the producer until `retireAfter`. */
25:  legacy: ShardGeneration[];
26:}
27:
28:export const DEFAULT_SHARD_COUNT = 4;
29:
30:export function defaultShardConfig(): ShardConfig {
31:  return { current: { shardFnVersion: 1, shardCount: DEFAULT_SHARD_COUNT }, legacy: [] };
32:}
33:
34:/** Generations the producer/reconciliation must query "now" (implementation-blueprint.md
35: * Â§9.2/Â§9.6: reads both old and new shardFnVersion generations during a resharding
36: * transition window). Excludes legacy generations whose retireAfter has passed. */
37:export function activeGenerations(config: ShardConfig, now: string): ShardGeneration[] {
38:  const stillActiveLegacy = config.legacy.filter((g) => !g.retireAfter || g.retireAfter > now);
39:  return [config.current, ...stillActiveLegacy];
40:}
41:
42:/**
43: * Doubles shard count, starting a reshard: the current generation becomes legacy (retired
44: * after `retireAfter`, default input by caller - the runbook's ~7 day worst-case
45: * materialization-ahead window), and a new current generation is created with double the
46: * shard count and the next `shardFnVersion`.
47: */
48:export function reshardDouble(config: ShardConfig, retireAfter: string): ShardConfig {
49:  return {
50:    current: { shardFnVersion: config.current.shardFnVersion + 1, shardCount: config.current.shardCount * 2 },
51:    legacy: [...config.legacy, { ...config.current, retireAfter }],
52:  };
53:}

43:  # invariants that must hold regardless of how many Lambdas exist today (distinctness, non-empty,
44:  # and the published-versions map covering EXACTLY the same set as the names list - not a subset,
45:  # not a superset).
46:  assert {
47:    condition     = length(output.lambda_function_names) > 0
48:    error_message = "At least one Lambda function must be declared"
49:  }
50:
51:  assert {
52:    condition     = length(distinct(output.lambda_function_names)) == length(output.lambda_function_names)
53:    error_message = "All Lambda function names must be distinct"
54:  }
55:
56:  assert {
57:    condition     = toset(keys(output.lambda_published_versions)) == toset(output.lambda_function_names)
58:    error_message = "lambda_published_versions must cover EXACTLY the same set of function names as lambda_function_names (deploy manifest / rollback mechanism, E-020/D-232) - neither a subset nor a superset"
59:  }

340:run "guest_credential_delivery_d233_lease_fix" {
341:  command = plan
342:
343:  # D-233 (SEC-R2-02 fix): unlimited retry/age on the mapping - a bounded count/age could
344:  # exhaust before the marker's 30s claim lease ever expires, stranding SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE
345:  # retries with no path to the SEND_UNCERTAIN reconciliation deliver.ts relies on.
346:  assert {
347:    condition     = aws_lambda_event_source_mapping.guest_credential_delivery_from_stream.maximum_retry_attempts == -1
348:    error_message = "guest-credential-delivery mapping must retry without a count limit (bounded by the marker lease reconciliation and IteratorAge alarm instead)"
349:  }
350:
351:  assert {
352:    condition     = aws_lambda_event_source_mapping.guest_credential_delivery_from_stream.maximum_record_age_in_seconds == -1
353:    error_message = "guest-credential-delivery mapping must retry without an age limit (bounded by DynamoDB Streams' own 24h native retention instead)"
354:  }
355:
356:  # D-233: the marker's own Put/Update/Delete writes must never re-trigger the handler - only a
357:  # real GuestCredentialDelivery INSERT should. filter_criteria/filter are sets (no addressable
358:  # index), so flatten via a for-expression to reach the single pattern string.
359:  assert {
360:    condition = anytrue([
361:      for f in aws_lambda_event_source_mapping.guest_credential_delivery_from_stream.filter_criteria :
362:      anytrue([for c in f.filter : try(jsondecode(c.pattern).dynamodb.NewImage.entityType.S[0], "") == "GuestCredentialDelivery"])
363:    ])
364:    error_message = "guest-credential-delivery mapping's filter must scope to entityType=GuestCredentialDelivery, excluding the marker's own stream events"
365:  }
366:
367:  # D-233: real-time backstop - IteratorAge, not the Lambda Errors metric (ReportBatchItemFailures
368:  # means a retried invocation still returns successfully, so Errors never fires for this class
369:  # of stuck record).
370:  assert {
371:    condition     = aws_cloudwatch_metric_alarm.guest_credential_delivery_iterator_age.metric_name == "IteratorAge"
372:    error_message = "guest-credential-delivery must have an IteratorAge alarm - ReportBatchItemFailures never surfaces a stuck record as a Lambda Errors metric"
373:  }
374:
375:  assert {
376:    condition     = aws_cloudwatch_metric_alarm.guest_credential_delivery_iterator_age.threshold == 300000
377:    error_message = "guest-credential-delivery IteratorAge alarm threshold must stay far below the stream's 24h native retention"
378:  }
379:
380:  # alarm_actions itself is unknown at plan time here (it embeds module.alert_topic.topic_arn, a
381:  # computed attribute for a topic not yet created in this workspace) - same posture this suite's
382:  # header establishes (plan-only, never apply): asserted on the comparison_operator/threshold
383:  # config instead, which are plan-time-known, never on the computed action list.
384:  assert {
385:    condition     = aws_cloudwatch_metric_alarm.guest_credential_delivery_iterator_age.comparison_operator == "GreaterThanThreshold"
386:    error_message = "guest-credential-delivery IteratorAge alarm must fire on GreaterThanThreshold, matching its stated 5-minute threshold"
387:  }
388:}

467:run "schedule_inputs_have_no_detail_wrapper_and_have_scheduled_time" {
468:  command = plan
469:
470:  # M3.5 contract: EventBridge Scheduler's Input is a top-level JSON payload with
471:  # scheduledTime, never a `detail` envelope (legacy Rule shape) - re-verified at root
472:  # wiring level, not just inside the reminder-schedule module's own unit test.
473:  assert {
474:    condition     = !strcontains(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.reminder_producer, "detail")
475:    error_message = "ReminderProducer schedule input must not have a detail envelope"
476:  }
477:  assert {
478:    condition     = strcontains(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.reminder_producer, "scheduledTime")
479:    error_message = "ReminderProducer schedule input must have scheduledTime"
480:  }
481:
482:  assert {
483:    condition     = !strcontains(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.reminder_claim_reconciliation, "detail")
484:    error_message = "CLAIMS reconciliation schedule input must not have a detail envelope"
485:  }
486:  assert {
487:    condition     = jsondecode(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.reminder_claim_reconciliation)["mode"] == "CLAIMS"
488:    error_message = "CLAIMS reconciliation schedule must set mode=CLAIMS"
489:  }
490:
491:  assert {
492:    condition     = !strcontains(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.reminder_dst_reconciliation, "detail")
493:    error_message = "DST reconciliation schedule input must not have a detail envelope"
494:  }
495:  assert {
496:    condition     = jsondecode(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.reminder_dst_reconciliation)["mode"] == "DST"
497:    error_message = "DST reconciliation schedule must set mode=DST"
498:  }
499:
500:  assert {
501:    condition     = !strcontains(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.outbox_sweeper, "detail")
502:    error_message = "Outbox sweeper schedule input must not have a detail envelope"
503:  }
504:  assert {
505:    condition     = strcontains(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.outbox_sweeper, "scheduledTime")
506:    error_message = "Outbox sweeper schedule input must have scheduledTime"
507:  }
508:
509:  # Real bug found 2026-08-21 (live CloudWatch evidence, Camada 3): jsonencode() HTML-escapes
510:  # `<`/`>` to their \uXXXX form, which silently defeats EventBridge Scheduler's literal-text
511:  # substitution of `<aws.scheduler.scheduled-time>` - every real invocation received the
512:  # placeholder string unsubstituted and failed validation. Re-verified at root wiring level.
513:  assert {
514:    condition     = !strcontains(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.reminder_producer, "\\u003c")
515:    error_message = "ReminderProducer schedule input must use the literal, unescaped context-attribute placeholder, not an HTML-escaped one"
516:  }
517:  assert {
518:    condition     = strcontains(module.schedule.schedule_inputs.reminder_producer, "<aws.scheduler.scheduled-time>")
519:    error_message = "ReminderProducer schedule input must contain the literal context-attribute placeholder text"
520:  }
521:
522:  assert {
523:    condition     = module.schedule.schedule_count == 4
524:    error_message = "Expected exactly 4 EventBridge Scheduler schedules (producer, claims-reconciliation, dst-reconciliation, sweeper)"
525:  }
526:}

547:run "gsi8_worker_isolation" {
548:  command = plan
549:
550:  # D-179/D-180 pilot slice - membership_purge_handler must be able to Query GSI8, scoped via
551:  # dynamodb:LeadingKeys to exactly its own WORK#/DLQ# namespace pair, plus TransactWriteItems on
552:  # the base table only. No other function's role may reference GSI8 at all (round-1 finding of
553:  # the approved design, corrected: "por Ã­ndice nÃ£o Ã© por worker" - isolation is per-worker, not
554:  # a single blanket "read GSI8" grant every consumer shares).
555:  assert {
556:    condition     = anytrue([for p in module.membership_purge_handler.capability_policy_documents : strcontains(p, "/index/GSI8")])
557:    error_message = "MembershipPurgeWorker must have a policy referencing GSI8"
558:  }
559:
560:  assert {
561:    condition     = anytrue([for p in module.membership_purge_handler.capability_policy_documents : strcontains(p, "WORK#MEMBERSHIP_PURGE") && strcontains(p, "DLQ#MEMBERSHIP_PURGE")])
562:    error_message = "MembershipPurgeWorker's GSI8 policy must condition dynamodb:LeadingKeys on exactly its own WORK#/DLQ# namespace pair"
563:  }
564:
565:  assert {
566:    condition     = anytrue([for p in module.membership_purge_handler.capability_policy_documents : strcontains(p, "dynamodb:TransactWriteItems")])
567:    error_message = "MembershipPurgeWorker must have dynamodb:TransactWriteItems - the atomic claim/revalidation action the approved design added as a real gap in the general policy"
568:  }
569:
570:  # TransactWriteItems is granted on the base table only, never on any GSI resource - it always
571:  # targets base-table items (ConditionCheck/Update/Delete), never a GSI directly.
572:  assert {
573:    condition = !anytrue([
574:      for p in module.membership_purge_handler.capability_policy_documents :
575:      strcontains(p, "dynamodb:TransactWriteItems") && (strcontains(p, "/index/GSI3") || strcontains(p, "/index/GSI4") || strcontains(p, "/index/GSI6") || strcontains(p, "/index/GSI8"))
576:    ])
577:    error_message = "dynamodb:TransactWriteItems must never be granted on a GSI resource, only the base table"
578:  }

581:  # namespace pair, isolated from membership_purge's.

584:    error_message = "InvitationPurgeWorker must have a policy referencing GSI8"

590:  }

601:    ])

1222:run "tenant_purge_iam_surface_is_the_minimum_the_approved_design_named" {
1223:  command = plan
1224:
1225:  # D-121 Rodada 3 Fix 8 enumerated the minimum IAM surface per role explicitly so a future
1226:  # session would not have to re-derive it. These assertions hold that surface in place.
1227:  assert {
1228:    condition     = length(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_workflow_invoke_lambdas.statement) == 3
1229:    error_message = "Expected exactly 3 statements: lambda:InvokeFunction (scoped to the 2 task handler ARNs), CloudWatch Logs delivery, and X-Ray write - logging/tracing are enabled on this state machine and fail at APPLY time without them"
1230:  }
1231:
1232:  # Note: the invoke statement's `resources` (the two Lambda ALIAS ARNs) are computed attributes
1233:  # unknown until apply, so they cannot be asserted from a plan-only run - the same limitation the
1234:  # extraction_workflow assertions above work around. The module's own tftest covers the
1235:  # substitution end of this, and the statement count above covers the shape.
1236:
1237:  # states:StartExecution is scoped to this ONE state machine ARN - not states:*, not a wildcard
1238:  # resource. This policy is attached to both the memberships handler and the sweeper.
1239:  assert {
1240:    condition     = length(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_start_execution.statement[0].resources) == 1
1241:    error_message = "StartExecution must be scoped to exactly the tenant-purge state machine ARN"
1242:  }
1243:
1244:  assert {
1245:    condition     = contains(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_start_execution.statement[0].actions, "states:StartExecution")
1246:    error_message = "The trigger policy must grant states:StartExecution (and only that action)"
1247:  }
1248:
1249:  # D-127: states:StopExecution (CancelOrganizationClosureService) and states:DescribeExecution
1250:  # (the sweeper's HELD_FOR_RECOVERY reconciliation) are each scoped to EXECUTION arns of this one
1251:  # state machine only - never the bare state machine ARN, never states:*, never a cross-machine
1252:  # wildcard.
1253:  assert {
1254:    condition     = length(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_stop_execution.statement[0].resources) == 1
1255:    error_message = "StopExecution must be scoped to exactly one resource pattern (this state machine's executions)"
1256:  }
1257:  assert {
1258:    condition     = contains(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_stop_execution.statement[0].actions, "states:StopExecution") && length(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_stop_execution.statement[0].actions) == 1
1259:    error_message = "The cancellation policy must grant states:StopExecution and ONLY that action"
1260:  }
1261:  assert {
1262:    condition     = strcontains(tolist(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_stop_execution.statement[0].resources)[0], ":execution:")
1263:    error_message = "StopExecution must target an EXECUTION arn pattern, not the state machine arn itself"
1264:  }
1265:  assert {
1266:    condition     = length(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_describe_execution.statement[0].resources) == 1
1267:    error_message = "DescribeExecution must be scoped to exactly one resource pattern (this state machine's executions)"
1268:  }
1269:  assert {
1270:    condition     = contains(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_describe_execution.statement[0].actions, "states:DescribeExecution") && length(data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_describe_execution.statement[0].actions) == 1
1271:    error_message = "The sweeper's reconciliation policy must grant states:DescribeExecution and ONLY that action - read-only, never Start/StopExecution added here"
1272:  }
1273:
1274:  # A purge deletes; it never reads object content. s3:GetObject appearing here would be a real
1275:  # privilege expansion, same discipline as document_purge_object_access.
1276:  assert {
1277:    condition = alltrue([
1278:      for s in data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_worker_s3.statement : !contains(s.actions, "s3:GetObject")
1279:    ])
1280:    error_message = "The purge worker must never be granted s3:GetObject - it deletes objects, it never reads their content"
1281:  }

BRANCH
design/reminder-producer-implementation-plan
DECISION
# ReminderProducer structural fix — Decisão (D-299)

## Status

**APROVADO** via protocolo Claude↔Codex (`AGENTS.md` §4), 4 rodadas (mínimo do protocolo é 3;
convergência só ocorreu na 4ª). Notas cegas finais: **Claude 9,1/10, Codex 9,2/10** — ambos ≥9,0
sem arredondar.

## Decisão

Adotar a **Opção B revisada**: redesenhar o `reminder-producer` desacoplando **descoberta** (scan
GSI3) de **reivindicação** (claim), via duas filas SQS, na forma final abaixo:

- **Fila de scan**: uma unidade de trabalho por (shard, minuto) elegível. O scanner (a MESMA
  função/role `reminder-producer` de hoje — única detentora de `gsi3_read`) processa **uma página**
  de `queryGsi3` por invocação (nunca o shard inteiro), publica os candidatos dessa página na fila
  de claim, e só então publica uma mensagem de continuação (`LastEvaluatedKey`). Progresso é
  **retomável e idempotente** via uma máquina de estados de lease por página
  (`IN_PROGRESS`/`ownerToken`/`leaseUntil`/`COMPLETED`, condicional, mesma tabela principal) —
  não um marcador único, que uma rodada intermediária do protocolo provou converter a garantia
  at-least-once do SQS em perda silenciosa quando a execução morre no meio do trabalho.
- **Fila de claim**: candidatos discriminados pelo `GSI3SK` bruto (preserva a distinção
  `ReminderOccurrence`/`DocumentChasingOccurrence` do M10 cluster 4). Um claim consumer novo (ou
  seguindo o padrão já usado por `reminder-dispatch`) executa o mesmo `get`+`transactWrite`
  condicional que o código atual já faz, distinguindo corretamente `CancellationReasons` (só
  `ConditionalCheckFailed` conta como corrida perdida entre claims concorrentes).
- Ambas as filas usam o módulo Terraform já existente (`modules/sqs-worker-queue`, o mesmo de
  `dispatch_queue`) — DLQ, alarme e idade de fila (`ApproximateAgeOfOldestMessage`) nativos,
  `ReportBatchItemFailures` nos dois event-source-mappings, `maximumConcurrency` +
  `reserved_concurrent_executions` com a invariante explícita de que a segunda deve ser ≥ a soma
  dos máximos da primeira.

**Fronteira GSI3 preservada por construção**: nenhuma policy `gsi3_read` nova é concedida — só a
função scanner (a mesma `reminder-producer` de hoje) lê o índice; o claim consumer nunca precisa
dele. `test/integration/gsi3-isolation.test.ts`-equivalente continua válido sem alteração.

## Por que esta opção, e não a reconciliação dedicada (Opção A)

Uma passagem de reconciliação dedicada consultando GSI3 diretamente exigiria conceder `gsi3_read`
a um segundo worker — cruzando a fronteira IAM hoje deliberadamente restrita a um único função — e
ainda deixaria o mecanismo primário (o producer) sem correção real: o burst continuaria podendo
estourar o timeout da MESMA forma, só que agora com uma rede de segurança correndo atrás dele
permanentemente. A Opção B corrige a causa raiz (nenhuma invocação, nem de scan nem de claim,
precisa mais processar uma unidade de trabalho ilimitada dentro de um timeout fixo) em vez de
adicionar uma segunda camada de mitigação sobre um mecanismo que já provou não escalar
(exatamente o padrão de falha que a mitigação timeout/concorrência/lookback já tentou e o PERF-12
10k mediu não ter resolvido).

## Trajetória de convergência (notas cegas, sem arredondar)

| Rodada | Claude | Codex | Achado principal fechado nessa rodada |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | 8,3/10 | 5,2/10 | — (proposta inicial: só a etapa de claim tinha sido desacoplada, o scan GSI3 continuava uma única invocação monolítica) |
| R2 | 8,8/10 | 8,3/10 | Scan paginado/retomável (LastEvaluatedKey como continuação), discriminação de tipo GSI3, tratamento de falha parcial de `SendMessageBatch` |
| R3 | [fechamento] | 8,4/10 | Checkpoint/fence, recuperação de DLQ, observabilidade nas duas filas, `CancellationReasons`, backpressure — mas o fence proposto (marcador único sem estado) ainda tinha um buraco real de perda silenciosa |
| R4 | 9,1/10 | 9,2/10 | Fence substituído por máquina de estados com lease (`IN_PROGRESS`/`ownerToken`/`leaseUntil`/`COMPLETED`) — **CONVERGIDO** |

## Pesquisa externa (research-protocol.md, E-014)

**SIM** — 3 fontes oficiais AWS verificadas e citadas nas rodadas: AWS Prescriptive Guidance
(padrão de desacoplar scan/poll de processamento via SQS), AWS Lambda Developer Guide ("Using
Lambda with Amazon SQS" — at-least-once, partial batch response, idempotência) e AWS SQS API
Reference (`SendMessageBatch`, falha parcial mesmo com HTTP 200). Uma quarta fonte citada na
Round 1 (`dev.to`, blog não-oficial) foi corretamente contestada pelo Codex e removida da lista de
fontes que fundamentam a decisão na Round 2 — mantida só como contexto de leitura.

## Escopo desta decisão

**Só arquitetural**, por instrução explícita da tarefa. Não foi escrito código de implementação.
Ficam para uma sessão de implementação dedicada: nomes exatos das filas/handlers, dimensionamento
de lease/timeout Lambda/visibility timeout SQS/`maxReceiveCount` (a invariante entre eles foi
nomeada, não os valores), testes (incl. atualização de `test/integration/gsi3-isolation.test.ts`
para cobrir a nova role do claim consumer), e a decisão sobre se/como `reminder-reconciliation`
existente precisa de algum ajuste (a expectativa arquitetural é que não precise — ele continua
cobrindo `CLAIMED` expirado e DST exatamente como hoje, já que o claim consumer ainda escreve
`claimExpiresAt` do mesmo jeito que o producer atual).

## Resumo executivo (não-técnico)

O sistema de lembretes automáticos tem um mecanismo (`reminder-producer`) que, a cada minuto,
procura lembretes que precisam ser disparados e os enfileira para envio. Um teste de carga
recente mostrou que, sob picos grandes de volume (muitos lembretes vencendo no mesmo minuto),
quase metade dos lembretes podia ficar presa e nunca ser enviada — um problema real de
confiabilidade, não hipotético. Uma correção rápida já havia sido tentada (dar mais tempo e mais
paralelismo ao processo), mas o teste em volume 10x maior provou que essa correção só adiava o
problema, não o resolvia. Depois de um debate técnico estruturado de 4 rodadas entre duas IAs
revisoras independentes (mínimo de 3 rodadas exigido pelo processo do projeto, chegando à
aprovação só na 4ª), foi decidida uma correção estrutural: dividir o trabalho em passos pequenos e
reiniciáveis, cada um garantidamente concluído dentro do tempo disponível, em vez de depender de
processar um pico inteiro de uma vez só. Isso segue o mesmo padrão que a AWS recomenda oficialmente
para este tipo de problema, e reaproveita um mecanismo que este próprio projeto já usa e já provou
funcionar sob 10x o volume em outro componente do sistema. Nenhum código foi escrito ainda — esta
etapa definiu só o desenho; a implementação fica para a próxima etapa.
