44:export interface DispatchCommand {
45:  /**
46:   * Real fix for a pre-existing bug found during M5's observability review (registered in
47:   * NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md): `schemas/queues/reminder-dispatch.v1.json` extends
48:   * `command-envelope.v1.json` via `allOf`, which already required `messageVersion`/
49:   * `messageId`/`createdAt`/`correlationId` at the top level - but this command never
50:   * carried them, so `reminder-dispatch-handler.ts`'s own schema validation against the
51:   * real SQS body would always reject it as schema-invalid. This is a bug fix making the
52:   * implementation match its own already-approved v1 contract, not a new schema version.
53:   */
54:  messageVersion: 1;
55:  messageId: string;
56:  createdAt: string;
57:  correlationId: string;
58:  commandType: "reminder.dispatch.v1";
59:  tenantId: string;
60:  deduplicationKey: string;
61:  data: {
62:    itemId: string;
63:    occurrenceId: string;
64:    occurrenceVersion: number;
65:    scheduledAt: string;
66:    itemVersion: number;
67:    policyVersion: number;
68:  };
69:}
70:
71:export interface ProducerDeps {
72:  store: ReminderProducerStore;
73:  shardConfig: ShardConfig;
74:  tableName: string;
75:  now: () => string;
76:  /** Short claim TTL - default 2 minutes, comfortably longer than one producer tick (1 minute) but short enough that a crashed dispatch worker's claim is reclaimable quickly by reconciliation (Â§9.5). */
77:  claimTtlMs?: number;
78:  /**
79:   * Lookback window in minutes, inclusive of the current minute (implementation-blueprint.md
80:   * Â§9.3 example: [M-5min, M]). Default raised from 5 to 15 (PERF-12 1k load test,
81:   * docs/engineering/performance/results/PERF-12-async-pipeline-1k.md): under a realistic
82:   * burst (many occurrences due the same minute) the producer's 10s timeout could be
83:   * exceeded for several consecutive minutes, and any occurrence still SCHEDULED once its
84:   * minute aged out of a 5-minute lookback was never reconciled by any other pass (see
85:   * reconciliation.ts's own file header - CLAIMS reconciliation only reverts already-CLAIMED
86:   * occurrences, DST reconciliation only re-materializes missing ones; neither covers
87:   * "SCHEDULED, past lookback, never claimed"). 15 minutes gives roughly 2x margin over the
88:   * observed 7-minute burst-recovery window, at a bounded extra cost (shardCount Ã—
89:   * lookbackMinutes extra GSI3 partition queries per NORMAL tick, most of them empty) - a
90:   * dedicated reconciliation pass for this case was considered but rejected: GSI3 query
91:   * access is deliberately isolated to ONLY this worker (reminder-store.ts's own doc comment,
92:   * enforced by test/integration/gsi3-isolation.test.ts and infra/main.tf's "the ONLY
93:   * function granted gsi3_read" policy comment) - widening the SAME worker's own lookback,
94:   * combined with the timeout raise and the parallelism below (both fix the actual cause,
95:   * this is the safety net), stays inside that existing boundary instead of punching a new
96:   * hole in it.
97:   */
98:  lookbackMinutes?: number;
99:  /** Deterministic-in-tests ID generator for the durable outbox event written in the same
100:   * transaction as the claim (M3.5 "DecisÃ£o central: outbox durÃ¡vel" - see runProducerTick). */
101:  newEventId: () => string;
102:  correlationId: () => string;
103:}
104:
105:export interface ProducerTickResult {
106:  claimed: DispatchCommand[];
107:  /** M10 cluster 4: DocumentChasingOccurrence claims from the SAME GSI3 scan, kept in a
108:   * separate array (different command shape) - never merged into `claimed`. */
109:  chasingClaimed: ChasingDispatchCommand[];
110:  failed: { occurrenceId: string; tenantId: string; error: unknown }[];
111:  /** GSI3SK shape matched neither the reminder nor the chasing pattern - fail-closed, never
112:   * processed. Should always be 0 in practice; a nonzero count is alarm-worthy (D-039). */
113:  unknownEntityType: number;
114:  scanned: number;
115:  minutesScanned: string[];
116:  shardPartitionsScanned: number;
117:}
118:
119:function minuteFloor(d: Date): Date {
120:  return new Date(Math.floor(d.getTime() / 60_000) * 60_000);
121:}
122:
123:/**
124: * PERF-12 1k load test finding (docs/engineering/performance/results/PERF-12-async-pipeline-1k.md):
125: * this loop used to process one occurrence at a time - one `store.get` + one
126: * `store.transactWrite` per occurrence, fully sequential - inside a Lambda with (at the time)
127: * a 10s timeout. With ~250 occurrences concentrated in one shard/minute under a realistic
128: * burst, that budget was nowhere near enough, the function was killed mid-loop repeatedly, and
129: * occurrences that hadn't been claimed yet eventually aged out of the lookback window,
130: * permanently stuck in SCHEDULED. Bounded concurrency here mirrors the exact pattern already
131: * used for the same reason in reminder-dispatch-handler.ts (D-170) - each occurrence's own
132: * `transactWrite` stays a single, independent atomic conditional claim (never merged across
133: * occurrences), so parallelizing here does not touch the transactional or idempotency
134: * guarantees of any individual claim, only how many claims run concurrently. A plain numeric
135: * constant, not unbounded `Promise.all`, to keep the burst of concurrent DynamoDB round trips
136: * per invocation predictable.
137: */
138:const PRODUCER_CLAIM_CONCURRENCY = 8;
139:
140:/**
141: * Pure alarm decision, extracted so the Lambda handler's "when should this tick throw" logic
142: * is unit-testable without mocking the whole handler/composition root (achado real de revisÃ£o
143: * adversarial, D-039/D-046/D-048: an earlier version of this fail-closed path was silently
144: * absorbed by the handler - `unknownEntityType` was counted but nothing ever read it). Two
145: * independent reasons, checked in this order for a stable, deterministic message:
146: * unrecognized GSI3SK shape (fail-closed, never a retry-race) first, then genuine per-occurrence
147: * claim failures.
148: */
149:export function shouldAlarm(result: ProducerTickResult): { alarm: boolean; reason?: string } {
150:  if (result.unknownEntityType > 0) {
151:    return { alarm: true, reason: `reminder-producer: ${result.unknownEntityType} GSI3 row(s) matched neither the reminder nor the chasing entityType - fail-closed` };
152:  }
153:  if (result.failed.length > 0) {
154:    return { alarm: true, reason: `reminder-producer: ${result.failed.length} occurrence(s) failed to claim` };
155:  }
156:  return { alarm: false };
157:}
158:
159:/** Runs one producer tick for wall-clock minute `tickMinute` (already floored to the minute by the caller/Lambda trigger). */
160:export async function runProducerTick(deps: ProducerDeps, tickMinute: Date): Promise<ProducerTickResult> {
161:  const generations = activeGenerations(deps.shardConfig, deps.now());
162:  const lookback = deps.lookbackMinutes ?? 15;
163:  const claimTtlMs = deps.claimTtlMs ?? 2 * 60_000;
164:
165:  const minutesToScan: Date[] = [];
166:  for (let i = lookback; i >= 0; i--) {
167:    minutesToScan.push(minuteFloor(new Date(tickMinute.getTime() - i * 60_000)));
168:  }
169:
170:  const claimed: DispatchCommand[] = [];
171:  const chasingClaimed: ChasingDispatchCommand[] = [];
172:  const failed: { occurrenceId: string; tenantId: string; error: unknown }[] = [];
173:  let unknownEntityType = 0;
174:  let scanned = 0;
175:  let partitions = 0;
176:
177:  // Dedup guard: the same occurrence could show up in more than one scanned minute only if
178:  // materialization ever wrote it under a stale bucket, which shouldn't happen - but a
179:  // Set guards against double-claiming within a single tick regardless.
180:  const seen = new Set<string>();
181:
182:  for (const minute of minutesToScan) {
183:    for (const generation of generations) {
184:      const partitionKeys = gsi3PartitionsForMinute(minute, generation.shardCount);
185:      partitions += partitionKeys.length;
186:      for (const gsi3pk of partitionKeys) {
187:        const rows = await deps.store.queryGsi3<{ PK: string; SK: string; GSI3SK: string }>({ gsi3pk });
188:        // PERF-12: this used to be `for (const row of rows)`, one `get`+`transactWrite` pair
189:        // processed strictly sequentially - see PRODUCER_CLAIM_CONCURRENCY's doc comment for
190:        // why bounded concurrency replaces that here. `seen`/`claimed`/`failed`/
191:        // `chasingClaimed`/counters are shared mutable state across concurrent callbacks, but
192:        // every access happens either synchronously (no `await` in between, so the JS event
193:        // loop cannot interleave two callbacks' synchronous sections) or as an atomic
194:        // push/increment - never a race in practice under Node's single-threaded model.
195:        await mapWithConcurrency(rows, PRODUCER_CLAIM_CONCURRENCY, async (row) => {
196:          scanned += 1;
197:
198:          // M10 cluster 4: try the chasing shape FIRST (never throws, `undefined` on no
199:          // match) - the reminder branch below is completely unchanged otherwise.
200:          const chasingParsed = parseChasingGsi3Sk(row.GSI3SK);
201:          if (chasingParsed) {
202:            if (seen.has(chasingParsed.occurrenceId)) return;
203:            seen.add(chasingParsed.occurrenceId);
204:            try {
205:              const outcome = await claimChasingOccurrence(
206:                { store: deps.store, tableName: deps.tableName, now: deps.now, claimTtlMs, newEventId: deps.newEventId, correlationId: deps.correlationId },
207:                { PK: row.PK, SK: row.SK },
208:              );
209:              if (outcome.kind === "CLAIMED") chasingClaimed.push(outcome.command);
210:              // SKIPPED_NOT_SCHEDULED / LOST_CLAIM_RACE: not failures, same as the reminder path below.
211:            } catch (err) {
212:              failed.push({ occurrenceId: chasingParsed.occurrenceId, tenantId: chasingParsed.tenantId, error: err });
213:            }
214:            return;
215:          }
216:
217:          let reminderParsed: { tenantId: string; occurrenceId: string };
218:          try {
219:            reminderParsed = parseGsi3Sk(row.GSI3SK);
220:          } catch {
221:            // Fail-closed (D-039): neither pattern matched - never process a row we can't
222:            // identify, never skip it silently either. Alarm-worthy, surfaced via the tick result.
223:            unknownEntityType += 1;
224:            return;
225:          }
226:          const { tenantId, occurrenceId } = reminderParsed;
227:          if (seen.has(occurrenceId)) return;
228:          seen.add(occurrenceId);
229:
230:          try {
231:            const occurrence = await deps.store.get<ReminderOccurrence>({ PK: row.PK, SK: row.SK });
232:            if (!occurrence || occurrence.status !== "SCHEDULED") {
233:              // Already claimed/cancelled/triggered by a prior tick or race - not a failure.
234:              return;
235:            }
236:
237:            const claimExpiresAt = new Date(Date.parse(deps.now()) + claimTtlMs).toISOString();
238:            const newVersion = occurrence.version + 1;
239:            const now = deps.now();
240:            // Same value used for both the command's own envelope correlationId (read
241:            // directly from the SQS body per m5-observability-design.md #2's general SQS
242:            // rule) and the outbox DomainEvent's correlationId - one business correlation
243:            // per dispatch, not two independently-generated ids for the same operation.
244:            const correlationId = deps.correlationId();
245:
246:            const command: DispatchCommand = {
247:              messageVersion: 1,
248:              messageId: deps.newEventId(),
249:              createdAt: now,
250:              correlationId,
251:              commandType: "reminder.dispatch.v1",
252:              tenantId,
253:              deduplicationKey: `${tenantId}|${occurrenceId}|${newVersion}`,
254:              data: {
255:                itemId: occurrence.itemId,
256:                occurrenceId,
257:                occurrenceVersion: newVersion,
258:                scheduledAt: occurrence.scheduledAt,
259:                itemVersion: occurrence.itemVersion,
260:                policyVersion: occurrence.policyVersion,
261:              },
262:            };
263:
264:            // M3.5 "DecisÃ£o central: outbox durÃ¡vel": TransactWrite(claim) followed by a
265:            // direct SendMessage in the handler is NOT atomic (Lambda can die between the
266:            // two steps, leaving a CLAIMED occurrence with no queued command - neither the
267:            // producer's own lookback window nor the daily DST reconciliation reliably
268:            // covers that gap). The fix: write a durable OutboxEvent
269:            // (destination=SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1) in the SAME transaction as the claim;
270:            // DispatchOutboxRelay (DynamoDB Streams) publishes it to SQS, a sweeper recovers
271:            // publication failures. Also set the GSI6 WORKSTATE#CLAIMED pointer so the
272:            // reconciliation job can find this claim if it expires unrecovered - removed
273:            // when ReminderDispatch advances to TRIGGERED or reconciliation reverts it.
274:            const event: DomainEvent = {
275:              specVersion: "1.0",
276:              eventId: deps.newEventId(),
277:              eventType: "ReminderDispatchRequested",
278:              source: "expiration-tracker.reminder-producer",
279:              occurredAt: now,
280:              correlationId,
281:              tenantId,
282:              actor: { type: "SYSTEM" },
283:              aggregate: { type: "ReminderOccurrence", id: occurrenceId, version: newVersion },
284:              data: command as unknown as Record<string, unknown>,
285:            };
286:            const outboxEntries: DynamoTransactPutEntry[] = [];
287:            appendToTransaction(outboxEntries, deps.tableName, event, "SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1");
288:
289:            await deps.store.transactWrite([
290:              {
291:                Update: buildVersionedUpdate({
292:                  tableName: deps.tableName,
293:                  key: { PK: row.PK, SK: row.SK },
294:                  tenantId,
295:                  expectedVersion: occurrence.version,
296:                  set: {
297:                    status: "CLAIMED",
298:                    claimedAt: deps.now(),
299:                    claimExpiresAt,
300:                    GSI6PK: GSI6PK_WORKSTATE_CLAIMED,
301:                    GSI6SK: buildExpiredClaimGsi6Sk(claimExpiresAt, tenantId, occurrenceId),
302:                  },
303:                }),
304:              },
305:              ...outboxEntries,
306:            ]);
307:
308:            claimed.push(command);
309:          } catch (err) {
310:            if (isTransactionCanceled(err)) {
311:              // Lost the claim race to a concurrent producer tick - not a failure to retry.
312:              return;
313:            }
314:            failed.push({ occurrenceId, tenantId, error: err });
315:          }
316:        });
317:      }
318:    }
319:  }
320:
321:  return {
322:    claimed,
323:    chasingClaimed,
324:    failed,
325:    unknownEntityType,
326:    scanned,
327:    minutesScanned: minutesToScan.map((d) => d.toISOString()),
328:    shardPartitionsScanned: partitions,
329:  };
330:}
===== src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-producer-handler.ts =====
1:/** Real handler for ReminderProducer (EventBridge Scheduler, 1 min), replacing the 501
2: * placeholder. EventBridge Scheduler's Lambda target does NOT wrap the payload in a
3: * `detail` envelope the way legacy EventBridge Rules do - the event IS whatever `input`
4: * the schedule sets (infra/lib/reminder-schedule.ts), never `event.time` (a Rule-shaped
5: * field that doesn't exist for Scheduler invocations - bug found by Codex implementation
6: * review, event.time was always undefined -> Invalid Date). */
7:import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
8:import { createDocumentClient } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/client.js";
9:import { buildReminderProducerDeps } from "../composition/reminder.js";
10:import { runProducerTick, shouldAlarm } from "../../../workers/reminder-producer/producer.js";
11:import { defaultShardConfig } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.js";
12:import { runWithContext } from "../../../shared/observability/context.js";
13:import { SecureLogger } from "../../../shared/observability/logger.js";
14:import { timeSpan, withHandlerTiming } from "../../../shared/observability/handler-timing.js";
15:import { ValidationError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
16:
17:const client = createDocumentClient();
18:const tableName = process.env["TABLE_NAME"];
19:if (!tableName) throw new Error("TABLE_NAME env var is required.");
20:const deps = buildReminderProducerDeps(client, tableName);
21:const logger = new SecureLogger({ baseContext: { service: "reminder-producer" } });
22:const NAMESPACE = "ExpirationTracker/ReminderProducer";
23:
24:export interface ReminderProducerEvent {
25:  scheduledTime: string;
26:}
27:
28:export const handler = withHandlerTiming<ReminderProducerEvent, void>(NAMESPACE, "reminder-producer handler", async (event) => {
29:  if (!event.scheduledTime) {
30:    throw new ValidationError("reminder-producer: missing scheduledTime in event payload.");
31:  }
32:  const tickMinute = new Date(event.scheduledTime);
33:  if (Number.isNaN(tickMinute.getTime())) {
34:    throw new ValidationError("reminder-producer: scheduledTime is not a valid date.", { scheduledTime: event.scheduledTime });
35:  }
36:  // m5-observability-design.md #2: EventBridge Scheduler producer, no upstream request to
37:  // inherit a correlationId from - new UUID per invocation.
38:  await runWithContext({ correlationId: randomUUID() }, async () => {
39:    const result = await timeSpan(NAMESPACE, "lambda.business_operation_ms", "reminder-producer business operation timing", () =>
40:      runProducerTick({ ...deps, shardConfig: defaultShardConfig() }, tickMinute),
41:    );
42:    logger.info("reminder-producer tick complete", {
43:      scanned: result.scanned,
44:      claimed: result.claimed.length,
45:      chasingClaimed: result.chasingClaimed.length,
46:      failed: result.failed.length,
47:      unknownEntityType: result.unknownEntityType,
48:      minutesScanned: result.minutesScanned,
49:    });
50:    // Achado real de revisÃ£o adversarial (Codex, D-039/D-046/D-048): o comentÃ¡rio original de
51:    // producer.ts prometia que `unknownEntityType` seria "surfaced via failed so a real alarm
52:    // can fire", mas nada de fato lanÃ§ava por causa dele - uma linha de GSI3 com forma
53:    // desconhecida ficava silenciosa em produÃ§Ã£o. Corrigido: decisÃ£o de alarme extraÃ­da para
54:    // `shouldAlarm()` (funÃ§Ã£o pura, testada diretamente), separada de `failed` (uma linha
55:    // desconhecida nunca teria occurrenceId/tenantId reais para colocar lÃ¡).
56:    const alarm = shouldAlarm(result);
57:    if (alarm.alarm) {
58:      throw new Error(alarm.reason);
59:    }
60:  });
61:});
===== src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-reconciliation-handler.ts =====
1:/** Real handler for ReminderReconciliation (EventBridge Scheduler, `mode` field), replacing
2: * the 501 placeholder. Two schedules invoke the same Lambda with different `mode`
3: * (m3.5-runtime-design.md Â§"ReconciliaÃ§Ã£o"): CLAIMS (every 5 min) and DST (daily). Also one
4: * of exactly two roles granted gsi6Read() - see infra/lib/dynamo-table.ts.
5: *
6: * EventBridge Scheduler's Lambda target does NOT wrap the payload in a `detail` envelope
7: * the way legacy EventBridge Rules do - the event IS whatever `input` the schedule sets
8: * (infra/lib/reminder-schedule.ts: `{mode, scheduledTime}` at the top level), never
9: * `event.detail.mode` (bug found by Codex implementation review - event.detail was always
10: * undefined, both schedules would throw on every invocation). */
11:import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
12:import { createDocumentClient } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/client.js";
13:import { buildReconciliationDeps } from "../composition/reminder.js";
14:import { runReconciliation, type DstReconciliationCandidate as FullDstCandidate } from "../../../workers/reminder-reconciliation/reconciliation.js";
15:import { itemKey } from "../../../modules/expiration/domain/expiration-item.js";
16:import { authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity } from "../../../modules/identity/domain/authorization.js";
17:import { policyKey, type ReminderPolicy } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/reminder-policy.js";
18:import { defaultShardConfig } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.js";
19:import type { ReminderOccurrence } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/reminder-occurrence.js";
20:import { defaultSchemaRegistry } from "../../../shared/contracts/schema-validator.js";
21:import { runWithContext } from "../../../shared/observability/context.js";
22:import { SecureLogger } from "../../../shared/observability/logger.js";
23:
24:const client = createDocumentClient();
25:const envTableName = process.env["TABLE_NAME"];
26:if (!envTableName) throw new Error("TABLE_NAME env var is required.");
27:const tableName: string = envTableName;
28:const { store, candidateSource, now } = buildReconciliationDeps(client, tableName);
29:const shardConfig = defaultShardConfig();
30:const logger = new SecureLogger({ baseContext: { service: "reminder-reconciliation" } });
31:
32:export interface ReminderReconciliationEvent {
33:  mode: "CLAIMS" | "DST";
34:  scheduledTime: string;
35:}
36:
37:/** Hard cap on pages drained per invocation - matches the design's "pÃ¡gina lÃ³gica mÃ¡x. 200"
38: * per page with a bounded number of pages, so a pathological backlog can't make a single
39: * invocation run indefinitely; anything beyond this is picked up by the NEXT scheduled
40: * run (every 5 min for claims, daily for DST) rather than blocking this one. */
41:const MAX_PAGES = 25;
42:
43:const RECONCILIATION_EVENT_SCHEMA_ID = "https://expiration-tracker/schemas/events/reminder-reconciliation-event.v1.json";
44:
45:export async function handler(event: unknown): Promise<void> {
46:  // m5-observability-design.md #2: EventBridge Scheduler producer, no upstream request to
47:  // inherit a correlationId from - new UUID per invocation.
48:  await runWithContext({ correlationId: randomUUID() }, async () => {
49:    // P2.1 (external audit 2026-09-11): `event` was previously typed (not runtime-validated)
50:    // as `ReminderReconciliationEvent` straight off the Lambda invocation - a TS annotation
51:    // enforces nothing at runtime. Same schema-registry pattern reminder-dispatch-handler.ts's
52:    // `DISPATCH_COMMAND_SCHEMA_ID` already uses for its own boundary, mirrored here.
53:    const { valid, errors } = defaultSchemaRegistry.validate(RECONCILIATION_EVENT_SCHEMA_ID, event);
54:    if (!valid) {
55:      logger.error("reminder-reconciliation schema-invalid event", { errors });
56:      throw new Error("schema-invalid ReminderReconciliationEvent");
57:    }
58:    await handleReconciliation(event as ReminderReconciliationEvent);
59:  });
60:}
61:
62:async function handleReconciliation(event: ReminderReconciliationEvent): Promise<void> {
63:  const mode = event.mode;
64:
65:  const expiredClaimCandidates: ReminderOccurrence[] = [];
66:  const dstCandidates: FullDstCandidate[] = [];
67:
68:  if (mode === "CLAIMS") {
69:    // GSI6 is ALL-projected - each candidate row already IS the full ReminderOccurrence
70:    // item (see ExpiredClaimCandidate's doc comment), so no extra fetch is needed.
71:    // Pagination is drained (bug found by Codex implementation review: the first version
72:    // only ever read page 1, silently leaving any backlog over one page unprocessed).
73:    let cursor: string | undefined;
74:    for (let page = 0; page < MAX_PAGES; page++) {
75:      const result = await candidateSource.listExpiredClaims({ before: now(), cursor });
76:      expiredClaimCandidates.push(...(result.items as unknown as ReminderOccurrence[]));
77:      if (!result.cursor) break;
78:      cursor = result.cursor;
79:    }
80:  }
81:
82:  if (mode === "DST") {
83:    // ReminderMaterializer (M3.5) writes a WORKSTATE#DST_PENDING GSI6 pointer whenever a
84:    // trigger's schedule was computed at an ambiguous/nonexistent local time, OR whose
85:    // timeZone observes DST at all (see reminder-materializer.ts - broadened per Codex
86:    // implementation review: a non-ambiguous 09:00 reminder in a DST-observing zone can
87:    // still need re-evaluation after an offset change, not just literally-ambiguous ones).
88:    // This query finds those candidates; occurrences in a fixed-offset timeZone never
89:    // appear here at all.
90:    const windowStart = now();
91:    const windowEnd = new Date(Date.parse(windowStart) + 7 * 24 * 60 * 60_000).toISOString();
92:    let cursor: string | undefined;
93:    for (let page = 0; page < MAX_PAGES; page++) {
94:      const result = await candidateSource.listDstCandidates({ window: { start: windowStart, end: windowEnd }, cursor });
95:      for (const light of result.items) {
96:        const policy = await store.get<ReminderPolicy>(policyKey(light.tenantId, light.policyId));
97:        if (!policy) continue;
98:        const item = await store.get<{ PK: string; SK: string; dueDate: string; version: number }>(itemKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(light), light.itemId));
99:        if (!item) continue;
100:        dstCandidates.push({
101:          tenantId: light.tenantId,
102:          itemId: light.itemId,
103:          itemVersion: item.version,
104:          itemDueDate: item.dueDate,
105:          policy,
106:        });
107:      }
108:      if (!result.cursor) break;
109:      cursor = result.cursor;
110:    }
111:  }
112:
113:  const result = await runReconciliation({ store, tableName, now, shardConfig }, { expiredClaimCandidates, dstCandidates });
114:  logger.info("reminder-reconciliation complete", { mode, scheduledTime: event.scheduledTime, ...result });
115:}
===== src/modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.ts =====
1:/**
2: * Versioned shard configuration - implementation-blueprint.md Â§9.2 "Reshard versionado":
3: * doubling N cannot make occurrences already materialized under the OLD generation
4: * invisible. `shardFnVersion` is the generation id; the producer/reconciliation query
5: * every ACTIVE generation's partitions during a reshard transition window (Â§9.6's runbook
6: * acceptance criterion), not just the newest one.
7: *
8: * A "generation" here is simply (shardFnVersion, shardCount) - the shard() function itself
9: * (stableHash mod N) never changes, only N does, so a generation is fully described by its
10: * shard count. `activeUntil` is set on a generation being retired so the producer knows
11: * when it can stop double-querying it (the runbook's "atÃ© a janela de materializaÃ§Ã£o
12: * antiga expirar, ~7 dias Ã  frente no pior caso").
13: */
14:export interface ShardGeneration {
15:  shardFnVersion: number;
16:  shardCount: number;
17:  /** ISO instant after which this generation no longer needs to be queried (undefined = still active/current). Set when a reshard retires this generation. */
18:  retireAfter?: string;
19:}
20:
21:export interface ShardConfig {
22:  /** The generation newly materialized occurrences are written under. */
23:  current: ShardGeneration;
24:  /** Older generations still holding not-yet-triggered occurrences, queried in parallel by the producer until `retireAfter`. */
25:  legacy: ShardGeneration[];
26:}
27:
28:export const DEFAULT_SHARD_COUNT = 4;
29:
30:export function defaultShardConfig(): ShardConfig {
31:  return { current: { shardFnVersion: 1, shardCount: DEFAULT_SHARD_COUNT }, legacy: [] };
32:}
33:
34:/** Generations the producer/reconciliation must query "now" (implementation-blueprint.md
35: * Â§9.2/Â§9.6: reads both old and new shardFnVersion generations during a resharding
36: * transition window). Excludes legacy generations whose retireAfter has passed. */
37:export function activeGenerations(config: ShardConfig, now: string): ShardGeneration[] {
38:  const stillActiveLegacy = config.legacy.filter((g) => !g.retireAfter || g.retireAfter > now);
39:  return [config.current, ...stillActiveLegacy];
40:}
41:
42:/**
43: * Doubles shard count, starting a reshard: the current generation becomes legacy (retired
44: * after `retireAfter`, default input by caller - the runbook's ~7 day worst-case
45: * materialization-ahead window), and a new current generation is created with double the
46: * shard count and the next `shardFnVersion`.
47: */
48:export function reshardDouble(config: ShardConfig, retireAfter: string): ShardConfig {
49:  return {
50:    current: { shardFnVersion: config.current.shardFnVersion + 1, shardCount: config.current.shardCount * 2 },
51:    legacy: [...config.legacy, { ...config.current, retireAfter }],
52:  };
53:}

228:## 5. Test plan
229:
230:- **Unit** (`test/unit/reminder/`, following existing `in-memory-store.ts` fixture pattern):
231:  page-lease acquire/renew/release pure logic â€” concurrent acquire attempts (both should not
232:  succeed), renew with stale `ownerToken` (must fail), renew with correct token but expired
233:  `leaseUntil` (must fail â€” a zombie holder cannot renew past its own expiry), release from wrong
234:  owner (must fail), reclaim after expiry (must succeed for a NEW ownerToken), redelivery-of-same-
235:  message-mid-lease (must be a safe no-op re-publish, not a double-claim â€” this is the exact D-299
236:  R3 finding, gets its own dedicated test).
237:- **Integration**: extend the EXISTING GSI3-isolation coverage â€” this codebase's actual mechanism
238:  is `infra/tests/stack.tftest.hcl` (Terraform test asserting IAM policy scoping to GSI3, not a
239:  `.test.ts` file of that exact name â€” corrected from the task prompt's assumption after checking;
240:  no `test/integration/gsi3-isolation.test.ts` exists in this repo today). Add a new
241:  `terraform test` assertion (same file, new `run` block) that the NEW `reminder_claim_consumer`
242:  Lambda's IAM role has NO `gsi3_read` statement, mirroring the existing assertion pattern for
243:  `reminder_dispatch`.
244:- **Integration** (application-level, Vitest): a scanâ†’claim round trip using the in-memory SQS +
245:  DynamoDB test doubles this codebase already has for `reminder-dispatch` (composition root
246:  fixtures) â€” burst volume at PERF-12's 10k scale, asserting ZERO loss (the actual regression this
247:  whole design fixes) and that pausing/resuming a shard mid-scan (simulated Lambda death) via a
248:  fresh invocation with the same continuation message reaches `COMPLETED` with all candidates
249:  published exactly once each.
250:
251:## 6. `reminder-reconciliation.ts` impact
252:
253:**Confirmed: no change needed**, with actual reasoning (not just repeating D-299's stated
254:expectation): `reminder-reconciliation.ts`'s job (per its own file, to be re-read in round 2 for a
255:citation-level confirmation) is to catch occurrences stuck in `CLAIMED` past `claimExpiresAt` â€”
256:that contract is entirely downstream of the claim consumer's `transactWrite`, which writes
257:`claimExpiresAt` identically to today's code (D-299's own premise, unchanged by this plan since the
258:claim logic itself is only relocated, not rewritten). The scan-side lease this plan adds is a
259:DIFFERENT state machine on a DIFFERENT item (`SCAN#...#LEASE`, never `CLAIMED` occurrence rows) that
260:reconciliation never reads today and has no reason to start reading â€” a stuck scan lease
261:self-heals via the next EventBridge tick's reclaim path (Â§2), not via reconciliation. One residual
262:open question for round 2: does reconciliation's own scan of `CLAIMED` rows need its OWN
263:correlation ID propagation update for consistency, even though its logic doesn't change? (Leaning
264:no â€” it's not part of this mechanism's message chain â€” but flagging rather than asserting.)
265:
266:## 7. Migration/rollout
267:
268:Big-bang cutover, not staged, for a concrete reason: the scan and claim halves are not
269:independently useful (a scan queue with no claim consumer just accumulates candidates; a claim
270:consumer with no new-shape scan producer never receives anything) â€” there's no safe intermediate
271:state to stage through, unlike, say, a dual-write migration. Sequencing:
272:1. Deploy claim consumer Lambda + claim queue (inert â€” nothing publishes to it yet).
273:2. Deploy scan queue (inert â€” nothing subscribes to the OLD producer's shard-loop yet, since it
274:   doesn't publish to it).
275:3. Deploy the MODIFIED `reminder-producer-handler.ts` (now page-based) in the SAME release as step
276:   1/2's infra â€” this is the actual cutover moment; the old monolithic-per-shard loop code path is
277:   deleted in this same change, not kept behind a flag (D-299 didn't ask for a flag, and a flag
278:   here would mean maintaining two claim code paths against the same table, doubling the
279:   concurrent-claim race surface this whole fix targets).
280:4. **Occurrences already `SCHEDULED` at cutover**: confirmed safe, with reasoning â€” `SCHEDULED`
281:   occurrences are discovered by `queryGsi3`, not by any in-flight queue state; the OLD mechanism's
282:   only in-flight artifact at cutover is whatever's already sitting in the OLD single-invocation
283:   claim loop of a tick that started before deploy (which either finishes normally before the
284:   deploy completes, since Lambda in-flight invocations aren't killed by a code update, or times
285:   out and is picked up fresh by the NEW scan mechanism's next tick â€” no occurrence-level state is
286:   lost either way, since `SCHEDULED` occurrences are read fresh from GSI3 every tick regardless of
287:   which mechanism is running).
288:5. Rollback path: revert step 3's Lambda code only (keep the new queues/consumer deployed but
289:   inert) â€” since GSI3 access never moved, a revert to the old handler code resumes the old
290:   monolithic scan immediately with no data migration needed in either direction.
291:
292:## Self-grade (Claude, blind â€” written before seeing Codex's round 1 critique)
293:
294:**7.8/10.** Strong on names/schema/timeout concreteness and correlation ID mechanism (directly
295:reused existing primitives, verified against real file contents rather than assumed). Weakest
296:points I can already see: (a) the outbox-boundary reasoning in Â§4 is my own inference, not
297:verified against what the CURRENT claim code actually does with outbox â€” flagged as a round-2
298:action item rather than resolved; (b) the corrected test-file reference (no
299:`gsi3-isolation.test.ts` exists) means I'm deviating from the task prompt's literal ask without
300:having fully traced whether some OTHER file is the intended target; (c) `ReminderLeaseConflictError`
301:error-taxonomy question left open rather than decided; (d) haven't verified `defaultShardConfig()`
302:shard count independently beyond one grep-confirmed read. Expect Codex to find at least one of
303:these, plus likely a sharper take on the claim-queue concurrency arithmetic in Â§3 (my 100-peak/16-
304:worker math is a plausibility argument, not a rigorous one).

=== src/modules/reminder/persistence/dynamodb-reminder-producer-store.ts ===
1:/**
2: * Real DynamoDB adapter for ReminderProducerStore (M3.5). Deliberately a SEPARATE class
3: * from DynamoDbReminderStore (never a shared "does everything" adapter) - the only place
4: * `queryGsi3` exists, matching the port-level isolation documented in
5: * src/modules/reminder/ports/reminder-store.ts. Only wired into the ReminderProducer
6: * Lambda's composition root.
7: */
8:import { GetCommand, QueryCommand, TransactWriteCommand } from "@aws-sdk/lib-dynamodb";
9:import type { DynamoDBDocumentClient } from "@aws-sdk/lib-dynamodb";
10:import type { EntityKey, Gsi3QueryInput, ReminderProducerStore, TransactWriteEntry } from "../ports/reminder-store.js";
11:import { mapDynamoError } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/sdk-errors.js";
12:import { auditGlobalIndexAccess, auditGlobalIndexAccessDenied, isAccessDeniedError } from "../../../shared/observability/security-audit.js";
13:
14:export class DynamoDbReminderProducerStore implements ReminderProducerStore {
15:  constructor(
16:    private readonly client: DynamoDBDocumentClient,
17:    private readonly tableName: string,
18:  ) {}
19:
20:  async queryGsi3<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(input: Gsi3QueryInput): Promise<T[]> {
21:    let pageCount = 0;
22:    try {
23:      const items: T[] = [];
24:      let exclusiveStartKey: Record<string, unknown> | undefined;
25:      do {
26:        const result = await this.client.send(
27:          new QueryCommand({
28:            TableName: this.tableName,
29:            IndexName: "GSI3",
30:            KeyConditionExpression: "GSI3PK = :pk",
31:            ExpressionAttributeValues: { ":pk": input.gsi3pk },
32:            ExclusiveStartKey: exclusiveStartKey,
33:          }),
34:        );
35:        pageCount += 1;
36:        items.push(...((result.Items ?? []) as T[]));
37:        exclusiveStartKey = result.LastEvaluatedKey;
38:      } while (exclusiveStartKey);
39:      // Security audit trail (full-audit-round1-focused-round2-summary.md, achado real): 1
40:      // evento por chamada lÃ³gica, nÃ£o por pÃ¡gina - ver
41:      // docs/architecture/reviews/security-audit-trail-design/.
42:      auditGlobalIndexAccess({ indexName: "GSI3", operation: "Query", component: "reminder-producer", pageCount, resultCount: items.length });
43:      return items;
44:    } catch (err) {
45:      if (isAccessDeniedError(err)) {
46:        auditGlobalIndexAccessDenied({ indexName: "GSI3", operation: "Query", component: "reminder-producer", awsErrorCode: "AccessDeniedException" });
47:      }
48:      throw mapDynamoError(err, "ReminderProducerStore.queryGsi3");
49:    }
50:  }
51:
52:  async get<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(key: EntityKey): Promise<T | undefined> {
53:    try {
54:      const result = await this.client.send(
55:        new GetCommand({ TableName: this.tableName, Key: key, ConsistentRead: true }),
56:      );
57:      return result.Item as T | undefined;
58:    } catch (err) {
59:      throw mapDynamoError(err, "ReminderProducerStore.get");
60:    }
61:  }
62:
63:  // No try/catch: TransactionCanceledException must reach the caller as-is - see DynamoDbReminderStore.transactWrite.
64:  async transactWrite(entries: TransactWriteEntry[]): Promise<void> {
65:    await this.client.send(
66:      new TransactWriteCommand({
67:        TransactItems: entries as unknown as ConstructorParameters<typeof TransactWriteCommand>[0]["TransactItems"],
68:      }),
69:    );
70:  }
71:}

=== src/shared/dynamodb/occ.ts ===
1:/**
2: * Optimistic concurrency control helper - implementation-blueprint.md #5.2 / M0 deliverable.
3: *
4: * M0 ships no tables/Lambdas yet (that's M1+), so this module only builds the DynamoDB
5: * command *parameters* for OCC-safe writes; it does not depend on @aws-sdk/client-dynamodb
6: * so it stays testable without AWS credentials/mocks. M1 wires these builders into the
7: * actual DocumentClient calls once tables exist.
8: */
9:
10:export interface EntityKey {
11:  PK: string;
12:  SK: string;
13:}
14:
15:export interface VersionedUpdateInput {
16:  tableName: string;
17:  key: EntityKey;
18:  tenantId: string;
19:  expectedVersion: number;
20:  /** Additional SET clauses beyond version/updatedAt, e.g. { status: "CANCELLED" }. */
21:  set: Record<string, unknown>;
22:  /** Attribute names to REMOVE atomically in the same conditional update - e.g. dropping
23:   * GSI6PK/GSI6SK when a reconciliation-candidate pointer (M3.5) stops applying. Gap noted
24:   * since M3 (occ.ts was SET-only, cancelled GSI3 pointers were left orphaned because
25:   * nothing actively queried them); M3.5 needs REMOVE for real because GSI6 pointers ARE
26:   * actively queried by reconciliation. */
27:  remove?: string[];
28:  now?: string;
29:  /** Extra ConditionExpression clauses ANDed to the base condition, each with its own
30:   * caller-supplied names/values (W3-06 purge worker's claim/fence conditions - e.g. checking
31:   * `legalHold`/`GSI6PK`/`purgeAfter` atomically alongside the version check). Each entry's
32:   * `expression` is wrapped in its own parentheses before being ANDed in, so callers never need
33:   * to hand-balance parens against the base condition. Placeholder names/values MUST NOT collide
34:   * with the base condition's (#version/#tenantId/#updatedAt/:expectedVersion/:tenantId/:one/:now)
35:   * or with any `set`/`remove` generated key (#set<i>/:set<i>/#rem<j>) - buildVersionedUpdate
36:   * throws before building an invalid ConditionExpression rather than silently overwriting one
37:   * caller's placeholder with another's. */
38:  extraConditions?: Array<{ expression: string; names?: Record<string, string>; values?: Record<string, unknown> }>;
39:}
40:
41:export interface DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
42:  TableName: string;
43:  Key: EntityKey;
44:  UpdateExpression: string;
45:  ConditionExpression: string;
46:  /** Optional, not `Record<string, string>` with an empty-object default (Wave B2B-14, D-119
47:   * real finding): DynamoDB's TransactWriteItems rejects an explicitly-empty
48:   * ExpressionAttributeNames outright (`ValidationException: ExpressionAttributeNames must
49:   * not be empty`) - the key must be OMITTED, never `{}`, when a caller has nothing to map.
50:   * `buildVersionedUpdate()` below never hits this (it always maps at least #version/
51:   * #tenantId/#updatedAt), but a hand-rolled Update (update-organization-settings.ts) can. */
52:  ExpressionAttributeNames?: Record<string, string>;
53:  ExpressionAttributeValues: Record<string, unknown>;
54:}

61:}

72:
73:/**
74: * Private core shared by `buildVersionedUpdate()` (tenant-fenced, every ordinary business
75: * entity) and `buildAccountScopedVersionedUpdate()` (D-197 fatia 3/5: WhatsApp `WebhookInbox`,
76: * the first entity in this repo with no tenant at all - see
77: * `docs/architecture/reviews/whatsapp-channel-scoping/webhook-inbox-purge-scoping/` for the
78: * 3-round Claude<->Codex protocol, both final notes >=9.0, that decided this shape). The ONLY
79: * difference between the two public builders is which attribute anchors the scope fence
80: * (`tenantId` vs `accountId`) - version increment, `updatedAt`, SET/REMOVE generation,
81: * `extraConditions` composition and placeholder-collision detection are a single implementation,
82: * never duplicated, so the two builders can never drift apart on anything but their fence.
83: */
84:function buildScopedVersionedUpdate(input: ScopedVersionedUpdateInput, scope: ScopeCondition): DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
85:  const now = input.now ?? new Date().toISOString();
86:
87:  const names: Record<string, string> = {
88:    "#version": "version",
89:    [scope.namePlaceholder]: scope.attributeName,
90:    "#updatedAt": "updatedAt",
91:  };
92:  const values: Record<string, unknown> = {
93:    ":expectedVersion": input.expectedVersion,
94:    [scope.valuePlaceholder]: scope.value,
95:    ":one": 1,
96:    ":now": now,
97:  };
98:
99:  const setClauses = ["#version = #version + :one", "#updatedAt = :now"];
100:  let i = 0;
101:  for (const [field, value] of Object.entries(input.set)) {
102:    const nameKey = `#set${i}`;
103:    const valueKey = `:set${i}`;
104:    names[nameKey] = field;
105:    values[valueKey] = value;
106:    setClauses.push(`${nameKey} = ${valueKey}`);
107:    i += 1;
108:  }
109:
110:  const removeClauses: string[] = [];
111:  let j = 0;
112:  for (const field of input.remove ?? []) {
113:    const nameKey = `#rem${j}`;
114:    names[nameKey] = field;
115:    removeClauses.push(nameKey);
116:    j += 1;
117:  }
118:
119:  const expression =
120:    removeClauses.length > 0
121:      ? `SET ${setClauses.join(", ")} REMOVE ${removeClauses.join(", ")}`
122:      : `SET ${setClauses.join(", ")}`;
123:
124:  const baseConditionParts = ["attribute_exists(PK)", "attribute_exists(SK)", "#version = :expectedVersion", `${scope.namePlaceholder} = ${scope.valuePlaceholder}`];
125:  for (const extra of input.extraConditions ?? []) {
126:    for (const [nameKey, name] of Object.entries(extra.names ?? {})) {
127:      if (nameKey in names) throw new Error(`extraConditions name placeholder collides with a reserved/generated key: ${nameKey}`);
128:      names[nameKey] = name;
129:    }
130:    for (const [valueKey, value] of Object.entries(extra.values ?? {})) {
131:      if (valueKey in values) throw new Error(`extraConditions value placeholder collides with a reserved/generated key: ${valueKey}`);
132:      values[valueKey] = value;
133:    }
134:    baseConditionParts.push(`(${extra.expression})`);
135:  }
136:
137:  return {
138:    TableName: input.tableName,
139:    Key: input.key,
140:    UpdateExpression: expression,
141:    ConditionExpression: baseConditionParts.join(" AND "),
142:    ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
143:    ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
144:  };
145:}
146:
147:/**
148: * Builds an UpdateItem input enforcing implementation-blueprint.md #5.2's exact
149: * ConditionExpression: attribute_exists(PK) AND attribute_exists(SK) AND
150: * #version = :expectedVersion AND #tenantId = :tenantId, and increments version atomically.
151: * Contractually unchanged by the account-scoped sibling below - same signature, same generated
152: * expression, byte-for-byte, for every one of this repo's ~40 existing call sites.
153: */
154:export function buildVersionedUpdate(input: VersionedUpdateInput): DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
155:  return buildScopedVersionedUpdate(input, { namePlaceholder: "#tenantId", attributeName: "tenantId", valuePlaceholder: ":tenantId", value: input.tenantId });
156:}
157:
158:export interface AccountScopedVersionedUpdateInput extends ScopedVersionedUpdateInput {
159:  /** External vendor account id (e.g. a WhatsApp Business Account id) - NEVER a tenant id, real
160:   * or fabricated. Fences the update to the row's own account, same OCC-plus-scope-fence
161:   * structure as `buildVersionedUpdate()`, for an entity that genuinely has no tenant. */
162:  accountId: string;
163:}
164:
165:/**
166: * Account-scoped sibling of `buildVersionedUpdate()` (D-197 fatia 3/5) - identical
167: * ConditionExpression shape, `#accountId = :accountId` in place of `#tenantId = :tenantId`. Exists
168: * for exactly one entity today: WhatsApp `WebhookInbox` (`purgeScope: "ACCOUNT"`, permanently
169: * account-owned - see the purge-scoping decision this sibling builder itself depends on). Never
170: * use this for an entity that has (or could ever have) a real tenant - `buildVersionedUpdate()`
171: * is the only builder any tenant-scoped entity may use.
172: */
173:export function buildAccountScopedVersionedUpdate(input: AccountScopedVersionedUpdateInput): DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
174:  return buildScopedVersionedUpdate(input, { namePlaceholder: "#accountId", attributeName: "accountId", valuePlaceholder: ":accountId", value: input.accountId });
175:}

268:/**
269: * Builds a version-conditioned Delete input (W3-06 purge worker's terminal step - a Document
270: * row can only be physically removed if it is still at the exact version/state the claim
271: * observed, same OCC discipline as `buildVersionedUpdate`, just for `DeleteItem` instead of
272: * `UpdateItem`). `extraConditions` uses the same collision-checked merge as
273: * `buildVersionedUpdate` - see that function's docs.
274: */
275:export function buildVersionedDelete(input: {
276:  tableName: string;
277:  key: EntityKey;
278:  tenantId: string;
279:  expectedVersion: number;
280:  extraConditions?: Array<{ expression: string; names?: Record<string, string>; values?: Record<string, unknown> }>;
281:}): DynamoDeleteCommandInput {
282:  const names: Record<string, string> = { "#version": "version", "#tenantId": "tenantId" };
283:  const values: Record<string, unknown> = { ":expectedVersion": input.expectedVersion, ":tenantId": input.tenantId };
284:  const conditionParts = ["attribute_exists(PK)", "attribute_exists(SK)", "#version = :expectedVersion", "#tenantId = :tenantId"];
285:
286:  for (const extra of input.extraConditions ?? []) {
287:    for (const [nameKey, name] of Object.entries(extra.names ?? {})) {
288:      if (nameKey in names) throw new Error(`extraConditions name placeholder collides with a reserved key: ${nameKey}`);
289:      names[nameKey] = name;
290:    }
291:    for (const [valueKey, value] of Object.entries(extra.values ?? {})) {
292:      if (valueKey in values) throw new Error(`extraConditions value placeholder collides with a reserved key: ${valueKey}`);
293:      values[valueKey] = value;
294:    }
295:    conditionParts.push(`(${extra.expression})`);
296:  }
297:
298:  return {
299:    TableName: input.tableName,
300:    Key: input.key,
301:    ConditionExpression: conditionParts.join(" AND "),
302:    ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
303:    ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
304:  };
305:}
306:
307:/**
308: * Sibling of `buildConditionalPut` for `DeleteItem` - symmetric reasoning: `buildVersionedDelete`
309: * above always ANDs `#version = :expectedVersion`, which only makes sense for a versioned
310: * (mutable) entity. An append-only entity that is NEVER updated after creation (e.g. the
311: * `AuditEvent` family - `expiration`/`organization`/`subject`/`activity` modules' audit rows,
312: * D-153) has no `version` counter to check at all, so forcing one through `buildVersionedDelete`
313: * would require faking a field that does not exist on the row. Callers instead supply their own
314: * equality condition over whatever field they observed at scan time (same
315: * "hasn't changed since scan" intent as `buildVersionedDelete`'s `extraConditions`, just without
316: * a version to anchor it to) - `names`/`values` follow the same collision-free-by-caller-control
317: * discipline as `buildConditionalPut` (no generated placeholders to collide with here, since this
318: * builder adds none of its own beyond `attribute_exists`).
319: */
320:export function buildConditionalDelete(input: {
321:  tableName: string;
322:  key: EntityKey;
323:  conditionExpression: string;
324:  names?: Record<string, string>;
325:  values?: Record<string, unknown>;
326:}): DynamoDeleteCommandInput {
327:  return {
328:    TableName: input.tableName,
329:    Key: input.key,
330:    ConditionExpression: input.conditionExpression,
331:    ExpressionAttributeNames: input.names,
332:    ExpressionAttributeValues: input.values,
333:  };
334:}

336:export type TransactWriteEntry = TransactPutEntry | TransactUpdateEntry | TransactConditionCheckEntry | TransactDeleteEntry;
337:
338:/**
339: * Builds a TransactWriteItems `ConditionCheck` entry asserting a row is still at an
340: * expected version (and, optionally, still holds a given attribute value) - used to fence
341: * a commit against a fact read earlier in the same operation but not itself written by
342: * this transaction (e.g. reminder dispatch asserting the ReminderPolicy it read is still
343: * enabled at the version it read, per the BLOCKER-B freshness-fencing fix: reading a row
344: * and later writing elsewhere based on that read is a TOCTOU gap unless the read fact is
345: * re-asserted atomically inside the same TransactWriteItems as the write it gates).
346: */
347:export function buildVersionConditionCheck(input: {
348:  tableName: string;
349:  key: EntityKey;
350:  expectedVersion: number;
351:  /** Extra attribute=value equality conditions beyond version, e.g. { enabled: true, status: "ACTIVE" }. */
352:  extra?: Record<string, unknown>;
353:}): TransactConditionCheckEntry {
354:  const names: Record<string, string> = { "#version": "version" };
355:  const values: Record<string, unknown> = { ":version": input.expectedVersion };
356:  const clauses = ["#version = :version"];
357:
358:  let i = 0;
359:  for (const [field, value] of Object.entries(input.extra ?? {})) {
360:    const nameKey = `#c${i}`;
361:    const valueKey = `:c${i}`;
362:    names[nameKey] = field;
363:    values[valueKey] = value;
364:    clauses.push(`${nameKey} = ${valueKey}`);
365:    i += 1;
366:  }
367:
368:  return {
369:    ConditionCheck: {
370:      TableName: input.tableName,
371:      Key: input.key,
372:      ConditionExpression: clauses.join(" AND "),
373:      ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
374:      ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
375:    },
376:  };
377:}
378:
379:/**
380: * Sibling of `buildVersionConditionCheck` for facts that have no version to pin - e.g.
381: * BLOCKER-B's ITEM-scoped-policy integrity check (a policy references an item's
382: * existence/status/tenant, not a specific item version - referencing an item doesn't pin
383: * it to a version the way an occurrence's materialization does). `extra` must include at
384: * least one condition (an existence-only check with no `extra` would degenerate to
385: * `attribute_exists(PK)`, which every real row satisfies trivially - not useful on its own).
386: */
387:export function buildExistenceConditionCheck(input: {
388:  tableName: string;
389:  key: EntityKey;
390:  extra: Record<string, unknown>;
391:}): TransactConditionCheckEntry {
392:  const names: Record<string, string> = {};
393:  const values: Record<string, unknown> = {};
394:  const clauses = ["attribute_exists(PK)"];
395:
396:  let i = 0;
397:  for (const [field, value] of Object.entries(input.extra)) {
398:    const nameKey = `#c${i}`;
399:    const valueKey = `:c${i}`;
400:    names[nameKey] = field;
401:    values[valueKey] = value;
402:    clauses.push(`${nameKey} = ${valueKey}`);
403:    i += 1;
404:  }
405:
406:  return {
407:    ConditionCheck: {
408:      TableName: input.tableName,
409:      Key: input.key,
410:      ConditionExpression: clauses.join(" AND "),
411:      ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
412:      ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
413:    },
414:  };
415:}
416:
417:/** Name of the AWS SDK error thrown when any entry in a TransactWriteItems call fails its
418: * ConditionExpression - used by callers to distinguish OCC/idempotency conflicts from other
419: * DynamoDB errors without importing the SDK type. */
420:export const TRANSACTION_CANCELED = "TransactionCanceledException";
421:
422:export function isTransactionCanceled(err: unknown): boolean {
423:  return (
424:    typeof err === "object" &&
425:    err !== null &&
426:    "name" in err &&
427:    (err as { name?: unknown }).name === TRANSACTION_CANCELED
428:  );
429:}
430:
431:/**
432: * Reads the per-item `CancellationReasons` array off a `TransactionCanceledException` (verify
433: * with `isTransactionCanceled` first) - the real AWS SDK error carries one entry per transact
434: * item, each with a `Code` ("None" for an item that did not cause the cancellation,
435: * "ConditionalCheckFailed" for one that did). Wave B2B-5 (D-095, Codex Rodada 3 achado): a
436: * `TransactWriteItems` with more than one conditioned entry must never collapse "the
437: * transaction was canceled" into "MY condition specifically failed" - a caller distinguishing,
438: * say, a cap-guard `Update` at index 0 from an unrelated `Put` collision at index 2 needs the
439: * reason for the SPECIFIC index it cares about, not just the fact that something failed.
440: * Returns `undefined` (never throws) for any error shape that doesn't carry this array - callers
441: * fall back to treating the cancellation as "index unknown" rather than guessing.
442: */
443:export function getCancellationReasonCodes(err: unknown): string[] | undefined {
444:  if (typeof err !== "object" || err === null || !("CancellationReasons" in err)) return undefined;
445:  const reasons = (err as { CancellationReasons?: unknown }).CancellationReasons;
446:  if (!Array.isArray(reasons)) return undefined;
447:  return reasons.map((reason) => (typeof reason === "object" && reason !== null && "Code" in reason ? String((reason as { Code?: unknown }).Code) : "Unknown"));
448:}
449:
450:/**
451: * Builds an UpdateItem input for a narrow, single-purpose guard write: set one attribute exactly
452: * once, gated on it not already existing - no `version` counter, no `tenantId` condition (unlike
453: * `buildVersionedUpdate`, whose reserved condition requires `tenantId` and is therefore unusable
454: * for a genuinely tenantless entity). Wave B2B-5 (D-095, Codex Rodada 2/3 achado): `GlobalUser`
455: * (identity/persistence/global-user-repository.ts) is tenantless, so its
456: * `hasCreatedOrganization` cap flag needs this instead. Not a general-purpose OCC update - an
457: * entity that needs real optimistic concurrency later should get its own tenantless variant of
458: * `buildVersionedUpdate`, not a generalization of this narrow helper.
459: */
460:export function buildAttributeOnceUpdate(input: { tableName: string; key: EntityKey; attribute: string; value: unknown }): DynamoUpdateCommandInput {
461:  return {
462:    TableName: input.tableName,
463:    Key: input.key,
464:    UpdateExpression: "SET #attr = :value",
465:    ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(#attr)",
466:    ExpressionAttributeNames: { "#attr": input.attribute },
467:    ExpressionAttributeValues: { ":value": input.value },
468:  };
469:}

471:/** Name of the AWS SDK error thrown when a ConditionExpression fails - used by callers
472: * to distinguish OCC conflicts from other DynamoDB errors without importing the SDK type. */
473:export const CONDITIONAL_CHECK_FAILED = "ConditionalCheckFailedException";
474:
475:export function isConditionalCheckFailed(err: unknown): boolean {
476:  return (
477:    typeof err === "object" &&
478:    err !== null &&
479:    "name" in err &&
480:    (err as { name?: unknown }).name === CONDITIONAL_CHECK_FAILED
481:  );
