=== src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-reconciliation-handler.ts ===
1:/** Real handler for ReminderReconciliation (EventBridge Scheduler, `mode` field), replacing
2: * the 501 placeholder. Two schedules invoke the same Lambda with different `mode`
3: * (m3.5-runtime-design.md Â§"ReconciliaÃ§Ã£o"): CLAIMS (every 5 min) and DST (daily). Also one
4: * of exactly two roles granted gsi6Read() - see infra/lib/dynamo-table.ts.
5: *
6: * EventBridge Scheduler's Lambda target does NOT wrap the payload in a `detail` envelope
7: * the way legacy EventBridge Rules do - the event IS whatever `input` the schedule sets
8: * (infra/lib/reminder-schedule.ts: `{mode, scheduledTime}` at the top level), never
9: * `event.detail.mode` (bug found by Codex implementation review - event.detail was always
10: * undefined, both schedules would throw on every invocation). */
11:import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
12:import { createDocumentClient } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/client.js";
13:import { buildReconciliationDeps } from "../composition/reminder.js";
14:import { runReconciliation, type DstReconciliationCandidate as FullDstCandidate } from "../../../workers/reminder-reconciliation/reconciliation.js";
15:import { itemKey } from "../../../modules/expiration/domain/expiration-item.js";
16:import { authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity } from "../../../modules/identity/domain/authorization.js";
17:import { policyKey, type ReminderPolicy } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/reminder-policy.js";
18:import { defaultShardConfig } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.js";
19:import type { ReminderOccurrence } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/reminder-occurrence.js";
20:import { defaultSchemaRegistry } from "../../../shared/contracts/schema-validator.js";
21:import { runWithContext } from "../../../shared/observability/context.js";
22:import { SecureLogger } from "../../../shared/observability/logger.js";
23:
24:const client = createDocumentClient();
25:const envTableName = process.env["TABLE_NAME"];
26:if (!envTableName) throw new Error("TABLE_NAME env var is required.");
27:const tableName: string = envTableName;
28:const { store, candidateSource, now } = buildReconciliationDeps(client, tableName);
29:const shardConfig = defaultShardConfig();
30:const logger = new SecureLogger({ baseContext: { service: "reminder-reconciliation" } });
31:
32:export interface ReminderReconciliationEvent {
33:  mode: "CLAIMS" | "DST";
34:  scheduledTime: string;
35:}
36:
37:/** Hard cap on pages drained per invocation - matches the design's "pÃ¡gina lÃ³gica mÃ¡x. 200"
38: * per page with a bounded number of pages, so a pathological backlog can't make a single
39: * invocation run indefinitely; anything beyond this is picked up by the NEXT scheduled
40: * run (every 5 min for claims, daily for DST) rather than blocking this one. */
41:const MAX_PAGES = 25;
42:
43:const RECONCILIATION_EVENT_SCHEMA_ID = "https://expiration-tracker/schemas/events/reminder-reconciliation-event.v1.json";
44:
45:export async function handler(event: unknown): Promise<void> {
46:  // m5-observability-design.md #2: EventBridge Scheduler producer, no upstream request to
47:  // inherit a correlationId from - new UUID per invocation.
48:  await runWithContext({ correlationId: randomUUID() }, async () => {
49:    // P2.1 (external audit 2026-09-11): `event` was previously typed (not runtime-validated)
50:    // as `ReminderReconciliationEvent` straight off the Lambda invocation - a TS annotation
51:    // enforces nothing at runtime. Same schema-registry pattern reminder-dispatch-handler.ts's
52:    // `DISPATCH_COMMAND_SCHEMA_ID` already uses for its own boundary, mirrored here.
53:    const { valid, errors } = defaultSchemaRegistry.validate(RECONCILIATION_EVENT_SCHEMA_ID, event);
54:    if (!valid) {
55:      logger.error("reminder-reconciliation schema-invalid event", { errors });
56:      throw new Error("schema-invalid ReminderReconciliationEvent");
57:    }
58:    await handleReconciliation(event as ReminderReconciliationEvent);
59:  });
60:}
61:
62:async function handleReconciliation(event: ReminderReconciliationEvent): Promise<void> {
63:  const mode = event.mode;
64:
65:  const expiredClaimCandidates: ReminderOccurrence[] = [];
66:  const dstCandidates: FullDstCandidate[] = [];
67:
68:  if (mode === "CLAIMS") {
69:    // GSI6 is ALL-projected - each candidate row already IS the full ReminderOccurrence
70:    // item (see ExpiredClaimCandidate's doc comment), so no extra fetch is needed.
71:    // Pagination is drained (bug found by Codex implementation review: the first version
72:    // only ever read page 1, silently leaving any backlog over one page unprocessed).
73:    let cursor: string | undefined;
74:    for (let page = 0; page < MAX_PAGES; page++) {
75:      const result = await candidateSource.listExpiredClaims({ before: now(), cursor });
76:      expiredClaimCandidates.push(...(result.items as unknown as ReminderOccurrence[]));
77:      if (!result.cursor) break;
78:      cursor = result.cursor;
79:    }
80:  }
81:
82:  if (mode === "DST") {
83:    // ReminderMaterializer (M3.5) writes a WORKSTATE#DST_PENDING GSI6 pointer whenever a
84:    // trigger's schedule was computed at an ambiguous/nonexistent local time, OR whose
85:    // timeZone observes DST at all (see reminder-materializer.ts - broadened per Codex
86:    // implementation review: a non-ambiguous 09:00 reminder in a DST-observing zone can
87:    // still need re-evaluation after an offset change, not just literally-ambiguous ones).
88:    // This query finds those candidates; occurrences in a fixed-offset timeZone never
89:    // appear here at all.
90:    const windowStart = now();
91:    const windowEnd = new Date(Date.parse(windowStart) + 7 * 24 * 60 * 60_000).toISOString();
92:    let cursor: string | undefined;
93:    for (let page = 0; page < MAX_PAGES; page++) {
94:      const result = await candidateSource.listDstCandidates({ window: { start: windowStart, end: windowEnd }, cursor });
95:      for (const light of result.items) {
96:        const policy = await store.get<ReminderPolicy>(policyKey(light.tenantId, light.policyId));
97:        if (!policy) continue;
98:        const item = await store.get<{ PK: string; SK: string; dueDate: string; version: number }>(itemKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(light), light.itemId));
99:        if (!item) continue;
100:        dstCandidates.push({
101:          tenantId: light.tenantId,
102:          itemId: light.itemId,
103:          itemVersion: item.version,
104:          itemDueDate: item.dueDate,
105:          policy,
106:        });
107:      }
108:      if (!result.cursor) break;
109:      cursor = result.cursor;
110:    }
111:  }
112:
113:  const result = await runReconciliation({ store, tableName, now, shardConfig }, { expiredClaimCandidates, dstCandidates });
114:  logger.info("reminder-reconciliation complete", { mode, scheduledTime: event.scheduledTime, ...result });
115:}
=== src/workers/reminder-reconciliation/reconciliation.ts ===
1:/**
2: * Reconciliation - implementation-blueprint.md Â§9.5, run as ONE job/Lambda serving TWO
3: * purposes (per the task brief: "don't build two separate reconciliation paths"), both
4: * documented in Â§9.3 point 4 ("claims expirados... via reconciliaÃ§Ã£o, mesmo job da Â§9.5")
5: * and Â§9.5 itself (DST):
6: *
7: *  (a) claim-expiry reconciliation: any occurrence still CLAIMED past its
8: *      `claimExpiresAt` (dispatch worker crashed/timed out before completing) reverts to
9: *      SCHEDULED, conditionally, so the next producer tick's lookback window picks it up
10: *      again;
11: *  (b) DST-affected re-evaluation (Â§9.5 steps 1-6): for each (item, enabled policy) pair
12: *      in the reconciliation batch, recompute the expected schedule from the policy's
13: *      IANA timeZone + rule against a `[now, now+7d]` window, compare to what's actually
14: *      materialized, cancel divergent SCHEDULED occurrences and materialize missing ones
15: *      idempotently - TRIGGERED/ACKED history is never touched.
16: *
17: * Both passes reuse the same store/materializer/clock and are invoked together by
18: * `runReconciliation`, in the order claim-expiry -> DST (claim-expiry is cheaper and
19: * unblocks the producer sooner).
20: */
21:import { buildVersionedUpdate } from "../../shared/dynamodb/occ.js";
22:import { isTransactionCanceled, type ReminderStore } from "../../modules/reminder/ports/reminder-store.js";
23:import { ReminderMaterializer } from "../../modules/reminder/application/reminder-materializer.js";
24:import type { ReminderOccurrence } from "../../modules/reminder/domain/reminder-occurrence.js";
25:import type { ReminderPolicy } from "../../modules/reminder/domain/reminder-policy.js";
26:import type { ShardConfig } from "../../modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.js";
27:
28:export interface ReconciliationDeps {
29:  store: ReminderStore;
30:  tableName: string;
31:  now: () => string;
32:  shardConfig: ShardConfig;
33:}
34:
35:/** One (item, active policy) pair the DST pass evaluates. In production this batch comes
36: * from a GSI6-style "active reminder policies" index (left as infra follow-up, see report
37: * - M3's scope per the task brief is the reconciliation MECHANISM, not that index's
38: * wiring); tests/callers supply the batch directly. */
39:export interface DstReconciliationCandidate {
40:  tenantId: string;
41:  itemId: string;
42:  itemVersion: number;
43:  itemDueDate: string;
44:  policy: ReminderPolicy;
45:}
46:
47:export interface ReconciliationResult {
48:  claimsReverted: number;
49:  dstCancelled: number;
50:  dstCreated: number;
51:  dstDivergences: number;
52:}
53:
54:/**
55: * M10 cluster 4 (D-039/D-046/D-048): minimal structural shape claim-expiry reconciliation
56: * actually touches - widened from the original `ReminderOccurrence[]`-only signature so
57: * `DocumentChasingOccurrence` claims (same GSI6 `WORKSTATE#CLAIMED` workstate, same
58: * SCHEDULED<->CLAIMED lifecycle) revert through the EXACT SAME mechanism, no duplicated
59: * reconciliation path. Deliberately a PLAIN interface, not the existing `ExpiredClaimCandidate`
60: * (`reconciliation-candidate-source.ts`) - that type intersects `Record<string, unknown>` for
61: * an unrelated reason (mock/page shape elsewhere), and TypeScript does NOT consider a plain
62: * interface like `ReminderOccurrence` (no index signature) assignable to an intersection that
63: * requires one, even with every named property present - confirmed by trying the reuse first
64: * and hitting exactly that compiler error. Pure type-level widening either way: the function
65: * body is byte-for-byte unchanged, `ReminderOccurrence` already satisfies THIS shape
66: * structurally, so every existing caller/test keeps compiling and behaving identically. */
67:export interface ClaimExpiryCandidate {
68:  PK: string;
69:  SK: string;
70:  tenantId: string;
71:  status: string;
72:  claimExpiresAt?: string;
73:  version: number;
74:}
75:
76:/** Pass (a): any CLAIMED occurrence whose claimExpiresAt is in the past reverts to SCHEDULED. */
77:export async function reconcileExpiredClaims(
78:  deps: ReconciliationDeps,
79:  candidateOccurrences: ClaimExpiryCandidate[],
80:): Promise<number> {
81:  let reverted = 0;
82:  const now = deps.now();
83:  for (const occurrence of candidateOccurrences) {
84:    if (occurrence.status !== "CLAIMED") continue;
85:    if (!occurrence.claimExpiresAt || occurrence.claimExpiresAt > now) continue;
86:    try {
87:      await deps.store.transactWrite([
88:        {
89:          Update: buildVersionedUpdate({
90:            tableName: deps.tableName,
91:            key: { PK: occurrence.PK, SK: occurrence.SK },
92:            tenantId: occurrence.tenantId,
93:            expectedVersion: occurrence.version,
94:            set: { status: "SCHEDULED" },
95:            // M3.5: this claim is resolved (reverted) - its WORKSTATE#CLAIMED GSI6 pointer
96:            // must go too, or the next reconciliation pass would find it again forever.
97:            remove: ["GSI6PK", "GSI6SK"],
98:          }),
99:        },
100:      ]);
101:      reverted += 1;
102:    } catch (err) {
103:      if (!isTransactionCanceled(err)) throw err;
104:    }
105:  }
106:  return reverted;
107:}
108:
109:/** Pass (b): DST-affected re-evaluation over `[now, now+7d]` (Â§9.5 steps 1-6). */
110:export async function reconcileDst(
111:  deps: ReconciliationDeps,
112:  candidates: DstReconciliationCandidate[],
113:): Promise<{ cancelled: number; created: number; divergences: number }> {
114:  const materializer = new ReminderMaterializer(deps.store, deps.tableName, deps.now);
115:  const windowStart = Date.parse(deps.now());
116:  const windowEnd = windowStart + 7 * 24 * 60 * 60_000;
117:
118:  let cancelled = 0;
119:  let created = 0;
120:  let divergences = 0;
121:
122:  for (const candidate of candidates) {
123:    if (!candidate.policy.enabled) continue;
124:
125:    const existing = await deps.store.queryByItem<ReminderOccurrence>(candidate.tenantId, candidate.itemId);
126:    const liveExisting = existing.filter(
127:      (o) =>
128:        o.policyId === candidate.policy.policyId &&
129:        o.itemVersion === candidate.itemVersion &&
130:        o.policyVersion === candidate.policy.version &&
131:        (o.status === "SCHEDULED" || o.status === "CLAIMED"),
132:    );
133:
134:    // Expected schedule per trigger, recomputed fresh from the IANA rule (never a frozen offset).
135:    const expectedByTrigger = new Map<string, { scheduledAtUtc: string }>();
136:    for (const trigger of candidate.policy.triggers) {
137:      const schedule = materializer.computeSchedule({
138:        itemDueDate: candidate.itemDueDate,
139:        offsetIso: trigger.offsetIso,
140:        localTime: trigger.localTime,
141:        timeZone: candidate.policy.timeZone,
142:        quietHours: candidate.policy.quietHours,
143:      });
144:      const t = Date.parse(schedule.scheduledAtUtc);
145:      if (t < windowStart || t > windowEnd) continue; // out of the 7-day reconciliation window
146:      expectedByTrigger.set(trigger.triggerId, { scheduledAtUtc: schedule.scheduledAtUtc });
147:    }
148:
149:    // Cancel occurrences whose triggerId is no longer expected, or whose scheduledAt now
150:    // diverges from a fresh DST-aware recomputation (history TRIGGERED/ACKED untouched -
151:    // liveExisting already excludes those statuses).
152:    for (const occurrence of liveExisting) {
153:      const expected = expectedByTrigger.get(occurrence.triggerId);
154:      const diverges = !expected || expected.scheduledAtUtc !== occurrence.scheduledAt;
155:      if (!diverges) {
156:        // M3.5 (bug found by Codex implementation review round 1 - the original code left
157:        // WORKSTATE#DST_PENDING pointers in place forever whenever recomputation confirmed
158:        // the schedule was still correct). Round 2 found a race in THIS fix: `liveExisting`
159:        // includes CLAIMED occurrences too, and the producer can claim (status->CLAIMED,
160:        // GSI6PK->WORKSTATE#CLAIMED, version+1) in the window between GSI6 discovering this
161:        // occurrence as a DST candidate and this loop running - `queryByItem` above would
162:        // then read the ALREADY-CLAIMED row. The trigger recomputation still "confirms" (the
163:        // schedule itself didn't change), so without this guard the code would strip the
164:        // CLAIMED pointer via a version-matching OCC update, since the read and the write use
165:        // the same fresh version - OCC alone does NOT catch this, only a status/GSI6PK guard
166:        // does. Only ever touch a SCHEDULED occurrence that still actually carries the DST
167:        // pointer - never CLAIMED (that pointer belongs to the producer/dispatch lifecycle).
168:        if (occurrence.status === "SCHEDULED" && occurrence.GSI6PK === "WORKSTATE#DST_PENDING") {
169:          try {
170:            await deps.store.transactWrite([
171:              {
172:                Update: buildVersionedUpdate({
173:                  tableName: deps.tableName,
174:                  key: { PK: occurrence.PK, SK: occurrence.SK },
175:                  tenantId: candidate.tenantId,
176:                  expectedVersion: occurrence.version,
177:                  set: {},
178:                  remove: ["GSI6PK", "GSI6SK"],
179:                }),
180:              },
181:            ]);
182:          } catch (err) {
183:            if (!isTransactionCanceled(err)) throw err; // lost a race - fine, not our job to force it
184:          }
185:        }
186:        continue;
187:      }
188:      divergences += 1;
189:      try {
190:        await deps.store.transactWrite([
191:          {
192:            Update: buildVersionedUpdate({
193:              tableName: deps.tableName,
194:              key: { PK: occurrence.PK, SK: occurrence.SK },
195:              tenantId: candidate.tenantId,
196:              expectedVersion: occurrence.version,
197:              set: { status: "CANCELLED" },
198:              remove: ["GSI6PK", "GSI6SK"],
199:            }),
200:          },
201:        ]);
202:        cancelled += 1;
203:      } catch (err) {
204:        if (!isTransactionCanceled(err)) throw err;
205:      }
206:    }
207:
208:    // Materialize any expected trigger missing a live (SCHEDULED/CLAIMED) occurrence -
209:    // materialize() itself is idempotent (putIfAbsent keyed by the deterministic
210:    // occurrenceId hash), so re-running it for triggers that already matched is a safe no-op.
211:    const stillMissing = candidate.policy.triggers.some((t) => {
212:      const expected = expectedByTrigger.get(t.triggerId);
213:      if (!expected) return false;
214:      return !liveExisting.some((o) => o.triggerId === t.triggerId && o.scheduledAt === expected.scheduledAtUtc);
215:    });
216:    if (stillMissing) {
217:      const result = await materializer.materialize({
218:        tenantId: candidate.tenantId,
219:        itemId: candidate.itemId,
220:        itemVersion: candidate.itemVersion,
221:        itemDueDate: candidate.itemDueDate,
222:        policy: candidate.policy,
223:        shardConfig: deps.shardConfig,
224:      });
225:      created += result.created.length;
226:    }
227:  }
228:
229:  return { cancelled, created, divergences };
230:}
231:
232:export async function runReconciliation(
233:  deps: ReconciliationDeps,
234:  input: { expiredClaimCandidates: ClaimExpiryCandidate[]; dstCandidates: DstReconciliationCandidate[] },
235:): Promise<ReconciliationResult> {
236:  const claimsReverted = await reconcileExpiredClaims(deps, input.expiredClaimCandidates);
237:  const dst = await reconcileDst(deps, input.dstCandidates);
238:  return { claimsReverted, dstCancelled: dst.cancelled, dstCreated: dst.created, dstDivergences: dst.divergences };
239:}
=== src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-dispatch-handler.ts ===
1:/** Real handler for ReminderDispatch (SQS ReminderDispatchQueue), replacing the 501
2: * placeholder. Partial batch failure (m3.5-runtime-design.md Â§"SQS + DLQ"): only failed
3: * items are reported back, successful/duplicate/already-resolved items in the same batch
4: * are never retried. */
5:import type { SQSBatchResponse, SQSEvent } from "aws-lambda";
6:import { createDocumentClient } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/client.js";
7:import { buildReminderDispatchDeps } from "../composition/reminder.js";
8:import { dispatchOccurrence } from "../../../workers/reminder-dispatch/dispatch.js";
9:import type { DispatchCommand } from "../../../workers/reminder-producer/producer.js";
10:import { correlationIdFromSqsRecord, runWithContext } from "../../../shared/observability/context.js";
11:import { timeSpan, withHandlerTiming } from "../../../shared/observability/handler-timing.js";
12:import { SecureLogger } from "../../../shared/observability/logger.js";
13:import { emitMetric } from "../../../shared/observability/metrics.js";
14:import { defaultSchemaRegistry } from "../../../shared/contracts/schema-validator.js";
15:import { toAppError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
16:import { mapWithConcurrency } from "../../../shared/concurrency/map-with-concurrency.js";
17:
18:const client = createDocumentClient();
19:const tableName = process.env["TABLE_NAME"];
20:if (!tableName) throw new Error("TABLE_NAME env var is required.");
21:const deps = buildReminderDispatchDeps(client, tableName);
22:const logger = new SecureLogger({ baseContext: { service: "reminder-dispatch" } });
23:
24:// Contract source of truth for this queue payload: schemas/queues/reminder-dispatch.v1.json
25:// (audit round1/qualidade finding: a schema already existed but no runtime handler ever
26:// validated against it - JSON.parse(...) as DispatchCommand was a silent, unchecked cast).
27:const DISPATCH_COMMAND_SCHEMA_ID = "https://expiration-tracker/schemas/queues/reminder-dispatch.v1.json";
28:
29:// D-170: batch items are independent occurrences (different tenants/items in the general
30:// case) - processing them one at a time serialized every message's DynamoDB round trips
31:// behind the previous message's, even though nothing here needs that ordering. Bounded
32:// rather than unbounded parallelism to keep the burst of DynamoDB/SQS calls per Lambda
33:// invocation predictable (batch size is already capped at 10 by the queue config, this just
34:// avoids adding a 4th unbounded fan-out on top).
35:const BATCH_CONCURRENCY = 5;
36:
37:const NAMESPACE = "ExpirationTracker/ReminderDispatch";
38:
39:export const handler = withHandlerTiming<SQSEvent, SQSBatchResponse>(NAMESPACE, "reminder-dispatch handler", async (event) => {
40:  const results = await mapWithConcurrency(event.Records, BATCH_CONCURRENCY, async (record) => {
41:    await processRecord(record);
42:  });
43:
44:  // mapWithConcurrency never throws per-item - each record's own failure is captured in its
45:  // own result slot, so a slow/failing message never causes an unrelated, already-succeeded
46:  // message in the same batch to be reported as failed (or vice versa: swallowed silently).
47:  const batchItemFailures: { itemIdentifier: string }[] = [];
48:  results.forEach((result, i) => {
49:    if (!result.ok) batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: event.Records[i]!.messageId });
50:  });
51:  return { batchItemFailures };
52:});
53:
54:// D-170: extracted so `handler` can run this per-record body under mapWithConcurrency -
55:// failure is now signaled by throwing (mapWithConcurrency captures it into that record's own
56:// result slot) instead of pushing into a shared `batchItemFailures` array, which would have
57:// been a data race under concurrent execution.
58:async function processRecord(record: SQSEvent["Records"][number]): Promise<void> {
59:  // m5-observability-design.md #2's general SQS rule: read correlationId from the
60:  // command's own body (DispatchCommand.correlationId - real fix landed alongside this
61:  // handler, see producer.ts's DispatchCommand envelope fields), not from
62:  // MessageAttributes in isolation. This fallback chain only ever affects LOG CONTEXT,
63:  // never whether a message is processed: a schema-invalid message (including any
64:  // in-flight message from before this fix, which lacked `correlationId` entirely) is
65:  // always rejected as a poison message below, regardless of which correlationId source
66:  // is available. The chain just decides what correlationId that rejection's own log line
67:  // carries - MessageAttributes (still set by the relay/sweeper, sourced from the exact
68:  // same outbox event as the body) before falling back to the SQS messageId.
69:  const fallbackCorrelationId = correlationIdFromSqsRecord(record);
70:  await runWithContext({ correlationId: fallbackCorrelationId }, async () => {
71:    // `schemaInvalid` distinguishes the two failure branches below so each is logged exactly
72:    // once - the schema-invalid branch already logs its own specific reason before throwing;
73:    // re-logging it as a generic toAppError "reminder-dispatch failed" line in the catch
74:    // would duplicate the same event under two different messages.
75:    let schemaInvalid = false;
76:    try {
77:      const parsed: unknown = JSON.parse(record.body);
78:      const { valid, errors } = defaultSchemaRegistry.validate(DISPATCH_COMMAND_SCHEMA_ID, parsed);
79:      if (!valid) {
80:        // Deterministic poison message - will fail identically on redelivery - but still
81:        // reported as a batch item failure and retried up to maxReceiveCount like any other
82:        // failure, redriven under the uniform native SQS policy (D-128: no handler in this
83:        // codebase branches on retryable/poison classification; m3.5-runtime-design.md Â§10).
84:        schemaInvalid = true;
85:        logger.error("reminder-dispatch schema-invalid payload", { messageId: record.messageId, errors });
86:        throw new Error("schema-invalid payload");
87:      }
88:      const command = parsed as DispatchCommand;
89:      // m5-observability-design.md #2: nest the command's own correlationId + tenantId
90:      // once known, without mutating the outer per-record context (AsyncLocalStorage.run
91:      // composition).
92:      await runWithContext({ correlationId: command.correlationId ?? fallbackCorrelationId, tenantId: command.tenantId }, async () => {
93:        const outcome = await timeSpan(NAMESPACE, "lambda.business_operation_ms", "reminder-dispatch business operation timing", () => dispatchOccurrence(deps, command));
94:        logger.info("reminder-dispatch outcome", { messageId: record.messageId, outcome: outcome.kind });
95:        // E-018/E-021 (D-290) - Outcome is one of dispatch.ts's own 5 closed kinds
96:        // (TRIGGERED/ALREADY_TRIGGERED/CANCELLED_STALE/SKIPPED_NOT_CLAIMED/
97:        // ABORTED_FRESHNESS_RACE), never a high-cardinality value - see metrics.ts's own doc
98:        // comment on dimension discipline.
99:        emitMetric("ExpirationTracker/ReminderDispatch", { name: "OccurrenceDispatchOutcome", value: 1, unit: "Count", dimensions: { Outcome: outcome.kind } });
100:      });
101:    } catch (err) {
102:      if (!schemaInvalid) {
103:        // errorCode/retryable logged for consistency with every other handler's pattern (e.g.
104:        // textract-task-handler.ts) - D-128 decided no handler branches on this value; it is
105:        // diagnostic metadata only (see app-error.ts's isRetryable() doc comment).
106:        const appErr = toAppError(err);
107:        logger.error("reminder-dispatch failed", { messageId: record.messageId, errorCode: appErr.code, retryable: appErr.retryable });
108:        emitMetric("ExpirationTracker/ReminderDispatch", { name: "OccurrenceDispatchOutcome", value: 1, unit: "Count", dimensions: { Outcome: "HANDLER_ERROR" } });
109:      }
110:      throw err;
111:    }
112:  });
113:}
=== src/runtime/aws/composition/reminder.ts ===
1:/** Composition root for the reminder module and its async workers against real DynamoDB/SQS (M3.5). */
2:import type { DynamoDBDocumentClient } from "@aws-sdk/lib-dynamodb";
3:import { GetCommand, QueryCommand } from "@aws-sdk/lib-dynamodb";
4:import { SQSClient, SendMessageCommand } from "@aws-sdk/client-sqs";
5:import { DynamoDbReminderStore } from "../../../modules/reminder/persistence/dynamodb-reminder-store.js";
6:import { DynamoDbReminderProducerStore } from "../../../modules/reminder/persistence/dynamodb-reminder-producer-store.js";
7:import { DynamoDbReminderReconciliationCandidateSource } from "../../../modules/reminder/persistence/dynamodb-reconciliation-candidate-source.js";
8:import { DynamoDbOutboxRelayStore } from "../../../shared/outbox/persistence/dynamodb-outbox-relay-store.js";
9:import { ReminderPolicyService } from "../../../modules/reminder/application/reminder-policy-service.js";
10:import { defaultShardConfig } from "../../../modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.js";
11:import type { TenantManagerLookup } from "../../../modules/reminder/ports/tenant-manager-lookup.js";
12:import { organizationKey } from "../../../modules/organization/domain/organization.js";
13:import { membershipKey, type Membership } from "../../../modules/organization/domain/membership.js";
14:import { globalUserKey } from "../../../modules/identity/persistence/global-user-repository.js";
15:import { authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity } from "../../../modules/identity/domain/authorization.js";
16:import { UlidIdGenerator, newCorrelationId } from "../ids.js";
17:
18:const MANAGER_ROLES: ReadonlySet<Membership["role"]> = new Set(["OWNER", "ADMIN"]);
19:
20:/** D-201 (MANAGER escalation): same 2-layer eligibility bar as
21: * `expiration.ts`'s `buildMemberEligibilityChecker` (Membership ACTIVE AND GlobalUser
22: * identityStatus ACTIVE), extended with a role filter - a "manager" is real RBAC
23: * (`OWNER`/`ADMIN`), never a separately configured list. */
24:export function buildTenantManagerLookup(client: DynamoDBDocumentClient, tableName: string): TenantManagerLookup {
25:  async function isGlobalUserActive(userId: string): Promise<boolean> {
26:    const result = await client.send(new GetCommand({ TableName: tableName, Key: globalUserKey(userId), ConsistentRead: true }));
27:    return (result.Item as { identityStatus?: string } | undefined)?.identityStatus === "ACTIVE";
28:  }
29:
30:  return {
31:    async listActiveManagers(tenantId: string): Promise<{ userId: string }[]> {
32:      const { PK } = organizationKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity({ tenantId }));
33:      const result = await client.send(new QueryCommand({ TableName: tableName, ConsistentRead: true, KeyConditionExpression: "PK = :pk AND begins_with(SK, :prefix)", ExpressionAttributeValues: { ":pk": PK, ":prefix": "MEMBER#" } }));
34:      const candidates = ((result.Items ?? []) as Membership[]).filter((m) => m.status === "ACTIVE" && MANAGER_ROLES.has(m.role));
35:      const activeFlags = await Promise.all(candidates.map((m) => isGlobalUserActive(m.userId)));
36:      return candidates.filter((_, i) => activeFlags[i]).map((m) => ({ userId: m.userId }));
37:    },
38:    async isActiveManager(tenantId: string, userId: string): Promise<boolean> {
39:      const result = await client.send(new GetCommand({ TableName: tableName, Key: membershipKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity({ tenantId }), userId), ConsistentRead: true }));
40:      const membership = result.Item as Membership | undefined;
41:      if (membership?.status !== "ACTIVE" || !MANAGER_ROLES.has(membership.role)) return false;
42:      return isGlobalUserActive(userId);
43:    },
44:  };
45:}
46:
47:export function buildReminderHttpDeps(client: DynamoDBDocumentClient, tableName: string) {
48:  const store = new DynamoDbReminderStore(client, tableName);
49:  const ids = new UlidIdGenerator();
50:  const policies = new ReminderPolicyService({ store, tableName, ids });
51:  return { store, policies };
52:}
53:
54:export function buildReminderDispatchDeps(client: DynamoDBDocumentClient, tableName: string) {
55:  const store = new DynamoDbReminderStore(client, tableName);
56:  const ids = new UlidIdGenerator();
57:  return {
58:    store,
59:    tableName,
60:    managerLookup: buildTenantManagerLookup(client, tableName),
61:    now: () => new Date().toISOString(),
62:    newIntentId: () => ids.newIntentId(),
63:    newEventId: () => ids.newEventId(),
64:    correlationId: () => newCorrelationId(),
65:  };
66:}
67:
68:export function buildReminderProducerDeps(client: DynamoDBDocumentClient, tableName: string) {
69:  const store = new DynamoDbReminderProducerStore(client, tableName);
70:  const ids = new UlidIdGenerator();
71:  return {
72:    store,
73:    tableName,
74:    now: () => new Date().toISOString(),
75:    newEventId: () => ids.newEventId(),
76:    correlationId: () => newCorrelationId(),
77:  };
78:}

94:/** M10 cluster 4 (D-039/D-046/D-048): `chasingQueueUrl` is optional so this function keeps

96: * SECOND sender to the SAME relay Lambda/DynamoDB Streams event source mapping (mirrors

98: * pattern, per m4-notification-engine-design.md Â§7.4 - never a new relay Lambda just for a

101:export function buildOutboxRelayDeps(
102:  client: DynamoDBDocumentClient,
103:  tableName: string,
104:  queueUrl: string,
105:  sqsClient: SQSClient = new SQSClient({}),
106:  chasingQueueUrl?: string,
107:  importCommitQueueUrl?: string,
108:  materializationTriggerQueueUrl?: string,

111:  // claim (import-service.ts#submitImportMapping). Same "bare event.data, self-contained"
112:  // payload shape as SQS_IMPORT_COMMIT_V1 (tenantId embedded, no extra envelope wrapping).
113:  importParseQueueUrl?: string,
114:  // D-193 item 6/9: FIFTH optional sender - `confirmFieldForDocumentArchive`/`commitRunOutcome`
115:  // dispatch this destination in the same TWI as `DocumentVersion`'s `validUntil` Update, only
116:  // when it actually changed. Same bare-event-data shape as SQS_IMPORT_PARSE_V1 above; the
117:  // payload's `validUntil` is a mere wake-up hint - requirement-evidence-refresh-handler.ts
118:  // never trusts it, always re-reads DocumentVersion+Requirement fresh.
119:  requirementEvidenceRefreshQueueUrl?: string,

123:  // `scheduledFor`) as the other bare-payload destinations above -
124:  // report-subscription-delivery-handler.ts never trusts anything beyond those four fields.
125:  reportSubscriptionDeliveryQueueUrl?: string,
126:  // D-205 fatia 2 (Roadmap P1 item 16): SEVENTH optional sender -
127:  // `DocumentArchiveService.confirmDossierExport` dispatches this destination in the same TWI
128:  // as its `DossierExportRun` status Update (CONFIRMED). Same bare-event-data shape
129:  // (`runId`/`subjectId`/`tenantId`) as the other bare-payload destinations above.
130:  dossierExportQueueUrl?: string,
131:  // D-226 (Roadmap P0 item 9): EIGHTH optional sender - `buildDocumentRequestCreatedOutboxEntry`
132:  // dispatches this destination in the same TWI that creates/reissues a `DocumentRequest`. Same
133:  // bare-event-data shape (`tenantId`/`subjectId`/`documentRequestId`/`issuanceGeneration`) as the
134:  // other bare-payload destinations above - document-request-credential-issuance-handler.ts (the
135:  // GUEST Lambda, holds the D-146 pepper) never trusts anything beyond those four fields, always
136:  // re-reads the authoritative DocumentRequest.
137:  guestCredentialIssuanceQueueUrl?: string,
138:) {
139:  const store = new DynamoDbOutboxRelayStore(client, tableName);
140:  const send = (targetQueueUrl: string) => async (payload: Record<string, unknown>, correlationId: string) => {
141:    await sqsClient.send(
142:      new SendMessageCommand({
143:        QueueUrl: targetQueueUrl,
144:        MessageBody: JSON.stringify(payload),
145:        MessageAttributes: { correlationId: { DataType: "String", StringValue: correlationId } },
146:      }),
147:    );
148:  };
149:  // BLOCKER-B (reminder-delivery-pipeline.md Â§4): unlike DispatchCommand, this destination's
150:  // payload is the bare domain event data (matches schemas/events/*.json exactly, no
151:  // envelope wrapping) - tenantId/eventType aren't embedded in it, so this sender folds them
152:  // in from the OutboxRecord's own envelope fields before sending, giving the trigger
153:  // handler a self-contained message it can build a TriggerEvent from without any other
154:  // context.
155:  const sendMaterializationTrigger = (targetQueueUrl: string) => async (payload: Record<string, unknown>, correlationId: string, tenantId: string, eventType: string) => {
156:    await sqsClient.send(
157:      new SendMessageCommand({
158:        QueueUrl: targetQueueUrl,
159:        MessageBody: JSON.stringify({ eventType, tenantId, data: payload }),
160:        MessageAttributes: { correlationId: { DataType: "String", StringValue: correlationId } },
161:      }),
162:    );
163:  };
164:  return {
165:    store,
166:    now: () => new Date().toISOString(),
167:    senders: {
168:      SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1: send(queueUrl),
169:      ...(chasingQueueUrl ? { SQS_DOCUMENT_CHASING_DISPATCH_V1: send(chasingQueueUrl) } : {}),
170:      ...(importCommitQueueUrl ? { SQS_IMPORT_COMMIT_V1: send(importCommitQueueUrl) } : {}),
171:      ...(importParseQueueUrl ? { SQS_IMPORT_PARSE_V1: send(importParseQueueUrl) } : {}),
172:      ...(requirementEvidenceRefreshQueueUrl ? { SQS_REQUIREMENT_EVIDENCE_REFRESH_V1: send(requirementEvidenceRefreshQueueUrl) } : {}),
173:      ...(reportSubscriptionDeliveryQueueUrl ? { SQS_REPORT_SUBSCRIPTION_DELIVERY_V1: send(reportSubscriptionDeliveryQueueUrl) } : {}),
174:      ...(dossierExportQueueUrl ? { SQS_DOSSIER_EXPORT_V1: send(dossierExportQueueUrl) } : {}),
175:      ...(guestCredentialIssuanceQueueUrl ? { SQS_DOCUMENT_REQUEST_CREDENTIAL_ISSUANCE_V1: send(guestCredentialIssuanceQueueUrl) } : {}),
176:      ...(materializationTriggerQueueUrl ? { SQS_REMINDER_MATERIALIZATION_TRIGGER_V1: sendMaterializationTrigger(materializationTriggerQueueUrl) } : {}),
177:    },
178:  };
179:}

2026-09-15T01:30:31.786557Z ERROR codex_models_manager::manager: failed to renew cache TTL: missing field `supports_parallel_tool_calls` at line 132 column 5
codex
CRITIQUE

The proposal correctly verifies several repository facts:

- The existing names are `module "reminder_producer"` and `${local.name_prefix}-reminder-producer` (`infra/main.tf:255-260`), while dispatch uses `module "dispatch_queue"`, `${local.name_prefix}-reminder-dispatch`, and `module "reminder_dispatch"` (`infra/main.tf:282-295, 944-959`). The proposed queue/function names broadly follow that convention.
- The shared queue module really fixes visibility timeout at `consumer_timeout_seconds * 6` and `maxReceiveCount = 5` (`infra/modules/sqs-worker-queue/main.tf:17-26`).
- `DEFAULT_SHARD_COUNT` really is 4 (`src/modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.ts:28-31`).
- Correlation extraction really uses the `correlationId` message attribute with fallback to SQS `messageId` (`src/shared/observability/context.ts:25-36`), and batch handlers are expected to establish context once per record (`context.ts:1-4`).
- `emitMetric` supports Count/Milliseconds and low-cardinality dimensions (`src/shared/observability/metrics.ts:23-29, 38-63`).
- The existing reminder claim really appends `SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1` to the same transaction as the conditional occurrence update (`src/workers/reminder-producer/producer.ts:266-289`).
- No `test/integration/gsi3-isolation.test.ts` exists. The real deployed-IAM equivalent is the GSI3 assertion block in `infra/tests/stack.tftest.hcl:55-149`. The proposal is right to correct that stale reference.

However, the implementation plan is not executable and its page-lease protocol is currently unsound.

1. The continuation cannot acquire the lease it was just handed.

The plan says every invocation gets a fresh `ownerToken` (`proposal:81-84`). After processing a page it renews the lease for 90 seconds and then publishes an immediately visible continuation (`104-119, 139-147`). That continuation enters a new invocation with a new owner token while the preceding invocation’s lease remains unexpired. It therefore cannot satisfy either first acquisition or expired-lease reclamation.

If this expected contention is treated as success/no-op, Lambda deletes the only continuation message and forward progress is lost. If treated as failure, the message remains invisible for 180 seconds and retries only after the lease expires, imposing at least a three-minute delay per page. Neither behavior matches the claimed page chain or capacity model. The plan needs an explicit ownership-transfer protocol, not simply “renew, then send continuation.”

2. There is an unrecovered failure window between checkpoint and continuation publication.

The proposed ordering is:

- publish candidates;
- update `lastEvaluatedKey`;
- send continuation.

If the process dies after the lease update but before sending the continuation, no SQS continuation exists for that checkpoint. The proposal claims the same continuation message will trigger reclamation (`121-126`), but that message was never sent.

The fallback assertion that a fresh EventBridge tick naturally reclaims the lease is not fully specified and contradicts the keying model. The lease key includes `tickMinuteISO` (`77-78`), while a new scheduler tick ordinarily has a different minute. The plan never specifies that each new scheduler invocation enumerates the historical lookback minutes and consults their existing lease rows before creating work.

3. “One page” is incompatible with the current port and adapter.

`ReminderProducerStore.queryGsi3` accepts only `gsi3pk` and returns `Promise<T[]>` (`src/modules/reminder/ports/reminder-store.ts:42-52`). Its DynamoDB implementation internally loops until `LastEvaluatedKey` is absent and returns every page (`src/modules/reminder/persistence/dynamodb-reminder-producer-store.ts:20-43`).

Consequently, `scan-page.ts` cannot perform one page or receive a `LastEvaluatedKey` without an explicitly planned port and adapter change. The proposal claims this is a bounded one-page operation but never specifies the required new input/output types, query `Limit`, `ExclusiveStartKey`, or security-audit behavior per logical page.

4. The proposed lease writes do not fit the cited OCC builders.

`buildVersionedUpdate` requires a `tenantId` and adds both a tenant fence and version condition (`src/shared/dynamodb/occ.ts:15-38, 124-155`). The proposed global shard/minute lease schema contains no `tenantId` (`proposal:76-94`). Calling `buildVersionedUpdate` as proposed is therefore impossible without inventing a fake tenant, adding an appropriate global/account-scoped builder, or redesigning the lease item.

`buildConditionalPut` can express the proposed acquisition condition (`occ.ts:198-223`), but the plan does not supply the exact attribute-name/value maps. More importantly, its completed arm is internally confused: the condition includes `status = :completedNoop`, yet the text says application code returns without performing the put (`proposal:96-102`). If the pre-read races with another writer, the exact safe conditional behavior remains undefined.

5. Exact queue schemas—the central requested deliverable—are missing.

The proposal names two destinations but never gives either SQS message body schema. It does not define required fields, `additionalProperties`, schema IDs, versions, timestamp formats, raw `GSI3SK` representation, serialized DynamoDB key typing, or validation points. The repository’s queue contracts are actual JSON Schemas under `schemas/queues/`, and AGENTS.md section 7 requires every new command/event to receive valid and invalid contract tests.

Adding `SQS_REMINDER_SCAN_CONTINUATION_V1` and `SQS_REMINDER_CLAIM_CANDIDATE_V1` to `OutboxDestination` is also unjustified because the proposal explicitly says neither message uses the transactional outbox (`proposal:60-65, 212-223`). `OutboxDestination` currently describes actual outbox routing (`src/shared/outbox/outbox.ts:21-33, 110, 126-180`). Direct SQS message types should not be added to that union merely to name them.

6. The scheduler-to-scan-queue topology is contradictory and incomplete.

The current scheduler invokes `reminder-producer-handler` once, and that handler calls `runProducerTick` once (`src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-producer-handler.ts:35-41`). `runProducerTick` computes partitions and loops them itself (`src/workers/reminder-producer/producer.ts:184-190`).

The proposal says the same Lambda retains EventBridge plus SQS triggers and describes EventBridge as “one message per eligible shard/minute — unchanged entry point” (`proposal:48-53`). That is not the current behavior. It never decides whether the scheduler invocation:

- performs page 1 directly for every shard/minute;
- emits start messages to the scan queue;
- processes exactly one shard/minute itself; or
- retains a bounded loop over several start units.

Without this, IAM send permissions, timeout bounds, message schema, and lease recovery cannot be implemented.

7. The timeout and throughput math is not rigorous.

The statement that 180-second visibility means the same message can never race an unexpired 90-second lease is true only for the original message’s redelivery. It does not address the newly sent, immediately visible continuation or fresh scheduler work arriving every 60 seconds.

`maxReceiveCount × visibility timeout` is not a reliable exact DLQ duration: processing time, receive timing, Lambda backoff, throttling, and event-source behavior affect elapsed time. “Up to 900s” is therefore not established by the cited module constants.

The claim that a page batches “up to 25 candidates per `SendMessageBatch` call” (`proposal:164-165`) is invalid: SQS `SendMessageBatch` accepts at most 10 entries. The plan also omits the partial-success algorithm required by D-299: retry only failed entries, classify sender faults, and avoid checkpoint/continuation advancement until every candidate is durably accepted.

The 16-worker capacity choice is based on an asserted sub-200ms p50 and a 100-item illustrative burst (`163-168`), not the 10,000/minute requirement. It does not calculate sustained records/second, batch concurrency behavior, DynamoDB capacity/throttling, Lambda event-source scaling, or worst-case/p95 processing time. Nor does it provide `maximum_concurrency`, despite D-299 explicitly requiring it and the invariant relating it to reserved concurrency.

8. Observability is named but not fully wired.

Correlation propagation is directionally correct, but the plan does not name the sender adapter/composition function that performs `SendMessageBatch`, its inputs, or how partial failures retain the original correlation ID. It also fails to reconcile per-record `runWithContext` with a batch-size-10 claim handler.

The custom metric list omits important failure and progress signals: continuation publication failure, partial candidate-send failure, lease age/expiry, reclaim lag, unknown GSI3 rows, page duration, and scan completion latency. No dashboard widgets or alarms are specified for the new custom metrics. Native queue alarms do not replace alarms for a scan that has stalled while its queue is empty because the continuation was never published.

The logging schema also lacks error code, attempt/message identity, owner token-safe diagnostic identity, lease age, and checkpoint/page identity needed to investigate the state-machine transitions.

9. Error taxonomy is explicitly unresolved.

The proposal says downstream failures use “unchanged AppError subclasses” but does not map DynamoDB conditional failure, malformed SQS payload, unknown discriminator, transient SQS partial failure, permanent SQS sender failure, lease contention, and lease-store failure to exact handler outcomes. It then leaves `ReminderLeaseConflictError` as a future open question (`proposal:205-211`). That directly violates the requirement that the later implementer face zero judgment calls.

10. The test plan misses or contradicts the highest-risk failure cases.

The application integration test demands candidates be “published exactly once each” (`proposal:244-249`), while the design explicitly republishes a page after failure between candidate send and checkpoint (`107-116`). At-least-once publication means that assertion is wrong; the correct invariant is eventual complete processing with duplicates tolerated and exactly one successful conditional claim per occurrence.

Missing dedicated tests include:

- death after candidate send but before checkpoint;
- death after checkpoint but before continuation send;
- immediate continuation arrival while predecessor lease is active;
- partial `SendMessageBatch` success with mixed successful/retryable/permanent entries;
- reclaim at the exact expiry boundary;
- stale continuation after a later page completed;
- duplicate scheduler starts;
- multiple lookback minutes and shard generations;
- invalid/malicious serialized `ExclusiveStartKey`;
- unknown raw `GSI3SK`;
- claim transaction cancellation reason classification;
- both ReminderOccurrence and DocumentChasingOccurrence end-to-end;
- Lambda timeout during batch processing with correct `batchItemFailures`;
- real DynamoDB Local pagination, conditional writes, and outbox transaction;
- Terraform assertions for both queue consumers’ `ReportBatchItemFailures`, batch sizes, queue policies, reserved/maximum concurrency, visibility, alarms, and sole GSI3 holder.

11. Reconciliation and migration conclusions are asserted before verification.

The reconciliation section labels “Confirmed: no change needed” but simultaneously says the file must be reread in round 2 (`proposal:251-264`). That is not confirmation. The plan needs citation-level verification of the existing GSI6/`claimExpiresAt` contract and tests proving the extracted claim logic writes exactly the same reconciliation pointer and expiry fields.

The rollout is described as “big-bang” but then as three deployment steps within the same Terraform release (`268-279`). Terraform does not guarantee that operational sequence unless explicit dependencies or a multi-apply rollout are defined.

The rollback claim is unsafe. Keeping the scan event-source mapping enabled while reverting to the old EventBridge-only handler means queued continuation records can invoke code that does not understand SQS events. The claim queue and consumer are not necessarily inert because already-published candidates can remain. Old in-flight producer invocations and the new claim consumer can coexist; conditional claims make that potentially safe, but it must be tested and documented. Lease rows and queued continuations also require an explicit drain/disable/cleanup policy.

SCORE: 4.3/10

REQUIRED CHANGES

1. Replace the current continuation/lease protocol with a complete, mechanically valid state transition that permits page N+1 to proceed without waiting for page N’s lease to expire. Define exact ownership transfer, conditions, state fields, and behavior for stale/duplicate continuations.

2. Close the checkpoint-before-continuation loss window. Use a durable transactional outbox tied to the lease checkpoint, or define another proven recovery mechanism that independently discovers every unfinished historical shard/minute. Specify it completely and test process death at every boundary.

3. Define the scheduler topology exactly: which component emits start messages, the complete shard/minute/lookback enumeration, how duplicate starts behave, and which IAM permissions each component needs.

4. Change the GSI3 port and adapter contract explicitly to a single-page API with exact request/response types, `ExclusiveStartKey`, `LastEvaluatedKey`, optional `Limit`, and security-audit semantics.

5. Provide the exact persisted lease schema, including timestamps, TTL field name, scope/fence strategy, version initialization, serialized key representation, and exact acquire/renew/complete/reclaim conditions. Resolve the incompatibility with tenant-fenced `buildVersionedUpdate`.

6. Provide complete JSON Schemas for both queue messages, with exact filenames, `$id`, required properties, `additionalProperties`, validation locations, valid/invalid contract cases, and versioning. Remove the new `OutboxDestination` members unless these messages genuinely use the outbox.

7. Specify `SendMessageBatch` behavior using its real maximum of 10 entries. Define chunking, partial-success retries, sender-fault handling, retry bounds, and the rule preventing checkpoint advancement until every candidate is accepted.

8. Recalculate timeouts, visibility, lease duration, batch sizes, maximum concurrency, and reserved concurrency from worst-case paths and the 10,000/minute target. Include event-source scaling and throttling assumptions; state exact Terraform values and enforce their invariants in tests.

9. Give an exact error/outcome table covering every failure class and whether each SQS record is acknowledged, returned in `batchItemFailures`, retried, or treated as a terminal no-op. Remove all deferred error-taxonomy questions.

10. Complete observability wiring: exact logger/metric call sites, dimensions, progress/failure metrics, dashboards and alarms, plus a signal that detects a missing continuation while no queue backlog exists.

11. Rewrite the test plan around at-least-once publication and exactly-once-effective claiming. Add all failure windows and edge cases listed in critique item 10, including real DynamoDB Local pagination/transactions and comprehensive Terraform assertions.

12. Verify reconciliation directly and cite the exact implementation lines. Prove through tests that extracted claim logic preserves `claimExpiresAt`, GSI6 reconciliation pointers, cancellation-reason handling, and transactional dispatch outbox behavior byte-for-byte semantically.

13. Define a safe deploy and rollback procedure with explicit event-source enable/disable ordering, queue drain behavior, coexistence handling for old invocations, rollback compatibility with queued message shapes, and lease-row cleanup/retention.
tokens used
60.363
CRITIQUE

The proposal correctly verifies several repository facts:

- The existing names are `module "reminder_producer"` and `${local.name_prefix}-reminder-producer` (`infra/main.tf:255-260`), while dispatch uses `module "dispatch_queue"`, `${local.name_prefix}-reminder-dispatch`, and `module "reminder_dispatch"` (`infra/main.tf:282-295, 944-959`). The proposed queue/function names broadly follow that convention.
- The shared queue module really fixes visibility timeout at `consumer_timeout_seconds * 6` and `maxReceiveCount = 5` (`infra/modules/sqs-worker-queue/main.tf:17-26`).
- `DEFAULT_SHARD_COUNT` really is 4 (`src/modules/reminder/domain/shard-config.ts:28-31`).
- Correlation extraction really uses the `correlationId` message attribute with fallback to SQS `messageId` (`src/shared/observability/context.ts:25-36`), and batch handlers are expected to establish context once per record (`context.ts:1-4`).
- `emitMetric` supports Count/Milliseconds and low-cardinality dimensions (`src/shared/observability/metrics.ts:23-29, 38-63`).
- The existing reminder claim really appends `SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1` to the same transaction as the conditional occurrence update (`src/workers/reminder-producer/producer.ts:266-289`).
- No `test/integration/gsi3-isolation.test.ts` exists. The real deployed-IAM equivalent is the GSI3 assertion block in `infra/tests/stack.tftest.hcl:55-149`. The proposal is right to correct that stale reference.

However, the implementation plan is not executable and its page-lease protocol is currently unsound.

1. The continuation cannot acquire the lease it was just handed.

The plan says every invocation gets a fresh `ownerToken` (`proposal:81-84`). After processing a page it renews the lease for 90 seconds and then publishes an immediately visible continuation (`104-119, 139-147`). That continuation enters a new invocation with a new owner token while the preceding invocation’s lease remains unexpired. It therefore cannot satisfy either first acquisition or expired-lease reclamation.

If this expected contention is treated as success/no-op, Lambda deletes the only continuation message and forward progress is lost. If treated as failure, the message remains invisible for 180 seconds and retries only after the lease expires, imposing at least a three-minute delay per page. Neither behavior matches the claimed page chain or capacity model. The plan needs an explicit ownership-transfer protocol, not simply “renew, then send continuation.”

2. There is an unrecovered failure window between checkpoint and continuation publication.

The proposed ordering is:

- publish candidates;
- update `lastEvaluatedKey`;
- send continuation.

If the process dies after the lease update but before sending the continuation, no SQS continuation exists for that checkpoint. The proposal claims the same continuation message will trigger reclamation (`121-126`), but that message was never sent.

The fallback assertion that a fresh EventBridge tick naturally reclaims the lease is not fully specified and contradicts the keying model. The lease key includes `tickMinuteISO` (`77-78`), while a new scheduler tick ordinarily has a different minute. The plan never specifies that each new scheduler invocation enumerates the historical lookback minutes and consults their existing lease rows before creating work.

3. “One page” is incompatible with the current port and adapter.

`ReminderProducerStore.queryGsi3` accepts only `gsi3pk` and returns `Promise<T[]>` (`src/modules/reminder/ports/reminder-store.ts:42-52`). Its DynamoDB implementation internally loops until `LastEvaluatedKey` is absent and returns every page (`src/modules/reminder/persistence/dynamodb-reminder-producer-store.ts:20-43`).

Consequently, `scan-page.ts` cannot perform one page or receive a `LastEvaluatedKey` without an explicitly planned port and adapter change. The proposal claims this is a bounded one-page operation but never specifies the required new input/output types, query `Limit`, `ExclusiveStartKey`, or security-audit behavior per logical page.

4. The proposed lease writes do not fit the cited OCC builders.

`buildVersionedUpdate` requires a `tenantId` and adds both a tenant fence and version condition (`src/shared/dynamodb/occ.ts:15-38, 124-155`). The proposed global shard/minute lease schema contains no `tenantId` (`proposal:76-94`). Calling `buildVersionedUpdate` as proposed is therefore impossible without inventing a fake tenant, adding an appropriate global/account-scoped builder, or redesigning the lease item.

`buildConditionalPut` can express the proposed acquisition condition (`occ.ts:198-223`), but the plan does not supply the exact attribute-name/value maps. More importantly, its completed arm is internally confused: the condition includes `status = :completedNoop`, yet the text says application code returns without performing the put (`proposal:96-102`). If the pre-read races with another writer, the exact safe conditional behavior remains undefined.

5. Exact queue schemas—the central requested deliverable—are missing.

The proposal names two destinations but never gives either SQS message body schema. It does not define required fields, `additionalProperties`, schema IDs, versions, timestamp formats, raw `GSI3SK` representation, serialized DynamoDB key typing, or validation points. The repository’s queue contracts are actual JSON Schemas under `schemas/queues/`, and AGENTS.md section 7 requires every new command/event to receive valid and invalid contract tests.

Adding `SQS_REMINDER_SCAN_CONTINUATION_V1` and `SQS_REMINDER_CLAIM_CANDIDATE_V1` to `OutboxDestination` is also unjustified because the proposal explicitly says neither message uses the transactional outbox (`proposal:60-65, 212-223`). `OutboxDestination` currently describes actual outbox routing (`src/shared/outbox/outbox.ts:21-33, 110, 126-180`). Direct SQS message types should not be added to that union merely to name them.

6. The scheduler-to-scan-queue topology is contradictory and incomplete.

The current scheduler invokes `reminder-producer-handler` once, and that handler calls `runProducerTick` once (`src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-producer-handler.ts:35-41`). `runProducerTick` computes partitions and loops them itself (`src/workers/reminder-producer/producer.ts:184-190`).

Without this, IAM send permissions, timeout bounds, message schema, and lease recovery cannot be implemented.
